  - Checks: `flagos-check-spacemit` runs 8 one-token cases with each setting against the CPU (n_kv 1000, 4000 of 4096 valid, 8192, GQA 2 and 1, head size 64, no mask, Qwen3-4B at 1024), and counts that their outputs differ from the one-chunk kernels' (that the split ran). `--bench-attn` times `split` and `split,paired` against the default at one token, taking turns.
  - On the Mac: all checks pass in the three host modes (the turns with real waits in the threads mode). The split's reference has max NMSE 2.3e-5 against the CPU, worst row 3.7e-5, 16/16 outputs differ from the default's. `test-backend-ops -o FLASH_ATTN_EXT` passes 1324/1324 with `split` and with `split,paired` (its permuted 512-position one-token cases, sinks included, take the split).
- Predictions refined at implementation, before measuring:
  - `split` stages then computes, in turn: copies of 16 MiB per call at n_kv 4096 at the 1-row path's 28 GB/s take about 0.6 ms, the arithmetic about 0.5 ms (round 1's `compute`), so about 1.1 ms per call, -30 to -40% against the default's 1.6-1.75.
  - `split,paired` overlaps one tile's copies with its partner's arithmetic: about 0.7-0.8 ms, -50 to -60%.
  - tg32 at depth 4096 accordingly about +15% (`split`) and about +25% (`split,paired`).
  - Outputs: NMSE against the CPU like the one-chunk kernels' or lower (V summed in F32); KLD at `-ub 1` about unchanged (at `-c 512` the split takes the positions from 256 on, where n_kv is 512).
- Rule, fixed before measuring:
  - at least 20% less 1-token attention time at n_kv 4096 than round 1's result, no 1-token shape slower;
  - tg32 at depth 4096 at least 8% higher, taking turns;
  - NMSE against the CPU within the bounds, perplexity within 1% of the CPU, mean KLD at `-ub 1` at most 0.0005 above M2e's 0.002050 (Qwen3-4B);
  - on Qwen3-0.6B too (added 2026-10-13, revised the same day, both before any measurement of (a)): over 32 chunks, M2e's state measured first, mean KLD at `-ub 1` at most 0.001 above M2e's, about 3 standard errors. No perplexity bound there: 8 chunks put ±0.74% on its ratio and the IME path itself measured +1.64%, and 32 chunks still leave about ±0.37%;
  - test-backend-ops passes every case.
  - Added 2026-10-14, before any measurement of (a): a one-token shape below n_kv 4096 that the split makes slower moves its threshold (`split_min_kv`) above that shape, to the crossover measured with added benchmark shapes, instead of rejecting the split. If both settings pass, the one with the higher tg32 at depth 4096 is adopted, `split` when the two are within 2% (it needs no pair waits).
- Results on the K3, 2026-10-14 (commit `e06cf90`; tables in `build.md` §7):
  - Correct: 16/16 one-token cases within the bounds (max NMSE 2.3e-5), each differing from the one-chunk kernels' output; test-backend-ops passes every case with `split` and with `split,paired`.
  - Per call against the one-chunk kernels: `split` -22.0 / -25.2 / -23.9% at n_kv 1024 / 4096 / 8192, `split,paired` -19.6 / -35.1 / -31.9%. Below the predictions (-30 to -40%, -50 to -60%): K and V read at 12.9 and 14.8 GB/s at n_kv 4096, half the 1-row matmuls' 28. Whether the arithmetic or the copies fall short of the estimate is not separated.
  - tg32, two rounds taking turns: at depth 4096 6.515 -> 7.515 (`split`, +15.3%) and 7.825 (`split,paired`, +20.1%); at depth 1024 +0.4% and +3.1%. At depth 1024 the round-robin deal likely leaves tile 0 with 3 of the 17 visible blocks while the others take 2 (n_kv 1280); not measured.
  - Accuracy, mean KLD at `-ub 1 -b 512`: Qwen3-4B 0.002050 -> 0.002001 (PPL ratio 1.0061 -> 1.0069), Qwen3-0.6B over 32 chunks 0.014032 -> 0.013979; both changes within one standard error.
- The `-ub 1` runs, corrected before the split's accuracy was measured: the first KLD runs, with llama-perplexity's default batch, gave both settings the one-chunk kernels' numbers to the last digit. llama-perplexity runs 4 sequences at once in separate cache streams, `split_equal` then puts a token of each of 2 sequences into every `-ub 1` ubatch, and the split takes one stream only (checked on the Mac, `build.md` §7). With `-b 512` (one sequence) every ubatch is one token, as in generation, and the split ran. So the rule's reference (M2e's 0.002050, Qwen3-4B) is the one-chunk kernels with one-token ubatches in the same run, which gave the same number. Every earlier `-ub 1` KLD had 2-token ubatches; on Qwen3-4B one-token ubatches reproduce its values to the last digit, so they stand. The runner and `build.md`'s recipe take `-b 512` since.
- Decision (2026-10-14, by the rule): adopted, `split,paired` (4.1% ahead of `split` at depth 4096, more than the 2% margin). Changes:
  - The split with the turns is the default for the calls `split_shape` takes; `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` keeps `split` (without the turns) and `one-chunk` (the kernels before) for comparisons, as `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_GEMV` keeps `staged` and `direct`.
  - `flagos-check-spacemit` runs its one-chunk and tiled cases with `one-chunk`, and checks the split's cases with and without the turns against the CPU and against each other: the two settings deal each tile the same blocks in the same order, so their outputs must be identical (on the Mac 8/8; a mutation where the odd tile skips its first block fails both checks).
  - The benchmark compares the default with `split` and `one-chunk` at one token, and adds n_kv 512, where the split starts: if it is slower there, the threshold moves (the rule's added line).
  - `spacemit_check.py` runs its `-ub 1` KLD with `-b 512`.
- Not covered by the split: several sequences generating at once (several streams, or several tokens per call); ggml-cpu restricts its own split of the positions to one stream too (`ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:9115`).
- Acceptance (2026-10-14, `build.md` §7): runner m2e with `--depth` on Qwen3-4B 35/35, on Qwen3-0.6B 34/34.
  - tg32 at depths 0 / 1024 / 4096: 11.30 / 9.93 / 7.83 (M2e's final state 11.38 / 9.56 / 6.49; the IME path 11.13 / 9.35 / 6.33). pp512 at depth 4096 34.63, unchanged.
  - Perplexity +0.05% against the CPU (4B). Mean KLD 0.003364 and 0.002021 (512-token batches, `-ub 1 -b 512`), moved by 1-2e-5 with the CPU's own run. Placement unchanged.
  - The benchmark in this run and the check run before it: one-token attention 1108-1109 us at n_kv 4096 (one-chunk kernels 1753-1771); the split without the turns 1093 and 1172 (1303 in the measuring session), so at depth the turns are the robust choice.
- n_kv 512, left as it is (user decision 2026-10-14): there every tile has one block, and the default takes 260-262 us against the one-chunk kernels' 252-253 (+3-4%, two runs), while the split without the turns takes 210-213 (-16 to -20%). The rule's "no 1-token shape slower" does not hold there; it costs about 2% of a token, at depths 256-511 only. The remedy, if wanted later: no turns when no tile has more than one block (both tiles of a pair decide alike, so the waits stay matched; outputs unchanged).
- Possible later: deal the visible blocks evenly over the tiles (the depth-1024 imbalance above), and separate copies from arithmetic in the split (why 15 rather than 28 GB/s).

**Not candidates:** prefetch hints (above). Candidate (c), the KV cache on huge pages (set aside). The 1-row one-chunk kernel (`_m1`), which runs for batches of 2-63 tokens, ALiBi and launches without TCM: it is no part of generation for the Qwen3 targets.

**Tests:**
- `flagos-check-spacemit`: the 1-token cases (n_kv 256 with 37 valid, 1024, 4096; Qwen3-0.6B 300 of 512). For `cached` and `burst`, each output compared byte for byte with the default's. For (a), more cases:
  - n_kv 8192;
  - a padded tail (4000 valid of 4096);
  - n_kv not a multiple of 64 x 8;
  - ALiBi, softcap and sinks;
  - GQA 4, 2 and 1;
  - head sizes 64 and 128.
- `test-backend-ops -o FLASH_ATTN_EXT` with every setting that keeps the outputs (all claimed cases).
- `--bench-attn`: the 1-token shapes at n_kv 256, 1024, 4096 and 8192, every setting against the default, taking turns twice, the faster turn of each.
- llama-bench tg32 at depths 1024 and 4096, taking turns. For (a) also the runner (perplexity, KLD at `-ub 1`).

**Exit (M2e.3):** every adopted change passed its rule; attention per token at depth 4096 and the tg32 depth series recorded against M2e's final state; accuracy as M2e.1's exit; all checks pass. Met 2026-10-14: round 2 passed its rule, and the acceptance run recorded the depth series (tg32 at depth 4096 6.49 -> 7.83; attention about 40 instead of 65.6 ms per token there) with accuracy within M2e.1's exit and every check passing (`build.md` §7). n_kv 512 left as it is (user, above).

**Risks:**
- The A100's vector unit may not keep several loads in flight (then `burst` shows nothing), and the scalar-read cost may not apply to these loops (then `cached` shows nothing). Both are cheap to measure.
- `stream` and `compute` produce wrong outputs by design. They stay out of every correctness check, like `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_GEMV=copy`.
- (a) is the largest change: a second step per attention call (about 1 us per layer), a 133 KB workspace, new strided helpers, and changed rounding.
- The benchmark rotates copies of the cache so that reads come from DRAM; at small n_kv the model's cache may partly stay in the caches.

### M3 - AOT package (Design Phase 3; D9 decided 2026-10-26)

**Status:** designed 2026-10-14 (user: "plan for m3?") from reading, not from runs: the AMD provider's AOT pipeline in this tree, FlagTree's SpacemiT backend and spine-runtime on GitHub, and the design documents (Design §9, §13, §15; mentor design §14, §17, §19). Not started. M3.0 is a spike without provider code; M3.1 waits for the mentor's answer to question 6. **Decided 2026-10-26 (user, D9):** M3 is in scope: for the ops FlagGems has a kernel for (and FlagTree's SpacemiT backend compiles), the provider uses that kernel; every other op keeps today's IME and RVV kernels as they are. The structural consequences are in `structural-review.md` §6: of the ops we claim, FlagGems covers `ADD`, `MUL`, `RMS_NORM`, `ROPE` (NEOX), `SET_ROWS` (F32 into F16), `GET_ROWS` (F32, and the Q6_K token embedding), SwiGLU (split form) and one-token attention; its quantized matmuls read GGUF's layout, not the IME layouts, so the matmuls stay on the IME (to confirm, Q13 there); an AOT op as a launch of its own costs 13.4-15.0 µs in a model run (E3a, E3c), so M3.0 first checks whether a FlagTree kernel can run as a step inside the split's launch; the speed rule below is to confirm (Q14 there). Question 6's remaining parts (which compiler copy, the manifest schema, x86 or K3 builds, the success criterion) stay with the mentor. **Deferred (user and mentor, 2026-10-26):** M3 and the FlagGems kernel work start after the structural work on the backend (the IGPU switch, the plugin split and what the mentor's answers require; `structural-review.md` §8) is done.

**Goal:** kernels from FlagOS's shared Triton sources, compiled ahead of time by FlagTree's SpacemiT backend, run on the AI cores inside the provider. They are loaded and launched under the Design's package contract (§9: manifest, exact profile, fail closed), as the AMD provider runs its HSACO packages. M3 shows that FlagOS's compiler path works on the K3; it is not expected to beat the hand-written kernels, and an AOT kernel enters the default path only where it is at least as fast (the speed rule; whether it stands under the decision of 2026-10-26, which says to use FlagGems' kernels where they exist, is `structural-review.md` Q14).

**Facts** (read 2026-10-14; nothing compiled or run yet):
- FlagTree's SpacemiT backend (`flagos-ai/FlagTree` @ `a134c5c`, `third_party/spacemit`, Triton 3.6.0):
  - Pipeline: Triton IR -> linalg (`spine-triton-opt`, built from source) -> LLVM IR (closed `spine-opt`, spine-mlir 0.8.0) -> riscv64 `.so` (`llc` with RVV and, for the A100, `xsmtvdotii`, the IME; scalable vectors, no VLEN fixed) (`backend/compiler.py:29-244, 342-353`).
  - Target: `AICPUTarget("cpu", arch, 0, core, ai_core, arch_id, num_threads, force_vector_interleave)` (`driver.py:344-352`). Only `arch_id` (0xA064 for the A100, `env.py:81-94`), `num_threads` and `force_vector_interleave` reach the code. `num_threads` is fixed at compile time: the native driver uses 4 on the K3 ("4 per stream"), FlagTree's CI 8.
  - Artifact: `compiled.asm["so"]`, exporting one function named after the kernel. Its C ABI: `void k(int64_t ctx /* spert::Context* */, {int64_t rank = 0, void * memref desc} per pointer, scalars by value, int32 grid x, y, z)`; fp16 scalars travel as float, constexprs are dropped, and `tl.program_id` reads `spine_grid(ctx, axis)` (`driver.py:82-138`).
  - Launch: one Triton program per spert tile, `stream.launch(spert::Grid(gx, gy, gz), fn, args..., gx, gy, gz)` (`driver.py:182-201`). The closure copies the arguments, so descriptors must outlive the launch; a C++ caller needs one typed stub per signature (SpacemiT's RPC server uses libffi instead).
  - Undefined symbols a kernel may need: `spine_grid`, `spine_thread_tcm_malloc/free` (TCM), libc and libm. Possibly also `spine_malloc/free` and `spine_mbarrier_*`, which libspert 0.6.x does not export (SpacemiT's own copy of the backend links a shim for the first), and `spine_require_stream` / `spine_parallel_dispatch_*` if a program fans out to other cores (`scf.forall`; not verified). Kernels resolve these from a libspert loaded with `RTLD_GLOBAL`.
  - No AOT export for this backend: Triton's `tools/compile.py` does not fit it (a 3-field target, scratch metadata, no C templates). What works: `triton.compile(src, target=AICPUTarget(...)).asm["so"]` with `metadata.name`, and the launcher the driver dumps (`main.cxx`) as a template.
  - Compiling without the board works in FlagTree's CI: an x86 runner with the Docker image `harbor.baai.ac.cn/flagtree/flagtree-spacemit-py312-torch2.10.0-ubuntu24.04:202607-3.6-base`, LLVM 22, spine-mlir x86_64 0.8.0, spine-runtime riscv64 0.6.0 and SpacemiT's toolchain 1.1.2 (`spacemit-ci.env`); the kernels then run in QEMU (VLEN 1024) over RPC. A native build on the K3 also exists (spine-mlir riscv64 0.8.0, SpacemiT's riscv64 wheels).
  - Maturity: 5 commits (2026-08-11 to 10-12), a smoke test and one example, CI only under QEMU, where an older QEMU computed the IME wrongly. SpacemiT's own repository of the backend (`spacemit-com/spine-triton`) is ahead: the `spine_malloc` shim, a mask fix, about 20 tests, FlagGems accuracy runs.
- The AMD provider (this tree):
  - Shared kernels in `kernels/generate_flagos_kernels.py` (about 70, also Denglin's), an AMD overlay of about 25 in `providers/amd/tools/generate_flagos_amd_kernels.py`. Shared ones a Qwen3 layer could use: `flagos_rms_norm_f32(output, x, n_cols, eps; BLOCK)` (one program per row, `:963`), `flagos_add_f32`, `flagos_mul_f32`, `flagos_swiglu_split_f32`, `flagos_rope_neox_f32`, `flagos_set_rows_f32_f16`, `flagos_flash_attn_decode_f32_f16`. The quantized `get_rows` and `mul_mat` kernels read ggml's own block layout.
  - Generator: besides launching and checking each kernel on the GPU, a compile-only mode (`triton.compile(ASTSource(...), target=GPUTarget("hip", arch, 32), options)`, `:1973-1983`). Output: one binary per kernel plus `manifest.json` (format 2: `package{abi, backend, binary_format, compiler, generator, sources, source_sha256, tuning_profile}`; per kernel `name, symbol, file, size_bytes, sha256, argument_count, num_warps, block_size, tile_*`), staged and published by an atomic rename. It imports torch even in compile-only mode.
  - Loader (`flagos-amd-aot.cpp:516-816`): transactional; regular files only, bounded sizes, safe paths; exact `format`, `abi`, `backend` and `binary_format`; the arch equal to the device's; per-kernel hash and size; the manifest cross-checked against the code object's own metadata; exact profile membership from a compiled-in table (`.inc` X-macros, also parsed by the Python tools). Packages are found through `FLAGOS_AMD_KERNEL_DIR`, a path relative to the library, then the configured and the installed directory; none is in git.
  - With no valid package the device stays and claims no compute op (`flagos-amd.cpp:6004-6006, 5805-5807`); `supports_op` claims a node only when its kernel is loaded; a failure after a write returns `GGML_STATUS_FAILED`.
- The design (Design §9.4-9.6, §15.3; mentor design §14, §17, §19):
  - Manifest envelope: ABI and version, backend and runtime, target and features, compiler, generator and source provenance, file, size and hash, entry symbol, argument count, type, size and order, launch geometry, scratch, alignment, shape constraints, profile identity.
  - Exact profile: target, kernel membership, each kernel's argument ABI, launch contract, shape contract and auto-enable policy; any change makes a new profile.
  - Fail closed: an unknown profile, a target mismatch, a missing, extra or duplicate kernel, a path outside the package, a size or hash mismatch, an ABI mismatch, or a resource over the device's limits rejects the package, and the ops go to other backends (Design and AMD code; the mentor design would fail the device's initialization instead).
  - The mentor design gives the AOT module loader and the typed launcher to the provider (SpacemiT's side), and keeps Triton out of the run time: offline AOT, a C/C++ loader.
- This provider: `execution` 0 and `aot_format` null, target `riscv64-a100-ime2` (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:543-573`); one spine-runtime launch per split on a persistent stream holding all 8 AI cores; weights IME-repacked (§2.3), which FlagOS's quantized Triton kernels cannot read.

**Plan:**
- **M3.0, spike** (no provider code; scripts in `docs/flagos-k3/scripts/`):
  1. Set up FlagTree's SpacemiT toolchain in its CI's Docker image on an x86 host (or the Mac's amd64 emulation). Compile `flagos_rms_norm_f32` and `flagos_add_f32` for the A100 (`arch_id` 0xA064) with `num_threads` 1, 4 and 8, dumping every stage.
  2. Record per `.so`: exported and undefined symbols, `DT_NEEDED`, the ELF's RISC-V attributes, and whether a program dispatches to other cores.
  3. On the K3, a stand-alone C++ program (`scripts/aot-spike.cpp`): load libspert with `RTLD_GLOBAL` and the kernel `.so` with `RTLD_NOW | RTLD_LOCAL`, launch it on a stream through a typed stub at Qwen3's shapes (rows of 2560, and of 128 per head), compare with a CPU reference, and time it against the provider's RVV kernels (`flagos-check-spacemit --bench`).
  - Continue only if the kernel runs correctly with symbols the board's libspert exports and without leaving its tile. Otherwise record the findings for the mentor, and try SpacemiT's own repository if FlagTree's copy is what fails.
- **M3.1, package and loader** (provider; the default path unchanged):
  - Generator `tools/generate_flagos_spacemit_kernels.py`, compile-only, from a chosen list of the shared kernels, with `AICPUTarget(..., arch_id=0xA064, num_threads=N)`. Per kernel a `.so`, plus `manifest.json`:
    - `package`: `abi` `flagos-spacemit-aot-v1`, `backend` `spert`, `binary_format` `riscv64-elf-so`, `arch_id`, `num_threads`, `compiler` (FlagTree commit, Triton version, spine-mlir and toolchain versions with their hashes), `generator`, `sources`, `source_sha256`, `profile`.
    - Per kernel: `name`, `symbol`, `file`, `size_bytes`, `sha256`, argument kinds in order, grid rule, block constexprs, undefined symbols.
    - Staged and published atomically, as AMD's.
  - Loader `flagos-spacemit-aot.{h,cpp}`, at probe after libspert:
    - AMD's policy: transactional, regular files, sizes, safe paths, hashes, exact profile from a compiled-in `.inc` table.
    - In place of the HSACO metadata, the checks a riscv64 `.so` allows: ELF64 little-endian, `EM_RISCV`, `ET_DYN`, exactly the manifest's symbol exported as a function, undefined symbols within an allowlist, no `DT_NEEDED` outside it, RISC-V attributes naming V and the IME.
    - The manifest's `arch_id` and `num_threads` checked against the device.
  - Typed stubs per kernel signature, generated with the profile table. Each AOT node is a launch of its own on the provider's stream, between the split's in-launch steps: the executor learns to cut a launch at an AOT node. The failure protocol stays: every check before the first write, `GGML_STATUS_FAILED` on a failed launch.
  - Device: `AOT_MODULE` and `aot_format` `flagos-spacemit-aot-v1` only with a valid package. Package path `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_KERNEL_DIR`, else the configured and the installed directory. Packages stay out of git (Design §16.2) and reach the board by copy.
- **M3.2, the first op from the package:** `RMS_NORM`, already claimed; the kernel's `BLOCK` of 4096 covers Qwen3's rows (2560, and 128 per head).
  - Test switch `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_AOT=rms_norm` routes it through the package.
  - The default path takes it only if, by a rule fixed before measuring, tg128 and pp128 stay within 1%. Each AOT node costs a launch (about 12 us; 4 RMS norms per layer, so about 1.7 ms per token to begin with).
- Later, by measurement and only if the mentor wants them:
  - more elementwise ops;
  - Triton kernels whose `tl.dot` reaches the IME (FlagTree passes `xsmtvdotii`), for attention in prompt processing at depth;
  - the embedding lookup, the last op on the X100 cores. It can read the token embedding in place from its CPU buffer, as attention reads the KV cache: the scheduler runs a node with a weight on the first backend that accepts the weight's buffer type and the op (`ggml/src/ggml-backend.cpp:877-889, 956-968`). A hand-written version (ggml's own dequantizer per row, bit-identical to the CPU's) is the simpler route (2026-10-16 note).

**Alternatives:**
- D9 rejected: hand-written kernels only. Nothing to build, and no FlagOS compiler path on the K3.
- FlagTree at run time (JIT through Python): ruled out by the mentor design (offline AOT, C/C++ loader).
- SpacemiT's `spine-triton` instead of FlagTree's copy: more mature, but not FlagOS's compiler of record; the mentor's call (question 6).
- Compiling natively on the K3: FlagTree's tested path is x86, and the generator imports torch, which has no ready riscv64 build here.
- AOT kernels inside the split's launch, with no extra launch: Triton's program id comes from the runtime's own grid, so a kernel could run inside a tile only if its grid were the 8 tiles and it looped over its rows itself, a provider-specific kernel. Worth it only if the launch cost matters.

**Tests:**
- Mac (no riscv64 execution): the loader's negative matrix (Design §15.3) on synthetic packages. Cases: manifest schema and version, unsafe or non-regular files, size and hash, a wrong ELF machine, class or type, a missing or extra exported symbol, an undefined symbol outside the allowlist, a wrong `arch_id` or `num_threads`, a missing, extra or duplicate kernel, a profile mismatch, truncated and oversized files. Each must leave no kernel loaded and no op claimed through the package.
- K3: the package loads; `test-backend-ops -o RMS_NORM` with the AOT switch; `flagos-check-spacemit` against the CPU; a launch-failure probe; the runner's KLD and llama-bench with and without the switch.

**Exit (M3):** the §15.3 matrix passes; one AOT op runs correctly from a package behind an exact profile, in the default path if the speed rule holds, else behind its switch; the package builds reproducibly from recorded tool versions (Design gate 6); nothing in the default path needs an environment variable (gate 9).

**Risks:**
- FlagTree's copy lags SpacemiT's (no `spine_malloc` shim, no mask fix), and kernels may need symbols the board's libspert lacks. M3.0 lists them; a shim can be linked into the kernel, as SpacemiT does.
- A program that fans out to other cores would need cores our stream holds (grants are all-or-nothing). Compile with `num_threads` 1, or show in M3.0 that it does not fan out.
- Generated RVV code may be slower than the hand-written kernels, and every AOT node costs a launch.
- Upstream tests run only under QEMU (no TCM, no timing): the board is the first real test.
- Two different binaries are labelled spine-mlir 0.8.0, so provenance records hashes of the tools, not only versions.
- Access: an x86 Linux host with Docker and the image registry; closed `spine-opt` and libspert (question 3).

### M4 - Fixed fusion (Design Phase 4, only from measured hotspots)

- Profile a generation step (per-node time) after M2e. Candidates from Common's catalog: `rms_norm_mul` (1), `add_rms_norm_mul` (2), `ffn_swiglu` (7), `rope_kv_store` (8), `flash_attn_decode` (10).
- Implement `query_lowering` and `execute_fusion` (`flagos-graph-plan.h:124-135`); every member op already has a direct path from M2d.
- Gate (Design §15.6): stable local gain, positive end-to-end, exact shape guards, required-output, alias and state tests.
- K3-specific fusions such as dequant + GEMM + epilogue tiled in TCM do not fit Common's fixed catalog (`upstream-review.md` A1). Raise them with the mentor instead of editing Common.

### S1 - The K3 core (decided 2026-10-26; the structural work, part 1)

**Status:** decided with the mentor 2026-10-26 (D10); code steps 1 to 3c accepted on the K3 2026-10-29 to 11-01, step 4 closed without code 2026-11-02 ("S1 step 4" below), then the code of decided experiments removed ("S1 cleanup before step 5"), step 5 built on the Mac and accepted on the K3 2026-11-02 ("S1 step 5 spec" below, its results); S1's code steps are done, S2 next. The design note for the core–plugin interface, S1's first deliverable, is written (2026-10-27, "S1 design" below), reviewed against upstream 2026-10-28 (`upstream-precedents.md` §4) and rewritten 2026-10-29 with A1-A3 and A5-A7 folded in; A4, the plan cache's key, is left open by the user with two candidates and the experiment that decides between them ("S1 design" §3); the rewritten note and the experiment's decision rule agreed by the user 2026-10-29; then each S1 code step gets its spec from it (the workflow in `CLAUDE.md`: a spec, then the coder, then the checks on the Mac's simulated device before the K3), the first being the header and the loader.

**Scope (the user's words, 2026-10-26):** a SpacemiT K3-only version of the deck's core (figure 2 of the split map; `structural-review.md` §3), without the AMD part; a draft version of Common planning; the plugin loader implemented; whatever else the core lacks completed. No separate `flagos-runtime.h`: the interface between the core and the plugin is defined in our tree.

**Content (proposed, from the mapping in `structural-review.md` §2-§3):**
1. The interface, first, as a design note: the required functions (device; memory, with the repack inside the buffer's set and get; copy; stream; launch), the capture replay (a split's steps recorded once per structure and replayed as one launch with barriers between the steps: today's executor), and the per-tile services the kernels use inside one launch (tile index and count, the agreed TCM, the workspace, the pair waits, the failure flag). `structural-review.md` §7.1 rank 2's requirements are its inputs. A header of ours, C-clean where that is cheap; no FlagOS-wide standard is awaited (the mentor, 2026-10-26).
2. L2 completed behind the interface: the contract check (`spacemit_find_op`, `flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp:129-176`), the op-to-steps expansion and the workspace sizing (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:662-723`), and the path and kernel choice now inside `flagos-spacemit-ime.cpp`, `-attn.cpp` and `-kernels.cpp` as the core's variant choice; the Q4 table (ranges, equality, strides, layout, TCM) becomes its vocabulary, in code, since there is no manifest yet.
3. L1, a draft: a plan cache keyed by the split's structure (as Common's `flagos_graph_plan` fingerprints a split; the step list and the workspace sizes reused when the structure repeats; the ceiling is the 0.55 ms of host work per token E3c measured; its key is A4, open since 2026-10-29: by structure, as here and as the deck's L1, or by the split's uid first, as llama.cpp's own backends do, decided by the experiment in "S1 design" §3), and the required-output and alias checks the two-step ops need (the matmul's quantize step, the split attention's merge). The fusion scan stays deferred (M4).
4. The plugin loader: `dlopen` of `libflagos-provider-spacemit.so` from the core's directory or a path variable, the entry symbol, an ABI version check, a host table (logging, the plugin directory); the package check only when a package exists (M3, deferred), with its negative tests then.
5. Workspace and capture driver: the core owns the workspace and records the split's steps once per structure; the plugin replays them; the per-token arguments (the KV length) updated in place. Delivered by steps 1, 2 and 3c, the per-token arguments by the design note's choice (no `update`: the kernels read the node at run time, "S1 design" §2); closed without code 2026-11-02 ("S1 step 4").
6. Diagnostics under the deck's names: `GGML_FLAGOS_STATS` (today's counters), `KERNEL_VARIANTS` (today's per-op settings), `DISABLE_OPS` (new: named ops back to the CPU), `TRACE_LAUNCH` (new: the steps by name), `SYNC_LAUNCH` (today's one launch per step).

**Exit (proposed):** `flagos-check-spacemit`, `test-backend-ops` and the acceptance runner pass on Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-0.6B with logits identical to today's; tg128 and pp128 within 1% of today (the plan cache may gain up to 0.6%); the core builds without `libspert` and without the IME toolchain; the Mac's simulated device still runs everything (the hostsim role).

**Not in S1:** the AMD provider (untouched), fusion kernels (M4), the package and the FlagGems kernels (M3, deferred), the pre-placement partition (never).

### S2 - The K3 plugin (decided 2026-10-26; the structural work, part 2, after S1)

**Scope:** `libflagos-provider-spacemit.so`, ours, source over the closed `libspert` (R3): device, probe and props (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:786-1101`); the buffer type on huge pages with the repack inside set and get (`flagos-spacemit-weights.{h,cpp}`, confirmed part of the plugin by the mentor 2026-10-26, with the buffer code of `flagos-spacemit.cpp:102-226`); copy; the persistent stream and the launch; the capture replay (`flagos-spacemit-exec.{h,cpp}`); the native IME and RVV kernels during the transition (the three kernel files and their orchestration in `-ime.cpp`, `-attn.cpp`, `-kernels.cpp`); `provider.cmake`; the tests and the acceptance runner.

**Exit (proposed):** the plugin loads through S1's loader and the same checks and acceptance runs pass with identical logits and speed within 1%; the plugin alone links `libspert` and needs the IME2 toolchain; TCM hygiene unchanged (nothing held after a run, failures included); unloading releases the stream (the core grant) and TCM.

**Order (proposed, for the user):** the IGPU switch (D3; `PROJECT.md` item 22) before S1, since it is small, independent and touches code S1 and S2 move; the interface note first in S1; M3 and fusion after S2.

### S1 design: the core–plugin interface (2026-10-27; rewritten 2026-10-29)

**Status:** S1's first deliverable (S1 item 1), a design note, agreed by the user 2026-10-29 together with §3's decision rule; no S1 code yet. Written 2026-10-27; rewritten 2026-10-29 with the review of its seven alterations against upstream folded in (`upstream-precedents.md` §4: A1, A2, A3, A5, A6 and A7 decided by the user 2026-10-28, A4 left open). The decisions it rests on (user, 2026-10-27 and 28): the interface is a pure C function table with opaque handles and a version, exported by the plugin's shared library through one entry symbol and loaded with `dlopen` only (A5: the linked-in form is not built); its structure is the deck's, with the mentor's `api_version` and `struct_size` fields and his `..._STATUS_...` spelling (A1, A3); what is not contract, the test counters, is reached by name through `get_proc_address` (A2); the deck's two zero-copy entries are declared now and implemented in S2 (A6); the core goes under `providers/spacemit/core/`, the plugin under `providers/spacemit/plugin/`; the mentor's Common is not changed. A4, the plan cache's key, stays open by the user's decision of 2026-10-29: §3 carries both candidates, the structural form of the first version and llama.cpp's uid-first form, with the experiment that decides between them. The deck says only that the plan is cached, redone when the graph's structure changes and hit in about 12 µs (`structural-experiments.md` R5), nothing of the key, and the mentor's Common keys by structure (`flagos-graph-plan.h:188-197`), so the structural form is the deck's and his, the uid form llama.cpp's. The rest is proposed and marked so. Line numbers are today's (HEAD `3d272f045`, every range re-read 2026-10-29).

**1. The boundary.** The core (compiled into `libggml-flagos`, built without `libspert` and without the IME2 toolchain) keeps: the ggml vtables (buffer and buffer type: `flagos-spacemit.cpp:124-226`; backend: `:232-239` and `:766-783`; device: `:789-914`) and the `flagos_provider_v1` callbacks and table (`:997-1104`); the contract check (`spacemit_find_op`, `flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp`) as a table of contracts; the op-to-steps expansion, the workspace sizing and the capture driver (`graph_compute`, `flagos-spacemit.cpp:642-764`); the plan cache (new, the L1 draft, §3); the loader (new, §6); the diagnostics (the test switches and the two counter classes, `:253-640`, renamed in S1 item 6). The plugin (`libflagos-provider-spacemit.so`, ours, source over the closed `libspert`) keeps: the probe and the device facts (`:916-991`; memory `:802-825`; the profile data `:1039-1065`); the buffers on huge pages (`:95-122`, `:189-207`) with the repack inside set, get and fill (`flagos-spacemit-weights.{h,cpp}`; D10); the stream and the launch (`flagos-spacemit-exec.cpp:388-395`, `:280-305`); the tile loop with its services (TCM taken once and agreed, the barriers, the pair waits, the failure flag); the kernels with their path choice (`flagos-spacemit-ime.cpp`, `-attn.cpp`, `-kernels.cpp` and the three kernel files); and the test-only counters that sit beside the executor and the kernels (`spacemit_steps_take` and `spacemit_ordered_counts_take`, `flagos-spacemit-exec.h:92-96`; `spacemit_mm_paths_take` and `spacemit_mm_tail_take`, `flagos-spacemit-ime.h:25-31`; the per-tile clock reads that fill `spacemit_run_stats`, `flagos-spacemit-exec.h:17-43`, `:76`), each reached by name through `get_proc_address` (A2, §2), none a table member. What the contract check needs from the plugin's side today, read in `flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp`: the layout rule (`spacemit_weight_layout`, `spacemit_tensor_is_repacked`), three shape predicates that are kernel facts (`spacemit_mul_mat_ime_supported`, `spacemit_flash_attn_supported`, `spacemit_flash_attn_split`, which reads shapes only: `flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:195-207`), three workspace rules (`spacemit_mul_mat_workspace`, `spacemit_rope_workspace`, `spacemit_flash_attn_split_workspace`) and the kernel entries; the placement predicate (`spacemit_buffer_usable`: our buffer or a host one) is the core's own. So in S1 the core's contract table holds the structural part of each contract (the ggml op, the operand types and placement, the step names in order) and the plugin's kernel table carries, next to each entry, its `supports` (the shape facts) and `workspace_size`; moving the shape facts into the core's table in the Q4 vocabulary is a later step, when the manifest's `when` exists (M3).

**2. The function table** (`providers/spacemit/core/flagos-spacemit-plugin.h`, C11, included by both sides; the sketch compiles as C11 with `<stdint.h>`, `<stdbool.h>` and `<stddef.h>` in front: the 2026-10-27 form was checked on the Mac with `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror -fsyntax-only`; the form below, with A1, A2, A3 and A6 folded in, is not compiled yet (no shell in the session that wrote it), and the exit's one-file check does it in the build):

```c
#define FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN_API_VERSION 1
typedef struct spacemit_plugin_device spacemit_plugin_device;   // opaque to the core
typedef struct spacemit_plugin_stream spacemit_plugin_stream;
typedef struct spacemit_plugin_buffer spacemit_plugin_buffer;
struct ggml_tensor;                                              // crosses as a pointer (in-tree plugin, one build)

enum spacemit_status {                      // the mentor's spelling (flagos_status, flagos-registry.h:23-30), our meanings;
    SPACEMIT_STATUS_SUCCESS = 0,            // the exact words are fixed with the header spec (A3)
    SPACEMIT_STATUS_FAILED,                 // a kernel returned false, or the runtime failed the launch
    SPACEMIT_STATUS_UNSUPPORTED,            // a contract, a layout or an access the plugin does not handle
    SPACEMIT_STATUS_ALLOC_FAILED,           // a buffer, the workspace or TCM
    SPACEMIT_STATUS_INVALID_ARGUMENT,       // a null handle, a range outside the tensor
};
const char * spacemit_status_name(enum spacemit_status status);  // as flagos_status_name (flagos-registry.h:90)

typedef struct { uint32_t struct_size; void (*log)(int level, const char * text); const char * plugin_dir; } spacemit_host_v1;
typedef struct { uint32_t struct_size, n_tiles, vector_bits; size_t tcm_bytes_per_tile;
                 const char * target, * runtime, * description; } spacemit_plugin_device_props;
typedef struct { uint32_t ith, nth; void * tcm; size_t tcm_size; void * workspace; size_t workspace_size;
                 void * pairs; } spacemit_tile;                  // today's struct (flagos-spacemit-ops.h:17-25)
typedef bool (*spacemit_step_fn)(const spacemit_tile * tile, struct ggml_tensor * node);
typedef struct { const char * name;                              // the step's contract name, "mul_mat.gemm"
                 spacemit_step_fn fn;
                 bool (*supports)(const struct ggml_tensor * op);       // the kernel's shape facts, or NULL
                 size_t (*workspace_size)(const struct ggml_tensor * node); } spacemit_plugin_kernel;  // or NULL
typedef struct { spacemit_step_fn fn; struct ggml_tensor * node; size_t workspace_offset; } spacemit_plugin_step;

typedef struct {
    uint32_t api_version, struct_size;                           // the mentor's fields (flagos-provider.h:66-67)
    /* device */
    uint32_t (*device_count)(void);
    enum spacemit_status (*device_open)(uint32_t index, spacemit_plugin_device ** dev);   // the probe; no runtime start here
    void (*device_close)(spacemit_plugin_device * dev);
    enum spacemit_status (*device_props)(spacemit_plugin_device * dev, spacemit_plugin_device_props * out);
    enum spacemit_status (*device_memory)(spacemit_plugin_device * dev, size_t * free, size_t * total);
    /* memory: buffers on huge pages from 2 MiB, 64-byte alignment, size 0 allowed */
    enum spacemit_status (*buffer_alloc)(spacemit_plugin_device * dev, size_t size, spacemit_plugin_buffer ** buf);
    void (*buffer_free)(spacemit_plugin_buffer * buf);
    void * (*buffer_base)(spacemit_plugin_buffer * buf);
    /* tensors: the layout rule and the repack inside (flagos-spacemit-weights.h:28-44) */
    uint32_t (*tensor_layout)(const struct ggml_tensor * t);                 // 0 plain, else a layout id
    size_t (*tensor_alloc_size)(const struct ggml_tensor * t);
    enum spacemit_status (*tensor_set)(struct ggml_tensor * t, const void * data, size_t offset, size_t size);
    enum spacemit_status (*tensor_get)(const struct ggml_tensor * t, void * data, size_t offset, size_t size);
    enum spacemit_status (*tensor_fill)(struct ggml_tensor * t, uint8_t value, size_t offset, size_t size);
    /* stream: one per backend context, the core grant held until destroyed */
    enum spacemit_status (*stream_create)(spacemit_plugin_device * dev, uint32_t n_tiles, spacemit_plugin_stream ** stream);
    void (*stream_destroy)(spacemit_plugin_stream * stream);
    enum spacemit_status (*stream_synchronize)(spacemit_plugin_stream * stream);
    /* kernels and the capture replay */
    const spacemit_plugin_kernel * (*kernels)(uint32_t * count);
    enum spacemit_status (*replay)(spacemit_plugin_stream * stream, const spacemit_plugin_step * steps, uint32_t n_steps,
                                   void * workspace, size_t workspace_size, uint32_t flags);  // flags: SYNC_LAUNCH (one step per launch)
    /* what is not contract (A2): the test counters and the per-tile clock reads, by their current names, with a typedef
       per name in this header; NULL for every name in a release plugin. A required member, like every one above */
    void * (*get_proc_address)(const char * name);
    /* zero copy (A6; the deck's Tier-2 group): the optional tail under struct_size, NULL until S2 implements them */
    enum spacemit_status (*register_host_ptr)(spacemit_plugin_device * dev, void * ptr, size_t size, spacemit_plugin_buffer ** buf);
    enum spacemit_status (*tensor_prepare)(struct ggml_tensor * t);         // the IME copy beside a mapped weight, kept in t->extra
} spacemit_plugin_v1;

const spacemit_plugin_v1 * flagos_spacemit_plugin_v1(const spacemit_host_v1 * host);   // the entry symbol
```

Not in the table: `memcpy`/`memset` (the buffer is host memory; the core copies with `memcpy` and refuses raw copies of repacked tensors as `cpy_tensor` does today, `flagos-spacemit.cpp:155-161`); events (the deck's optional group, none on the K3); `update` of per-token arguments (the steps carry the node pointers and the kernels read the node at run time; under either A4 candidate the core rebinds the pointers of a cached step list itself, §3, so no plugin call is needed); `module_load` and `kernel_get`, which join the table as members with M3 (A2); a `test_control` entry (A2: the counters go by name). The pair wait (`spacemit_pair_wait`) is called by kernels only, both inside the plugin, so it stays a plugin function, not a table entry. Environment switches stay environment switches, each read on the side that acts on it: the core reads `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_DISABLE`, `TEST_DEVICE_TYPE`, `TEST_LAUNCH`, `QUANTIZE`, `TEST_STATS` and `TEST_OP_STATS` (today `flagos-spacemit.cpp:961`, `:833`, `:263`, `:285`, `:316`, `:493`); the plugin reads `STREAM` (today read by the core at `:865-869` and handed to the executor; in S1 inside `stream_create`), `TEST_NO_HUGE_PAGES` (`:193`), and the kernels' and the executor's own: `MM`, `ATTN`, `GEMV`, `Q4_1`, `TAIL`, `TEST_REFERENCE`, `TEST_THREADS`, `TEST_FAIL_NODE`, `TEST_Q4_0_SCALE`.

**3. The capture protocol, with the plan cache in front (A4 closed 2026-11-01: the trusted uid hit, step 3b's run 12; the default since step 3c).** `graph_compute` in five steps: (i) the contract check, at every miss of the plan cache and on every graph the scheduler did not stamp (uid 0: `test-backend-ops`, the check program, the views of the eval-callback path): the core checks every node of the split against its contract table before any write (the same predicates as `supports_op`; a miss returns `GGML_STATUS_FAILED` with nothing written) and expands each op into its steps as it goes; a hit skips the check and trusts the one made at the entry's store, as CUDA and Hexagon trust the uid, under llama.cpp's reuse rule (a split keeps its uid only while the graph is reused with the same tensor objects and allocations, `src/llama-context.cpp:1339-1350`; a rebuilt graph gets a new uid, `ggml-backend.cpp:1537-1539`); what the trust gives up: a change to a node's operands or placement under a kept uid and the same objects is not seen (nothing in llama.cpp does this); amended from "the contract check on every graph" by step 3b's decision rule, since run 11 showed the check, not the expansion, to be the host cost (about 0.5 ms of a 4B token); (ii) the plan: one entry, the last plan, keyed by the split's uid, the very node objects (the node count and each `nodes[i]` pointer) and the configuration word; on a hit the split's step list (bound to those objects) and its two workspace sizes come from the entry; on a miss, a uid of 0 included, the expansion of (i) and a store; the test-only switch `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none` (the full path on every graph, step 3c) for comparisons; (iii) the workspace grown if needed (`:713-723`, `SPACEMIT_STATUS_ALLOC_FAILED` with nothing written); (iv) under the device's mutex (`:725`) `replay(stream, steps, n, workspace, size, flags)`; the plugin launches one grid of `n_tiles` tiles; each tile takes its TCM once, the tiles agree one TCM size at the first barrier, each step runs on every tile and a barrier follows it, a tile that fails sets the shared flag and every tile then skips its remaining kernels but reaches every barrier (today's protocol, §2.4); the plugin waits for the launch and returns the status; (v) the core maps the status to `GGML_STATUS_SUCCESS` or `GGML_STATUS_FAILED`. With `SYNC_LAUNCH` (S1 item 6) the core passes the flag and the plugin runs one step per launch with a host wait between them (today's `per-step` mode, `:734-744`, test-only, E3a). What is cached under either candidate: the step list (kernel entries and node indices) and the two workspace sizes, nothing else of a graph; `data` pointers are read by the kernels at run time, so a rotation of the scheduler's buffers changes nothing (Common treats direct steps the same way, `flagos-graph-plan.cpp:1541-1547`), and the per-token facts the kernels read (the KV length, the rows) come from the node, so there is no `update` call; the two test switches that change the step list and are read at every graph today (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE`, `:285`; `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` in `spacemit_flash_attn_split`) enter the key as one configuration word, as Common's `configuration_id` does (`flagos-graph-plan.h:130`); the launch mode (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_LAUNCH`) is read at replay time and needs no place in the key. The record of the decision, as written 2026-10-29 before the measurement: the two candidates for (ii), both built behind a test switch and decided by the experiment below (step 3, run 11: neither passed the rule, the checked forms keeping the contract check; step 3b, run 12: U's key with the trusted hit adopted):

- **Candidate S, the deck's form** (the first version of this note; Common's planner realized): no uid. Every graph is canonicalized and compared. The key is each node's structure with the pointer fields canonicalized, as Common's `flagos_canonical_tensor` does it (the producer and the view source as node indices, external tensors numbered by first appearance, then type, op, flags, view offset, `ne`, `nb` and `op_params`: `flagos-graph-plan.cpp:194-237`), a fingerprint first (`:331-356`) and a full compare on a fingerprint hit (`:275-320`); the node pointers rebound on a hit, a rebuild on a miss; one entry, the last plan (Common keeps 32 with a `last_used` tick and a last-entry fast path, `flagos-graph-plan.h:230-270`, `.cpp:1523-1533`; in llama.cpp's loops one split shape is live at a time, so the 32 matter only for alternating shapes). Common's class is not reused as it stands: its plan holds fusion steps, not our step list, and its canonical helpers are file-local, so the core carries a copy of the canonical form (about 150 lines, an estimate) or Common gains an exported helper (an M-entry in `upstream-changes.md`, the mentor's call); the spec decides. The deck's L1 is this planner: the deck says only "an execution plan cache", a hit of about 12 µs, redone when the structure changes (R5); the mentor's code keys by structure alone, with no uid in its canonical form or its cache entry (`:194-237`, `:1523-1533`), and rejects the uid on purpose: "Match structure directly because scheduler UIDs do not identify shapes or edges" (`:1616`, the comment at the cache's last-entry check; read 2026-10-29). That holds for the uid alone, which is why candidate U keeps the structural compare behind it.
- **Candidate U, llama.cpp's form** (A4's refined form, `upstream-precedents.md` §4): the split's `uid` first. The scheduler stamps every split's graph when it splits (`ggml-backend.cpp:1538`) and splits again only when the graph is not allocated (`:1949-1962`, `:1924-1941`); llama.cpp allocates only when it rebuilds the graph (`src/llama-context.cpp:1339-1373`: on reuse neither the reset nor the allocation runs), so in generation a split keeps its uid from token to token and gets a new one when the graph is rebuilt, which in llama.cpp's own loops nearly always carries a shape change (reuse fails when the mask's `n_kv` changes, `src/llama-graph.cpp:47-58`, padded to 256, `src/llama-kv-cache.cpp:1238-1243`). Behind the uid one entry, the last plan: on an equal uid the entry is taken after a check that the graph still has the same node objects (the node count and each `nodes[i]` pointer; a reused uid with other objects is a miss; a changed `op_params` inside the same objects is not detected, the trust CUDA places in the uid); on a new uid, candidate S's structural compare against the entry, a rebuild on a miss. A uid of 0 takes the structural path: graphs that did not come through the scheduler (test-backend-ops, the check program; `ggml.c:7372`) and the views of a split that the scheduler's eval-callback path computes one range at a time (`ggml_graph_view` sets uid 0, `ggml.c:7400`; `ggml-backend.cpp:1751`). Precedents: CUDA (`U ggml-cuda.cu:2807-2836`), Hexagon's one-entry `cached_uid` (`U ggml-hexagon.cpp:337, :2376, :2416-2417`), RPC; none in the deck or in Common.
- Predictions (estimates, written before the measurement): the host work a cache can remove is E3c's 0.55-0.64 ms per Qwen3-4B token (`structural-review.md` §4.2; not split between the node loop and the rest). U's hit costs one compare and a pointer walk over the split's nodes (about 830 on Qwen3-4B), under 5 µs on the X100. S's hit costs a canonicalization or a full compare of every node and its sources, field by field with a hash probe per tensor: the deck's 12 µs was measured on AMD's x86 host; on the X100 at 2.4 GHz we estimate 0.2-0.6 ms per graph, the order of the expansion it replaces, so S may save little or nothing on the K3 while U saves nearly all of the 0.55 ms. If S's hit measures near the deck's 12 µs instead, the two are equivalent in generation and S wins by the rule below as the simpler trust.
- The experiment (S1 code step 3; the Mac for the checks first, then the K3): the three settings behind a test switch `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none|structure|uid` (`none` today's path, the default until the decision). (1) Correctness, on the Mac's simulated device and on the K3: every check with logits identical under all three settings; `test-backend-ops` (uid 0) under all three; cache probes in the check program: the same structure built twice as new tensor objects (a hit by structure with rebind under S and U, outputs identical), one `op_params` value changed (a miss), one shape changed (a miss), the same graph object computed twice (a hit under both), a stamped uid reused with other node objects (a miss under U), a switch flipped between two graphs (a miss by the configuration word), a cached plan replayed after the workspace grew (the offsets still valid). (2) The counters, written with `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_STATS`: hits by uid, hits by structure, misses and rebinds per run, and the lookup's own time per graph. (3) The time: E3c's `graph_us` minus `launch_us` per graph (the host work in `graph_compute`) for tg128 and pp128 on Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-0.6B, three runs per setting taking turns. (4) llama-bench tg128 and pp128 under the three settings, taking turns, and the acceptance runner on the winner. Decision rule (fixed before any measurement; agreed by the user 2026-10-29): a setting is a candidate only if (1) passes entirely; a cache is kept at all only if it lowers the host work by at least 0.2 ms per generated token on Qwen3-4B (about 0.2% of a token; E3c's clock has 1 µs resolution per graph); between the two, U is adopted if its host work per token is at least 25 µs under S's on either model (twice the deck's hit cost), else S, the deck's and the mentor's form, as the simpler trust; the loser's code is removed with the switch, and the contract check stay s per graph either way.

**4. The per-tile services** stay as today and inside the plugin: `spacemit_tile` (tile index and count, the agreed TCM, the shared workspace, the pairs for `spacemit_pair_wait`), the barrier after every step, the failure flag. Since 2026-10-30 the tile loop takes its TCM view again after every barrier, as spine-runtime's header grants the view only while the tile runs on that worker (`structural-experiments.md` F16, E7, run 7: -0.3% of tg128 on Qwen3-4B, within the 1% rule; nothing of ours keeps data in TCM across a barrier), and E7b showed what one launch per kernel ordered by `join` would need to be correct on this runtime: a slice the tile owns of at most a pool eighth, and pair waits that yield; not a path the plugin takes (F15's cost floor), kept as test-only switches. The TCM-dependent path choice (the matmuls' 1-row path, A, B and C; attention's tiled or one-chunk kernels; the split's block count from the TCM size) stays inside the kernels for S1 (decision recorded here); the core may take it later from `device_props.tcm_bytes_per_tile`, once the choices are in the Q4 vocabulary.

**5. Lifetimes, threads, errors.** `device_open` at registration (the probe; no runtime start), `device_close` when the registry is torn down; `stream_create` in the backend's `init` (today `:871`), `stream_destroy` in its `free` (`:236`); one stream per backend context, every run of the device serialized by the core's mutex. No exception crosses the boundary: the plugin catches spine-runtime's and returns `SPACEMIT_STATUS_FAILED`; every failure is a status, named by `spacemit_status_name` in the core's log; nothing is written after a failed check; a failed replay leaves the workspace and TCM released (today's protocol). ggml tensors cross as pointers: the plugin is in-tree and built with the core, so the ggml struct is shared; this is not a long-term ABI (V2.0 §11.8's caveat), and the day the plugin is built elsewhere the tensor fields the kernels read become a descriptor.

**6. Loading.** The core finds the plugin next to its own library (`dladdr` on a core symbol, then `libflagos-provider-spacemit.so` in that directory) or at `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN=<path>` (test-only), `dlopen`s it with `RTLD_NOW | RTLD_LOCAL`, resolves `flagos_spacemit_plugin_v1`, calls it with the host table (`struct_size` set, `log` forwarding to ggml's logger so a plugin message reaches `ggml_log_set`'s callback, the plugin's directory) and checks the returned table as the mentor's registry checks a provider (`flagos-provider.cpp:5-8`; A1, A3): `api_version == FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN_API_VERSION` exactly, `struct_size >= offsetof(spacemit_plugin_v1, register_host_ptr)` (the required part; the zero-copy pair is the optional tail, A6, NULL allowed), every required pointer non-null, `get_proc_address` included (it may answer NULL for every name), and every step name of the core's contract table present in `kernels()`. Any failure logs a warning and registers no device (as a probe failure today). Negative tests: no library, a wrong version, a `struct_size` short of the required part, a missing entry symbol, a required pointer NULL, a missing step name, a plugin whose `device_count` is 0. The package check (manifest, sha256) waits for M3. `dlopen` only (A5, user 2026-10-28): the linked-in form, in which upstream builds its own backends, is not built, one core binary serving every chip; the consequences, not blockers: a statically linked executable cannot use the plugin, and the check program, ThreadSanitizer and a debugger run through the loader. Build: `provider.cmake` builds the core into `libggml-flagos` and the plugin as its own target; on riscv64 the plugin alone links `libspert` and gets the IME2 flags (today's lines for the whole provider: `provider.cmake:38-47`, `:49-73`); on the Mac `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_HOST_TEST_DEVICE=ON` (`:78-81`) builds the plugin with the host executor and the references, so the same loader loads it there and the checks run before the K3 (the hostsim role).

**7. Where today's files go** (the review's §2 rows; S1 moves the core's parts behind the table with the plugin still in the same build, S2 separates the plugin's build; the line ranges re-read 2026-10-29, the line counts of 2026-10-27):

| Today | Lines | Goes to | Step |
|---|---|---|---|
| `flagos-spacemit.cpp` :124-226 (buffer and buffer type vtables), :232-239 and :766-783 (backend), :789-914 (device vtable), :997-1104 (the `flagos_provider_v1` callbacks and table) | about 330 | core | S1 (the vtables call the table) |

**8. Out of scope:** the package and the manifest (M3), fusion (M4), events, zero-copy of repacked weights (G6: the CPU never reads them), the AMD provider, `update`; and, after the review of 2026-10-28: the linked-in plugin (A5, not adopted), `module_load` and `kernel_get` (table members with M3, A2), the shape facts in the Q4 vocabulary (M3), `graph_optimize` for reordered barriers (M4, A7), a typed argument descriptor in place of the tensor pointer (M3's typed launch, A7).

**Exit of the note:** reviewed and agreed by the user 2026-10-29 (A1-A7 reviewed 2026-10-28, A4 open by decision until its experiment, §3's rule agreed); the header sketch compiles as C on the Mac (`tests/check_plugin_header.c`: includes the header and takes the address of the entry, built with `-std=c11 -Wall -Werror`; the 2026-10-27 sketch passed the syntax check, the rewritten one has not been compiled yet); every file of the review's §2 has a destination (§7 above). Then S1's code, one coder pass each, specified from this note and checked on the Mac's simulated device before the K3: (1) the header and the loader, with today's code behind the table in one build (the loader's negative tests); (2) L2 behind the interface: the contract table and the binding of step names at load; (3) the L1 draft as A4's experiment: both candidates behind `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN`, the probes, the counters, the K3 runs, the decision by §3's rule, the loser removed with the switch; (4) the workspace and capture driver as the core's (delivered by steps 1, 2 and 3c; closed without code 2026-11-02, "S1 step 4"); (5) the diagnostics under the deck's names. Each step's exit is S1's: all checks pass with logits identical to today's and tg128 and pp128 within 1%.

### S1 step 1 spec: the header and the loader (2026-10-29)

**Status:** the spec of S1 code step 1 (the exit of "S1 design"), written 2026-10-29 and approved by the user the same day. **Implemented 2026-10-29** by the coder subagent (Opus) as written, in one build: the header `core/flagos-spacemit-plugin.h`, the loader `core/flagos-spacemit-loader.{h,cpp}`, the table `plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp`, the six fake plugins with `tests.cmake`, the vtables and `graph_compute` through the table, the 14 entry points in pointer form; the three reviewers (boundary semantics, the mechanical conversion, build and tests; fresh contexts, each with the diff and the spec) found four defects, all verified in the code and fixed in one round: the E3b counters read uninitialized after the struct moved to the C header (`check_spacemit.cpp:3220`), `tensor_layout` answering `plain` for a view where `cpy_tensor`'s old predicate resolved the storage (now `spacemit_storage_layout`), the plugin keeping a pointer to the caller's host table (now the two fields copied on the first call), an unused function in the NULL-member fake that only compiled through ggml's warning suppressions; plus the entry's line number in `upstream-changes.md`, `spacemit_status_name` inside the `extern "C"` block, and the own-image `not_found` of a static build logged at debug level. Checked on the Mac after the round (by the coder and again by hand): the host build and the ThreadSanitizer build with no warnings; `flagos-check-spacemit` all checks passed in the serial, threaded, per-step and per-step-join modes with the loader line (`libggml-flagos.0.19.0.dylib: api 1, struct_size 176, 14 kernels, 5 names by get_proc_address`), `--e3b=1` to the end; the six fakes refused with the exact result each and compiling under `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` alone; `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN=/nonexistent/plugin.so` gives no device with one warning; the two loader mutations caught; every `test-backend-ops` set passing (ADD 54, MUL 46, RMS_NORM 52, ROPE 273, SET_ROWS 180, GET_ROWS 9, SWIGLU 12, MUL_MAT 3, FLASH_ATTN_EXT 1324, CPY 15, the four graphs 30/25/144/24); ThreadSanitizer 0 reports; the riscv64 syntax check 0 errors; the plain build "device checks skipped". Committed 2026-10-29 (`c4fd4a92e`) and run on the K3 the same day (`build.md` §7, "S1 step 1 on the K3"): the model side as predicted on both models (every layer and the KV cache on the provider, the perplexity and KLDs of 2026-10-27 to the last digit, speed within 1%), the loader opening `libggml-flagos.so.0` beside the core; but 36/41 and 36/40, one event: `flagos-check-spacemit` crashing in every pass in the test-only Q4_0 scale-policy check of 2026-10-28, a GCC 15.2.0 epilogue fault triggered by a call per block inside the inlined RVV quantizer, nothing of S1. The fix (the factor read once per quantizer call, the adaptive form deleted) verified on the K3 2026-10-30 by the disassembly, the check program's passes and the runner on Qwen3-0.6B (39/39, logits unchanged, speed within spread): **step 1 accepted on the K3.**

**Goal of the step:** the core–plugin interface exists as a C header, the plugin's table is implemented over today's code, the core's ggml vtables and `graph_compute` reach the device, the buffers, the stream and the replay only through the table, and the core finds and checks the table through a `dlopen` loader with its negative tests; today's code otherwise unchanged, the outputs bit-identical, one build.

**Approach, and the alternative.** The plugin side stays inside `libggml-flagos` in this step; the loader `dlopen`s the library that holds the entry symbol, which in this step is the core's own library, found with `dladdr` on a core symbol, and in S2 becomes `libflagos-provider-spacemit.so` beside it, searched first. The loader's code is then the same in both steps, the negative tests run now on fake plugins built as small shared libraries, and nothing moves between libraries yet. The alternative, the separate plugin library already in this step, loses because the contract check (`spacemit_find_op`, `flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp:133-143`, `:204-209`) still calls plugin-side functions directly (`spacemit_weight_layout`, `spacemit_flash_attn_supported`, `spacemit_flash_attn_split`, the workspace rules), so a separate library would need step 2's binding by name in the same change: two steps in one, about twice the diff. The kernels keep their bodies: each of the 14 entry points takes the tile as a pointer, the C form, with one added line (`const spacemit_tile & tile = *tile_p;`) so the body is untouched; a shim per kernel was the other option and loses because the op table in the core would then hold the shims, which are plugin-side templates.

**Predictions (before the run):** every output bit-identical to today's (no kernel body changes, the same step lists in the same order, the same workspace offsets); `flagos-check-spacemit` and every `test-backend-ops` set pass on the Mac's simulated device with the serial executor, on threads and under ThreadSanitizer; the K3 acceptance runner passes with the perplexity and both KLDs of 2026-10-27 to the last digit (4B 9.7736, 0.002489 / 0.001229; 0.6B 14.4471, 0.004722 / 0.002533) and tg128 and pp128 within 1% (one indirect call per table member per graph, nothing per step); the probe gains one `dlopen` of an already loaded library (under a millisecond, once). Risks: a build with hidden symbol visibility would hide the entry (the export attribute covers it); a statically linked executable finds no entry through `dladdr` and gets no device (A5's documented consequence; the Mac's and the K3's builds are shared libraries, `BUILD_SHARED_LIBS` defaults ON, `ggml/CMakeLists.txt:74`); `GGML_BACKEND_DL` builds (the module form, E4) load the module's own path the same way.

**1. Files.** New, under `providers/spacemit/`: `core/flagos-spacemit-plugin.h` (the C11 interface), `core/flagos-spacemit-loader.h` and `.cpp` (the loader, C++), `plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp` (the entry and the table over today's functions), `tests/plugins/bad-version.c`, `short-struct.c`, `null-member.c`, `missing-kernel.c`, `no-device.c`, `no-entry.c` (fake plugins, C11), `tests.cmake` (the fake plugins' targets). Changed: `flagos-spacemit.cpp` (the vtables and `graph_compute` through the table, the probe through the loader), `flagos-spacemit-ops.h` and `.cpp` (the tile and the step type from the C header; the required kernel names), `flagos-spacemit-exec.h` and `.cpp` (the C step struct, `run` on a pointer and a count, the per-step loop gone to the plugin), `flagos-spacemit-ime.h/.cpp`, `-attn.h/.cpp`, `-kernels.h/.cpp` (the 14 entry points in pointer form; `spacemit_mm_paths_take` on pointers), `provider.cmake` (the new sources), `ggml/src/ggml-flagos/CMakeLists.txt` (one line in the fork's own `flagos-check-spacemit` block, `:156-160`: `include(providers/spacemit/tests.cmake)`; the `upstream-changes.md` entry that records that block gains the line), `tests/check_spacemit.cpp` (the loader checks; direct kernel calls, if any, take `&tile`), `tools/spacemit_check.py` (the `loader` line). Today's files stay where they are; the directory move is S2's.

**2. The header `core/flagos-spacemit-plugin.h`,** C11, exactly the sketch of "S1 design" §2 with these names fixed: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN_API_VERSION` 1; `enum spacemit_status { SPACEMIT_STATUS_SUCCESS = 0, SPACEMIT_STATUS_FAILED, SPACEMIT_STATUS_UNSUPPORTED, SPACEMIT_STATUS_ALLOC_FAILED, SPACEMIT_STATUS_INVALID_ARGUMENT }` and `const char * spacemit_status_name(enum spacemit_status)` (the words "success", "failed", "unsupported", "alloc_failed", "invalid_argument", as `flagos_status_name` spells its own, `flagos-registry.cpp:41-51`); `spacemit_host_v1 { uint32_t struct_size; void (*log)(int level, const char * text); const char * plugin_dir; }`, `level` an `enum ggml_log_level` value; `spacemit_plugin_device_props { uint32_t struct_size, n_tiles, vector_bits; size_t tcm_bytes_per_tile; const char * target, * runtime, * description; }`; `struct spacemit_pairs;` opaque (defined in `flagos-spacemit-exec.cpp:24-32` as today) and `typedef struct spacemit_tile { uint32_t ith, nth; void * tcm; size_t tcm_size; void * workspace; size_t workspace_size; struct spacemit_pairs * pairs; } spacemit_tile;` (today's `flagos-spacemit-ops.h:17-25`, moved); `typedef bool (*spacemit_step_fn)(const spacemit_tile * tile, struct ggml_tensor * node);`; `spacemit_plugin_kernel { const char * name; spacemit_step_fn fn; bool (*supports)(const struct ggml_tensor *); size_t (*workspace_size)(const struct ggml_tensor *); }`; `spacemit_plugin_step { spacemit_step_fn fn; struct ggml_tensor * node; size_t workspace_offset; }`; the replay flags `SPACEMIT_REPLAY_SYNC_LAUNCH = 1u << 0` (one step per launch with a host wait between them: today's `per-step`) and `SPACEMIT_REPLAY_ORDERED_LAUNCHES = 1u << 1` (today's `per-step-join`, E3b, test-only); `typedef struct spacemit_ordered_counts { uint64_t launches, shared_core, moved_tiles, tcm_elsewhere, pairs_on_one_core; } spacemit_ordered_counts;` (moved from `flagos-spacemit-exec.h:85-91`); `struct spacemit_run_stats;` forward-declared (its C++ definition stays in `flagos-spacemit-exec.h:20-43`); the typedefs of the names `get_proc_address` serves: `spacemit_steps_take_fn` (`uint64_t (*)(void)`), `spacemit_ordered_counts_take_fn` (`spacemit_ordered_counts (*)(void)`), `spacemit_mm_paths_take_fn` (`void (*)(uint64_t * a, uint64_t * b, uint64_t * c, uint64_t * b_staged)`), `spacemit_mm_tail_take_fn` (`void (*)(uint64_t * rows, uint64_t * calls, uint32_t n_tiles)`), `spacemit_run_stats_attach_fn` (`void (*)(spacemit_plugin_stream * stream, struct spacemit_run_stats * stats)`); the table `spacemit_plugin_v1` as sketched, `register_host_ptr` and `tensor_prepare` its last two members; the entry `const spacemit_plugin_v1 * flagos_spacemit_plugin_v1(const spacemit_host_v1 * host)` under `extern "C"` when `__cplusplus` is defined, marked `__attribute__((visibility("default")))` through a macro `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN_EXPORT` (empty on compilers without the attribute). The header includes only `<stdbool.h>`, `<stddef.h>`, `<stdint.h>`.

**3. The plugin file `plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp`,** the table over today's code, each member a thin wrapper: `device_count` returns 1 when the hardware probe passes (today's `spacemit_count_ai_cores` and `spacemit_ai_gate_available`, `flagos-spacemit.cpp:920-951`, moved here as they are, the simulated device included), else 0; `device_open(0, &dev)` allocates the plugin's device object (the core count, the description string of `:977-981`), any other index `SPACEMIT_STATUS_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; `device_close` frees it; `device_props` checks `out->struct_size >= sizeof` (else `INVALID_ARGUMENT`) and fills `n_tiles` (the core count), `vector_bits` 1024, `tcm_bytes_per_tile` (the K3's 384 KiB per core on the spine-runtime build, `k3-hardware.md` §2; on the host build the bytes of `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_TCM`, else 0; informational in S1: the tiles still agree their TCM at the first barrier), `target` "riscv64-a100-ime2", `runtime` "spine-runtime" or "none" (`:1058-1062`), `description`; `device_memory` is today's `:802-825`; `buffer_alloc` is today's `:189-207` (huge pages from 2 MiB unless `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_NO_HUGE_PAGES`; size 0 gives a buffer with a null base), returning `ALLOC_FAILED` on failure; `buffer_free`, `buffer_base` on the plugin's buffer object; `tensor_layout` returns `(uint32_t) spacemit_weight_layout(t)` (0 is `plain`, the enum's first value); `tensor_alloc_size` is `spacemit_weight_alloc_size`; `tensor_set`, `tensor_get`, `tensor_fill` call `spacemit_tensor_write`, `_read`, `_fill` and return `SUCCESS` (their aborts on invalid partial access stay as today; statuses for them are S2's memory part); `stream_create(dev, n_tiles, &stream)` reads `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_STREAM` (today's `spacemit_stream_policy_from_env`, `:865-869`, moved here) and wraps `spacemit_executor_create(policy, n_tiles)` in the plugin's stream object, `ALLOC_FAILED` if the executor could not be made; `stream_destroy` deletes it; `stream_synchronize` returns `SUCCESS` (the replay is synchronous); `kernels(&count)` returns the static array of 14 entries named `add.f32`, `mul.f32`, `rms_norm.f32`, `rope`, `set_rows`, `cpy.quant`, `get_rows.f32`, `swiglu.f32`, `mul_mat.quantize`, `mul_mat.gemm`, `flash_attn`, `flash_attn.split.partial`, `flash_attn.split.partial_paired`, `flash_attn.split.merge`, their `fn` today's entry points, `supports` `spacemit_flash_attn_supported` on the four attention entries and NULL elsewhere, `workspace_size` `spacemit_mul_mat_workspace` on both `mul_mat` entries, `spacemit_rope_workspace` on `rope`, `spacemit_flash_attn_split_workspace` on the three split entries, NULL elsewhere; `replay(stream, steps, n, workspace, size, flags)` runs the executor: with `SPACEMIT_REPLAY_ORDERED_LAUNCHES` its `run_ordered`, with `SPACEMIT_REPLAY_SYNC_LAUNCH` one step per `run` in order, stopping at the first failure (today's loop, `:734-744`, moved here), else one `run`; a `try`/`catch(...)` around the executor turns any exception into `SPACEMIT_STATUS_FAILED` with an error log; a false result is `FAILED`, else `SUCCESS`; `get_proc_address(name)` returns, for exactly the five names `spacemit_steps_take`, `spacemit_ordered_counts_take`, `spacemit_mm_paths_take`, `spacemit_mm_tail_take` and `spacemit_run_stats_attach`, those functions (the last one new: sets the executor's `stats` pointer, today `flagos-spacemit.cpp:731`), NULL for any other; `register_host_ptr` and `tensor_prepare` NULL. The entry returns NULL when `host` is NULL or `host->struct_size < sizeof(spacemit_host_v1)`, otherwise keeps `log` and `plugin_dir` in file statics (unused in this step: the plugin side keeps logging through `GGML_LOG_*`, being in the same library) and returns the static table with `api_version` 1 and `struct_size sizeof(spacemit_plugin_v1)`.

**4. The loader `core/flagos-spacemit-loader.{h,cpp}`** (C++, internal): `enum class spacemit_load_result { ok, not_found, no_entry, entry_refused, bad_version, short_struct, null_member, missing_kernel, no_device }`; `struct spacemit_plugin;` opaque (the `dlopen` handle, the table, the path); `spacemit_load_result spacemit_plugin_load(const char * path, const spacemit_host_v1 & host, const char * const * required_kernels, size_t n_required, spacemit_plugin ** out, std::string * detail)`; `void spacemit_plugin_unload(spacemit_plugin *)` (`dlclose`); `const spacemit_plugin_v1 * spacemit_plugin_table(const spacemit_plugin *)`; `const char * spacemit_plugin_path(const spacemit_plugin *)`; `std::string spacemit_plugin_default_path()`: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN` when set (that path only, no fallback), else `libflagos-provider-spacemit.so` (`.dylib` on macOS) in the directory of the core's own library if that file exists, else the core's own library, the path from `dladdr` on the address of `flagos_spacemit_provider` (`dli_fname`); an empty string when `dladdr` fails. `dlopen(path, RTLD_NOW | RTLD_LOCAL)` and `dlsym` are called directly from `<dlfcn.h>` (ggml's `dl_load_library` lives in `libggml`, `ggml/src/CMakeLists.txt:242-244`, which a backend library must not depend on); Windows is not a target. The checks, in this order, each ending the load with `dlclose`, `*out = nullptr` and `detail` naming the cause (the `dlerror` text, the member, the kernel name): the library opens (`not_found`); the entry symbol resolves (`no_entry`); the entry returns a table (`entry_refused`); `api_version == FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN_API_VERSION` (`bad_version`); `struct_size >= offsetof(spacemit_plugin_v1, register_host_ptr)` (`short_struct`); every member before `register_host_ptr` non-NULL, `get_proc_address` included (`null_member`); `kernels()` returns an array holding every required name (`missing_kernel`); `device_count() > 0` (`no_device`). The handle is kept for the process lifetime, as ggml keeps its backends (`U ggml-backend-reg.cpp:177-181`); `unload` serves the tests.

**5. The core `flagos-spacemit.cpp`.** The probe (`:953-991`): `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_DISABLE` first, as today; then `spacemit_plugin_load(nullptr, host, spacemit_required_kernels, spacemit_n_required_kernels, ...)` with a host table whose `struct_size` is set, whose `log` forwards to `ggml_log_internal((enum ggml_log_level) level, "%s\n", text)` and whose `plugin_dir` is the directory of the loaded path; `no_device` is logged at debug level (the ordinary case on a host without the AI cores) and every other failure at warning level with the path and the detail, and either registers no device; on `ok`: `device_open(0)`, `device_props`, then `g_device` filled from the props (the name stays "FlagOS:SpacemiT:0", `n_ai_cores` from `n_tiles`, the description from the props) with the plugin handle and the device handle kept in `spacemit_device_context`. The required names are a `const char * const spacemit_required_kernels[14]` with `spacemit_n_required_kernels` in `flagos-spacemit-ops.h/.cpp`, the 14 names of §3. The vtables: the buffer context holds the plugin's buffer handle, its base and size; `alloc_buffer` calls `buffer_alloc` and `ggml_backend_buffer_init` with the base; `free_buffer` calls `buffer_free`; `get_base` the stored base; `memset_tensor`, `set_tensor`, `get_tensor` call `tensor_fill`, `tensor_set`, `tensor_get`; `cpy_tensor` tests `tensor_layout(dst) != 0` where it tested `spacemit_tensor_is_repacked(dst)` (`:156`); `clear` memsets the base over the size (the buffer is host memory; no table call); `get_alloc_size` calls `tensor_alloc_size`; `get_memory` calls `device_memory`; `init_backend` calls `stream_create(dev, n_tiles, &stream)` and resolves `spacemit_run_stats_attach` once through `get_proc_address` into the backend context (NULL allowed: the counters then report "unavailable" once and stay off); `free` calls `stream_destroy`; `graph_compute` builds `std::vector<spacemit_plugin_step>` as today (`:661-712`), grows the workspace as today, and under the mutex calls `replay(stream, steps.data(), n, workspace, size, flags)` with the flags from today's `spacemit_launch_mode()` (`per-step` → `SYNC_LAUNCH`, `per-step-join` → `ORDERED_LAUNCHES`), the counters attached through the resolved function around it; `SUCCESS` maps to `GGML_STATUS_SUCCESS`, anything else to `GGML_STATUS_FAILED`. The profile (`:1039-1065`) takes `concurrency` from `n_tiles` and `runtime` from the props. `spacemit_device_type`, `spacemit_device_props`, the identity, the caps and the `flagos_provider_v1` table are unchanged. `spacemit_find_op` and the op table are unchanged but for the step type (step 2 binds the names).

**6. The executor and the kernels.** `flagos-spacemit-exec.h`: `spacemit_step` is replaced by `spacemit_plugin_step` (the same three fields; `kernel` becomes `fn`); `run` and `run_ordered` take `const spacemit_plugin_step * steps, size_t n_steps` instead of the vector; the per-step loop of `graph_compute` is the plugin's (§3), not the executor's; `spacemit_run_step` (`flagos-spacemit-exec.cpp:94-101`) calls `step.fn(&part, step.node)`; everything else in the executor is unchanged, the tile initializers (`:182-183`, `:480-481`, `:511`) included, since `spacemit_tile`'s fields keep their order. `flagos-spacemit-ops.h` includes the C header, keeps `SPACEMIT_MAX_TILES`, `spacemit_pair_wait(const spacemit_tile &)`, `spacemit_op` and `spacemit_find_op`, and defines `using spacemit_kernel_fn = spacemit_step_fn;`. The 14 entry points (`spacemit_kernel_add_f32` ... `spacemit_kernel_swiglu_f32`, `spacemit_mul_mat_quantize`, `spacemit_mul_mat_gemm`, `spacemit_kernel_flash_attn`, `_split_partial`, `_split_partial_paired`, `_split_merge`) take `const spacemit_tile * tile_p` and begin with `const spacemit_tile & tile = *tile_p;`, their bodies untouched; `spacemit_mm_paths_take` takes four pointers (its callers in the check program pass addresses); `spacemit_step_label` (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:415-479`) compares `step.fn`.
