Tests.** (a) The fake plugins, C11, each a complete table of dummy functions with one fault: `bad-version.c` (`api_version` 2), `short-struct.c` (`struct_size` = `offsetof(..., register_host_ptr) - sizeof(void *)`), `null-member.c` (`replay` NULL), `missing-kernel.c` (13 names, `mul_mat.gemm` left out), `no-device.c` (`device_count` 0), `no-entry.c` (a library whose only symbol is `flagos_spacemit_not_the_entry`); `tests.cmake` builds each as a `MODULE` library `flagos-spacemit-test-plugin-<name>` (EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL, `PREFIX ""`, `LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY ${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/spacemit-test-plugins`, compile options `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`, which is the header's C check: no separate `check_plugin_header.c`), makes `flagos-check-spacemit` depend on them and gives it `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_PLUGIN_DIR="<that directory>"`. (b) `check_spacemit.cpp`: a new `check_loader(dev, global_index)` right after `check_registry`, printing one line `loader   <path>: api 1, struct_size <n>, 14 kernels, <n> names by get_proc_address, tcm <bytes> per tile` and checking: the loaded path (`spacemit_plugin_path` of the registered device's plugin, reached through a small core accessor `spacemit_device_plugin(dev)` added to `flagos-spacemit-api.h`) is a readable file; a second `spacemit_plugin_load` of that path succeeds, its table's `kernels()` carries the 14 names, `get_proc_address` returns non-NULL for the five names and, this build being one library, the same addresses as the direct functions, and NULL for `"nonexistent"`, then `spacemit_plugin_unload`; the negative matrix: `spacemit_plugin_load` on `<dir>/<name>.so` (`.dylib` on macOS) for the six fakes gives exactly `bad_version`, `short_struct`, `null_member`, `missing_kernel`, `no_device`, `no_entry`, and on `<dir>/absent.so` `not_found`, each with `out == nullptr` and a non-empty `detail`; with `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN` set to `<dir>/absent.so` (an `env_override`), `spacemit_plugin_default_path()` returns that path (the variable is read at every call). (c) The coder verifies two mutations of the loader before finishing, each caught by (b): the version check skipped (`bad-version` then loads) and the name check skipped (`missing-kernel` then loads); both reverted. (d) `spacemit_check.py`: `"loader "` added to the prefix tuple of `check_spacemit` (`:136-137`), and under `igpu` a `report("plugin loaded through the loader", ...)` true when the check program's output has a `loader` line naming an existing file. (e) The direct kernel calls in the check program, if any, pass `&tile`; nothing else in it changes.

**8. What must not change:** every kernel body; `spacemit_find_op`'s answers and the set of claimed ops; the environment switches' names and meanings; the counters' names and values; the acceptance runner's other checks; the AMD and Denglin providers and Common; no file outside `providers/spacemit/`, `ggml/src/ggml-flagos/CMakeLists.txt` (the one `include` line) and the docs.

**10. For the coder:** first quote `PROJECT.md` §6's Git rules in one line; no commits; implement in this order: the header, the kernels' pointer form and the executor, the plugin file, the loader, the core, the tests and the build files; report files changed, the commands run with their output, and anything left undone.

### S1 step 2 spec: the contract table bound by name (2026-10-30)

**Status:** written 2026-10-30 from "S1 design" §1 and §7 (the exit's step (2)), approved by the user the same day. Line numbers are HEAD `8d976d1cc`'s. **Implemented 2026-10-30** by the coder subagent (Opus) as written, in one build: the typedef, the four attention predicates, the plugin's `supports` entries and sixth name, `k_contracts[]` with 12 entries and the bind / find / workspace functions, the core's bound table in the device context with the probe's bind, the labels by name, `check_contracts`. The three reviewers (boundary semantics, the mechanical conversion, build and tests; fresh contexts, each with the diff and the spec): the first two found no defect; the third found that `check_contracts` opened the `missing-kernel` fake with `dlopen` while `<dlfcn.h>` was included under a `_WIN32` guard the calls lacked, and `${CMAKE_DL_LIBS}` was not on the check program's link line; fixed in one round by replacing the `dlopen` with a copy of the real table whose `kernels()` lacks `mul_mat.gemm` (§7(b)), plus `GGML_ASSERT(names.size() == n)` in `spacemit_op_stats_file::add` and the redundant null test of `same_activations` dropped where `share` is already forced false. Checked on the Mac after the round (by the coder and again by hand): the host build with no warnings; `flagos-check-spacemit` all checks passed in the default, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_THREADS=1`, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE=shared` (no sharing warning), `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN=split`, `=one-chunk` and `per-step` runs, the lines `loader ... 14 kernels, 6 names by get_proc_address` and `contracts 12 bound to 14 kernels: checked`; every `test-backend-ops` set passing (the 14 sets, the same counts as step 1's); ThreadSanitizer 0 reports; the riscv64 syntax check 0 errors on the three changed `.cpp` files; the include grep printing nothing; the two mutations caught (the MUL_MAT names swapped: 226 matmul failures; the paired contract's first name replaced: `check_contracts` fails). Untested: `spacemit_ops_bind`'s "not a required kernel" branch (no contract names such a kernel; a gap accepted). On the K3 the same day (`build.md` §7, "S1 step 2 on the K3"): as predicted, the logits of 2026-10-27 to the last digit on both models, every layer and the KV cache on the provider, speed within 1% (4B pp128 106.55, tg128 11.00, tg32 at depth 4096 7.71; 0.6B 693.95 / 54.61 and 691.26 / 54.66), the loader report with 6 names and the contracts line in every pass; Qwen3-0.6B 41/41 twice, Qwen3-4B 39/40, the one failure the `--bench` pass dying with signal 11, unplaced, not reproduced in 3 reruns under the crash reporter nor in the session's three other passes of the same checks (the check program's 8-thread CPU references are question 8's exposure; a one-thread change proposed). **Step 2 accepted on the K3.** Next: step 3, A4's experiment (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none|structure|uid`).

**Goal of the step:** the core's contract check (`spacemit_find_op`) no longer calls any plugin-side function: it holds, per contract, the ggml op, the structural check (operand types, shapes, modes and placement; the layout rule through the table's `tensor_layout`) and the step names in order, and binds each name once at load to the plugin's kernel entry (`fn`, `supports`, `workspace_size`); the kernel's shape facts and the choice between attention's variants live in the plugin's `supports`; `flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp` and the core's `flagos-spacemit.cpp` include no kernel header. Outputs bit-identical, the same step lists in the same order, one build.

**Approach, and the alternatives.** Today `spacemit_find_op` (`flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp:182-232`) picks a `spacemit_op` whose steps are the kernel functions themselves and calls five plugin-side functions directly: `spacemit_weight_layout` (`:136`), `spacemit_tensor_is_repacked` (`:88`, `:105`), `spacemit_flash_attn_supported` and `spacemit_flash_attn_split` (`:213-217`), and the three workspace rules through the op (`:152`, `:160`, `:167`, `:171`); `graph_compute` calls `spacemit_mul_mat_same_activations` (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:657`) and the step labels compare function pointers (`:390`, `:436-437`). The step replaces the function pointers by names bound at load and the direct calls by the table: the layout rule by `table->tensor_layout` (the plugin's member since step 1), the attention facts by the entries' `supports`, the workspace rules by the entries' `workspace_size`, the activation-sharing fact (test-only, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE=shared`) by name through `get_proc_address` (A2), the labels by the kernel's name. The variant choice of attention (one-step, split, split with the turns) becomes three contracts for `GGML_OP_FLASH_ATTN_EXT` tried in order, each accepted when every step's `supports` passes; the plugin's `supports` for the three first steps carry the split decision (`spacemit_flash_attn_split`, which reads the shape and `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN`), so the switch stays on the side that acts on it (§2 of the design). Alternatives: (a) `spacemit_find_op` keeps calling the plugin's functions: S2 then cannot build the plugin as its own library without this step folded in; loses. (b) The plugin's `supports` does all the checking and the core holds no contract: the deck's L2 would be empty and placement would be the kernel's say; loses. (c) A `choose` member in the table for ops with variants: one member for one op, and the variant choice would still be plugin code; the ordered contracts need no member; loses. The attention contracts' structural check in the core is `true` in this step: today all of attention's type and shape facts sit in `spacemit_flash_attn_supported` (`flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp`), and moving the type part into the core is the Q4-vocabulary step (M3), recorded in §1 of the design; placement stays the core's (`spacemit_buffer_usable`).

**Predictions (before the run):** `spacemit_find_op`'s answers unchanged for every node (the structural checks are today's predicates with the two layout calls replaced by `tensor_layout`, which equals `spacemit_weight_layout` for a non-view, `flagos-spacemit-weights.cpp:146-153`, and the view case is excluded by `view_src == nullptr`, as `spacemit_weight_layout` excludes it at `:42`); the same step lists in the same order, so the same workspace offsets and bit-identical outputs; every Mac check and `test-backend-ops` set passing; the K3 runner with the perplexity and KLDs of 2026-10-27 to the last digit and speed within 1% (per graph the check walks at most three contracts per node and calls at most two `supports` per attention node, pointer compares otherwise: under 50 µs per Qwen3-4B graph, an estimate).

**1. Files.** Changed: `flagos-spacemit-ops.h` and `.cpp` (the contract table, the binding, `spacemit_find_op` on the bound table), `core/flagos-spacemit-plugin.h` (one typedef), `flagos-spacemit-attn.h` and `.cpp` (the four variant predicates), `plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp` (the kernel table's `supports`, the sixth name), `flagos-spacemit.cpp` (the bound table in the device context, the probe, `graph_compute`, the labels, the two includes gone), `tests/check_spacemit.cpp` (`check_contracts`, the loader check's six names). No new files; no kernel body changes; `tools/spacemit_check.py` unchanged.

**2. `flagos-spacemit-ops.h`.** Keeps `SPACEMIT_MAX_TILES`, `spacemit_pair_wait`, `spacemit_kernel_fn`, `spacemit_required_kernels` and `spacemit_n_required_kernels` as they are (the kernel headers include this file for the first two; S2 moves them). Replaces `spacemit_op` and `spacemit_find_op` with:

```cpp
constexpr uint32_t SPACEMIT_MAX_OP_STEPS = 2;

// a contract bound to the plugin's kernels: its steps in order, each the plugin's entry under the contract's name
struct spacemit_op {
    const spacemit_plugin_kernel * steps[SPACEMIT_MAX_OP_STEPS];
    uint32_t                       n_steps;
};

// the core's contracts bound to one plugin's kernel table (spacemit_ops_bind); the table is kept for the layout rule
struct spacemit_ops {
    const spacemit_plugin_v1 * table = nullptr;
    spacemit_op                ops[16];   // one per contract, in the contract table's order
    uint32_t                   n_ops = 0;
};

// binds every contract's step names to table->kernels(); false, with detail naming the step, when a name is missing
// (the loader refuses such a plugin first; this is the core's own guard) or when a contract names a kernel that
// spacemit_required_kernels does not list (the two lists must agree)
bool spacemit_ops_bind(const spacemit_plugin_v1 * table, spacemit_ops * out, std::string * detail);

// the op for a node, or nullptr when the provider does not handle exactly this op, type, shape and placement: the
// first contract of the node's op whose structural check and whose every step's supports (where not NULL) pass.
// supports_op and graph_compute both call it, so placement and execution cannot disagree.
const spacemit_op * spacemit_find_op(const spacemit_ops & ops, const ggml_tensor * op, ggml_backend_buffer_type_t own_buft);

// the shared workspace the op needs for node: the largest of its steps' workspace_size (each distinct function once)
size_t spacemit_op_workspace(const spacemit_op * op, const ggml_tensor * node);
```

**3. `flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp`.** Includes only `flagos-spacemit-ops.h`, `ggml-impl.h` and the standard headers: the four kernel headers (`:3-6`) go. A file-local `struct spacemit_contract { ggml_op op; bool (*check)(const ggml_tensor * op, const spacemit_plugin_v1 * table, ggml_backend_buffer_type_t own_buft); const char * steps[SPACEMIT_MAX_OP_STEPS]; uint32_t n_steps; }` and the table `k_contracts[]`, in this order: `ADD` {binary_f32, "add.f32"}, `MUL` {binary_f32, "mul.f32"}, `RMS_NORM` {rms_norm_f32, "rms_norm.f32"}, `ROPE` {rope, "rope"}, `SET_ROWS` {set_rows, "set_rows"}, `CPY` {cpy_quant, "cpy.quant"}, `GET_ROWS` {get_rows_f32, "get_rows.f32"}, `GLU` {swiglu_f32, "swiglu.f32"}, `MUL_MAT` {mul_mat_ime, "mul_mat.quantize", "mul_mat.gemm"}, `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` {always, "flash_attn.split.partial_paired", "flash_attn.split.merge"}, `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` {always, "flash_attn.split.partial", "flash_attn.split.merge"}, `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` {always, "flash_attn"}. The structural checks are today's static predicates with the signature above, their bodies unchanged except: `spacemit_set_rows_supported` and `spacemit_cpy_quant_supported` test `table->tensor_layout(op) != 0` where they test `spacemit_tensor_is_repacked(op)` (`:88`, `:105`; the same predicate, `flagos-spacemit-weights.cpp:151-153`); `spacemit_mul_mat_ime_supported` tests `w->view_src == nullptr && table->tensor_layout(w) != 0` where it tests `spacemit_weight_layout(w) != plain` (`:136`; `spacemit_weight_layout` answers `plain` for any view, `:42`, and equals the storage layout otherwise), the rest of it unchanged; `always` returns true. The comments of the predicates stay. `spacemit_ops_bind`: `table->kernels(&n)` once; for each contract and each step name, the entry with that name (`strcmp`), `detail` = `"no kernel <name>"` and false when absent; also false with `detail` = `"<name> is not a required kernel"` when the name is not in `spacemit_required_kernels`; fills `out->ops[i]` in the contracts' order, `out->n_ops`, `out->table`. `spacemit_find_op`: the placement checks of `:223-230` first as today (`spacemit_buffer_usable` on the node and its sources; the function stays), then for each contract with `contract.op == op->op` in order: `check(op, ops.table, own_buft)`, then every bound step's `supports` where non-NULL; the first that passes is returned. `spacemit_op_workspace`: the maximum over the steps' `workspace_size(node)`, a function called once even when two steps share it (the two MUL_MAT entries, the split's two entries), 0 when none. The required-kernel list stays at `:174-180` unchanged.

**4. The attention predicates, `flagos-spacemit-attn.h` and `.cpp`.** New, next to `spacemit_flash_attn_supported` and `spacemit_flash_attn_split` (both kept): `bool spacemit_flash_attn_one_step_supported(const ggml_tensor * op)` = `spacemit_flash_attn_supported(op) && spacemit_flash_attn_split(op) == 0`; `spacemit_flash_attn_split_supported` = `... == 1`; `spacemit_flash_attn_split_paired_supported` = `... == 2`; `spacemit_flash_attn_split_any_supported` = `... != 0` (the merge step, shared by both split contracts). The header's comment on `spacemit_flash_attn_split` (`:18-19`) names the contracts instead of `spacemit_find_op`.

**5. The plugin file and the header.** `plugin_kernels` (`plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp:328-352`): `flash_attn`'s `supports` becomes `spacemit_flash_attn_one_step_supported`, `flash_attn.split.partial`'s `spacemit_flash_attn_split_supported`, `flash_attn.split.partial_paired`'s `spacemit_flash_attn_split_paired_supported`, `flash_attn.split.merge`'s `spacemit_flash_attn_split_any_supported`; everything else in the table unchanged. `plugin_get_proc_address` (`:389-410`) answers a sixth name, `"spacemit_mul_mat_same_activations"`, with that function; the header gains `typedef bool (*spacemit_mul_mat_same_activations_fn)(const struct ggml_tensor * a, const struct ggml_tensor * b);` next to the other five typedefs (`core/flagos-spacemit-plugin.h:113-118`), its comment: test-only, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE=shared`.

**6. The core, `flagos-spacemit.cpp`.** The includes of `flagos-spacemit-attn.h` and `flagos-spacemit-ime.h` (`:7`, `:9`) go (`flagos-spacemit-exec.h` stays for `spacemit_run_stats` until step 5). `spacemit_device_context` gains `spacemit_ops ops;`. The probe, after `device_props` (`:930-937`): `spacemit_ops_bind(table, &g_device.ops, &detail)`; on false a warning `"FlagOS SpacemiT: the plugin's kernels do not bind to the core's contracts: %s"`, `device_close`, `spacemit_plugin_unload`, no device. `spacemit_device_supports_op` (`:840-842`) and `graph_compute` (`:642`) call `spacemit_find_op(dev->ops, node, &dev->buffer_type)`. `spacemit_backend_context` gains `spacemit_mul_mat_same_activations_fn same_activations = nullptr;` resolved once in `init_backend` next to `attach` through `get_proc_address`, and `std::vector<const char *> step_names;` kept parallel to `steps`. In `graph_compute`: `ctx->steps.push_back({ op->steps[s]->fn, node, 0 })` and `ctx->step_names.push_back(op->steps[s]->name)` (`:664-666`, both vectors cleared at `:628`); the workspace from `spacemit_op_workspace(op, node)` (`:667-670`); the sharing test `ctx->same_activations != nullptr && ctx->same_activations(held, node)` (`:657`), and when `share` is set but the function is NULL, one warning `"FlagOS SpacemiT: the plugin has no activation-sharing fact; FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE=shared stays off"` (a `static std::atomic<bool>` as at `:694-699`) and `share` treated as false. `spacemit_step_label(steps, names, i)` (`:382`) takes the names and decides by `strcmp`: `"mul_mat.quantize"` → `quantize`, else `gemm` (`:390`); `"flash_attn"` → `one-step`, `"flash_attn.split.merge"` → `merge`, else `partial` (`:436-438`); the labels' text unchanged (`scripts/op-stats.py` reads them); `spacemit_op_stats_file::add` (`:477`, `:726`) takes the names along.

**7. Tests, `tests/check_spacemit.cpp`.** (a) `check_loader` (`:177-301`): six names by `get_proc_address`, the sixth `spacemit_mul_mat_same_activations` with the same address as the direct function; its printed line says `6 names`. (b) New `check_contracts(dev)` right after `check_loader` in `main` (`:3898-3901`), printing one line `contracts <n> bound to <m> kernels: checked`: through `spacemit_device_plugin(dev)` and `spacemit_plugin_table`, `spacemit_ops_bind` into a local `spacemit_ops` succeeds with 12 ops whose every step's `fn` and `name` equal the entry of that name in `table->kernels()`; `spacemit_ops_bind` on a copy of the real table whose `kernels()` returns the real entries but `mul_mat.gemm` fails with `detail` naming `mul_mat.gemm` (the loader refuses such a plugin before the core sees it, so the check program builds the table itself rather than opening the `missing-kernel` fake, which would make it a `dlopen` caller: a review finding of 2026-10-30); `spacemit_find_op` on a `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` node that meets the split's shape (one query token, one stream, n_kv 512, F16 K and V in the cache layout, no mask: build it as the attention checks build theirs, unallocated) returns the op whose first step is the `flash_attn.split.partial_paired` entry by default, the `flash_attn.split.partial` entry under `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN=split` and the `flash_attn` entry under `one-chunk` (`env_override`), and for a 64-token query the `flash_attn` entry under all three; `spacemit_find_op` on a `MUL_MAT` of a Q4_0 weight that is a view returns nullptr and on the same weight unviewed the two-step op, `spacemit_op_workspace` of it equal to the `mul_mat.gemm` entry's `workspace_size(node)`. (c) Mutations the coder verifies before finishing, each caught, both reverted: the two MUL_MAT step names swapped in `k_contracts` (the matmul checks fail); the paired contract's first name replaced by `flash_attn.split.partial` (`check_contracts` fails). (d) Every other check unchanged.

For the coder:** first quote `PROJECT.md` §6's Git rules in one line; no commits; implement in this order: the header's typedef, the attention predicates, the plugin file, `flagos-spacemit-ops.{h,cpp}`, the core, the tests; report files changed, the commands run with their output, the two mutations' outcomes, and anything left undone.

### S1 step 2b: the default path pruned before the plan cache (2026-10-30)

**Status:** the user's request after sessions 1-4 of the step-by-step plan ("remove unnecessary checks within the default run; only the must-needed core code moves on to step 3"); written and implemented by Fable the same day, checked on the Mac; **accepted on the K3 2026-10-31** (`build.md` §7): no loss on either model, Qwen3-4B tg128 +0.55% in all three pairs, Qwen3-0.6B within spread, the `--full` pass with every switch-flipping line as before. Next: step 3, A4's experiment, on this `graph_compute`.

**What the default path does per graph after it, and must keep:** the contract check per node (`spacemit_find_op`), the operand-allocated check, the expansion into steps with the workspace sizes, the workspace growth, the device mutex, the replay; in the tile loop the TCM agreement, the barrier after each step, the failure flag and the view taken again after every barrier (run 7). Step 3's plan cache caches the expansion and the workspace sizes on a hit and keeps the contract check per graph.

**What was on it and is not any more:** (1) four switches parsed from the environment on every call, on every tile: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_GEMV` at every 1-row matmul step (about 2,000 getenv-and-parse calls per generated Qwen3-4B token on the AI cores), `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_MM` and `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TAIL` at every several-row matmul step, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` at every attention node at placement and at every graph. Each is now parsed once into an atomic (`spacemit_cached_switch`, `flagos-spacemit-kernels.h`; the GEMV strategy packed into one word) and parsed again only after `spacemit_switches_reset()`, which bumps a generation counter; the check program, which flips switches inside one process, calls it from `set_env` (every switch change there goes through it, directly or through `env_override`), and the plugin serves it by name through `get_proc_address` (the seventh name, A2). The two per-graph switches of the core (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE`, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_LAUNCH`) stay read per graph: two reads per graph, and step 3 reads the first into the cache key anyway. (2) In `graph_compute`, the op counters' step-name vector is filled only when the op-stats file is on, and the step counters attach to the stream only for a counted graph; an uncounted graph makes neither call.

**What stays:** the counters, the trace and the fail-step hook (cheap branches; the runner and the checks depend on them; S1 step 5 renames them under the deck's names).

**Checked on the Mac:** the host build clean; the check program all checks passed in eight settings (the default, the tiles on threads, `QUANTIZE=shared`, `ATTN=split`, `ATTN=one-chunk`, `MM=split`, `GEMV=direct`, `TEST_LAUNCH=per-step`) with the loader line at 7 names, the gemv, mm and attention lines unchanged (they flip switches in-process, so they test the reset); `test-backend-ops` MUL_MAT and FLASH_ATTN_EXT; ThreadSanitizer clean with the tiles on threads; the riscv64 syntax check 0 errors on the three kernel-side files. Mutation, met by accident before the fix: with the reset only in `env_override` and not in `set_env`, the gemv check's `copy` control stopped differing and every mm setting took path C ("the 1-row strategies are not in use"); with the reset in `set_env` all pass.

**Prediction for the K3 (before the run):** outputs unchanged by the code (no arithmetic or path choice changes, only when a switch is read); tg128 within +0.1 to +0.5% on Qwen3-4B (the 2,000 parses per token, an estimate), never a loss; pp128 within spread; the check program's `--full` pass with every switch-dependent line as before. Rule: adopted unless the A/B shows a loss above 1% (it cannot by the code; the measurement is for the record).

### S1 step 3 spec: the plan cache, A4's experiment (2026-10-31)

**Status:** written 2026-10-31 from "S1 design" §3 (the two candidates, the probes, the counters and the decision rule agreed by the user 2026-10-29), on the `graph_compute` of step 2b; approved, implemented by the coder (Opus, effort high) with three reviewers and one fix round, verified on the Mac, and run on the K3 the same day: the result and the decision are at the end of this section ("Result on the K3"). Line numbers are HEAD's after step 2b (`flagos-spacemit.cpp` `graph_compute` :618-748, `spacemit_backend_context` :58-72).

**Goal of the step:** the draft of the deck's L1. On a hit, `graph_compute` takes the split's step list and its two workspace sizes from a cache instead of expanding every node again; the contract check stays per graph under every setting (fail closed, "S1 design" §3 (i)). Both candidates for the key are built behind one test switch, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none|structure|uid` (`none`, today's path, the default until the K3 decides), with probes, counters and one board session per model; the loser's code is removed with the switch afterwards. Outputs bit-identical under all three settings.

**What is cached** (the same under both candidates): the step list as kernel entries and node indices (not node pointers), the two workspace sizes (`scratch_size`, `act_size`), and the configuration word. On a hit the core rebinds each cached step to `ggml_graph_node(cgraph, index)` of the current graph and recomputes the offsets from the cached sizes (the offsets are relative to the workspace base, so a workspace that grew since keeps them valid; a probe checks it). The `data` pointers are read by the kernels at run time from the node, so a scheduler buffer rotation changes nothing of the plan ("S1 design" §3). The configuration word: bit 0 the `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE=shared` fact (read per graph today, :632) and, above it, the plugin's switch generation (`spacemit_switches_generation`, served by name as the eighth `get_proc_address` name; it moves whenever a kernel switch is reset, which is how `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` reaches the key without the core reading a plugin switch). A different word is a miss.

**Candidate S, the structural key, through Common's cache as it is.** Common exposes what S needs: `flagos_graph_plan_cache::get_or_create(cgraph, query_lowering, user_data, &created, configuration_id)` (`flagos-graph-plan.h:230-245`, `.cpp:1603`) canonicalizes the graph, looks it up by its structural fingerprint and a full compare of the canonical nodes (`:1273` `matches`), and on a miss builds a `flagos_graph_plan` through the `query_lowering` callback, which our core answers with "direct, no lowering" for every node (the coder reads `flagos_query_lowering_fn`'s contract in the header and answers it so that the plan carries no fusion step). The core keeps one entry of its own, the last plan: `{ const flagos_graph_plan * key; uint64_t word; steps; names; sizes }`; a `get_or_create` that returns the same plan object with `created == false` and the same word is a hit (rebind); anything else is a miss (expand as today, store). Common's cache keeps up to 32 structures, but our entry is one, so alternating shapes (the batch graph and the one-token graph of a `llama-perplexity` run) miss on every alternation in this draft (a step list per Common plan object would remove that; not in this step); the design note's reason for not copying the canonical form is void: nothing is copied and no `upstream-changes.md` entry is needed (Common is used, not changed).

**Candidate U, the uid first, S behind it.** The scheduler stamps every split's graph (`ggml-backend.cpp:1538`) and a whole graph (`:1074`); a graph view carries uid 0 (`ggml.c:7372`, `:7400`). On `cgraph->uid != 0` and equal to the entry's uid, the entry is taken after a check that the graph still holds the same node objects (the node count and each `ggml_graph_node(cgraph, i)` pointer equal to the ones recorded; a reused uid with other objects is a miss), and the same word; a uid of 0 or a new uid goes through S's path (Common's `get_or_create`), so U never trusts less than S plus the uid. CUDA's and Hexagon's precedent (`upstream-precedents.md` §4, A4).

**Counters** (test-only, in the E3c stats line, `spacemit_stats_file`, and readable by the check program through a core function `spacemit_plan_counters_take()` declared in a new core header `core/flagos-spacemit-plan.h`, which also declares the switch reader): `plan_hits_uid`, `plan_hits_structure`, `plan_misses`, `plan_rebinds`, and `plan_lookup_us` (the time from the top of `graph_compute` to the point where the step list is in hand, summed per kind of graph; the existing `graph_us` and `launch_us` give the host column as in run 8).

**1. Files.** `flagos-spacemit.cpp` (the cache in `spacemit_backend_context`, the lookup at the top of `graph_compute`, the counters), `core/flagos-spacemit-plan.{h,cpp}` (new: the switch, the plan entry, the lookup and store, the counters; the core's own code, built without `libspert`), `core/flagos-spacemit-plugin.h` (the eighth typedef, `spacemit_switches_generation_fn`), `plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp` (the eighth name), `tests/check_spacemit.cpp` (`check_plan`), `scripts/structural-experiments.sh` (two modes), `provider.cmake` (the new file). No kernel change, no change to Common.

**2. `graph_compute` after the step** (the duties of step 2b, in order): the contract check over every node as today (:645-666, with the `rows`/`n_kv` scan), then `spacemit_plan_lookup(ctx, cgraph, word)`: a hit fills `ctx->steps` by rebinding and sets the two sizes; a miss runs today's expansion (:667-689) and stores. Then the workspace growth, the mutex, the replay, the counters, unchanged. With `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none` the lookup is skipped and nothing else moves, so `none` is today's path to the instruction.

**3. Tests, `check_spacemit.cpp`, a new `check_plan(dev)` run under each of the three settings by `env_override`** (the probes of "S1 design" §3, each with the counters read before and after): (a) one layer graph computed twice as the same object: a hit under S and U, outputs identical to the CPU both times; (b) the same structure built twice as new tensor objects: a hit by structure under S and U with a rebind, outputs identical; (c) one `op_params` value changed (RoPE's base): a miss; (d) one shape changed: a miss; (e) a uid stamped on the second graph equal to the first's with other node objects: a miss under U (and a hit by structure if the structure is equal); (f) `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` flipped between two one-token attention graphs: a miss by the word, the step list the other variant's; (g) a cached plan replayed after the workspace grew (a larger graph in between): outputs identical; (h) a graph view (uid 0) under U: the structural path, counted as a structure hit; (i) under `none` every counter stays 0. Plus `test-backend-ops` MUL_MAT and FLASH_ATTN_EXT under all three settings, the ThreadSanitizer build. Mutations the coder verifies, each caught and reverted: the rebind skipped (b fails), the switch generation left out of the word (f fails), U's node-pointer check removed (e fails).

**4. The runner, `scripts/structural-experiments.sh`:** two modes on build A, `plan-s` (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=structure`) and `plan-u` (`uid`), with the counters (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_STATS`) as `e3c` has them, in `bench_modes` and `kld_modes`; the E3c summary prints the four plan counters and `plan_lookup_us` per token beside the host column.

**5. On the K3 (one session per model, the user):** `EXPS="plan" REPS=3 DEPTH=0` on Qwen3-4B with the perplexity text (the KLD of both settings against `ref` must read identical, maximum 5.4e-5), then Qwen3-0.6B without it; the modes `ref`, `e3c` (the counters, today's path), `plan-s`, `plan-u` interleaved. Read: hits and misses per token (prediction: in generation every graph a hit, by uid under U, by structure under S; in prompt processing the same within one ubatch size, a miss at each change of shape), `plan_key_us` per token, the lookup alone after the contract check (prediction: U under 5 µs; S 200-600 µs on the X100, an estimate against the deck's 12 µs on x86; `plan_lookup_us` includes the contract check and is read against `e3c`'s host column), the host column against `e3c`'s (the ceiling 551-699 µs per token, run 8), tg128 and pp128 (prediction: U up to +0.5%, S between -0.3% and +0.3%).

**6. Decision rule** (agreed 2026-10-29, "S1 design" §3): a setting is a candidate only if every probe and every `test-backend-ops` set passes with identical logits; a cache is kept at all only if it lowers the host work by at least 0.2 ms per generated Qwen3-4B token against `e3c`; between the two, U is adopted if its host work per token is at least 25 µs under S's on either model, else S as the deck's and the mentor's form; the loser is removed with the switch in the commit that follows, and the contract check stays per graph either way.

**7. For the coder:** first quote `PROJECT.md` §6's Git rules in one line; no commits; read `flagos-graph-plan.h` :110-275 and `flagos-graph-plan.cpp` :1535-1700 before writing S; implement in this order: the plan header and file, the eighth name, the lookup in `graph_compute`, the counters, `check_plan`, the runner's modes; report files changed, the commands run with their output, the three mutations' outcomes, and anything left undone.

**Result on the K3 (2026-10-31; `build.md` §7 "S1 step 3 on the K3"; `structural-experiments.md` run 11).** Part (1) passed: on the Mac every check under `none`, `structure` and `uid` (the plan probes 12/12 under `none`, 11/11 under the other two, outputs identical to `none`'s by `memcmp`), `test-backend-ops` MUL_MAT and FLASH_ATTN_EXT under both candidates, ThreadSanitizer clean; on the K3 the three settings of the check program passed, and on Qwen3-4B both candidates' logits are the reference's (mean KLD 0.000000, maximum 5.4e-5 at 512-token batches and 5.7e-5 at one token, same top p 100%, the base file's floor). Part (2), the hits, as predicted: 0.99 per generated token under both (the first graph of a run the miss), by uid under U and by structure under S, no rebind. Part (3), the host work per generated token (µs, the mean of 3 interleaved `llama-bench` runs with the counters): Qwen3-4B `e3c` 613, `plan-s` 1,588, `plan-u` 557; Qwen3-0.6B 406, 1,230, 409; the lookup from the top of `graph_compute` 599 / 1,570 / 542 on 4B and 394 / 1,214 / 397 on 0.6B; the key alone 1,015 µs under S and 50 under U on 4B, 801 and 39 on 0.6B. Part (4), tg128 against `ref`: 4B `e3c` -0.5%, `plan-s` -1.7%, `plan-u` -0.6%; 0.6B -1.4%, -5.8%, -1.5% (0.6B's counters cost above the 1% rule, so its time columns are disturbed as in run 10; its differences between modes stand). **By the rule, no cache:** U saves 56 µs per 4B token, under the 0.2 ms the rule demands; S costs 0.8-1.0 ms per token. The predictions were right on the hits and on U's key (under 5 µs predicted, 50 measured: the pointer walk over 830 nodes plus the hit guard's walk over the steps) and wrong on S's key (200-600 µs predicted, 801-1,015 measured: Common's compare costs 2-5x the estimate on the X100) and on what the cache could save: the lookup columns, read as differences of means (an inference, not a timed split), put the per-node contract check at about 490 µs of a 4B token and the expansion it replaces at about 105 µs (0.6B: about 357 and 37); the step list was never the cost, the contract check is, and both candidates as specified keep it on every graph (§3 (i)). The deck's "③ and ④ are redone only when the graph's structure changes" (`structural-experiments.md` R5) caches the contract check with the plan; our §3 (i) was stricter than the deck. llama.cpp's precedents trust the uid outright: CUDA in our base returns "no update required" on an equal uid with only an assert on the node count (`ggml-cuda.cu:2555-2559`); Hexagon upstream (`8ca4060b`) skips its fusion tagging, its per-node op loop, its weight repack and its kernel-parameter solve on `cached_uid == graph->uid` with no node check; RPC's client sends the uid alone and the server reruns the stored graph. Their trust rests on llama.cpp's reuse rule (a split keeps its uid only while the graph is reused with the same structure and shapes, `src/llama-graph.cpp:47-58`).

**Decision (user, 2026-10-31):** candidate S removed with its setting (Common's compare is not the K3's way to the deck's hit: 12 µs on AMD's x86 host, 0.8-1.0 ms here), candidate U kept, and a third form added for one more session per model, **step 3b**: the trusted uid hit, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=uid-trust`, in which an entry whose uid, node pointers and configuration word match is taken without the contract check (every miss and every uid-0 graph keeps today's check and expansion; without S there is no structural fallback, so a new uid or a uid of 0 is a rebuild, Hexagon's form). Predictions (estimates from this session's columns): host work per generated token 4B 613 to about 120 µs, 0.6B 406 to about 50; tg128 about +0.5% on 4B and up to +2% on 0.6B; logits identical. The probe "one `op_params` value changed inside the same objects under a kept uid" becomes a hit under `uid-trust` (the trust CUDA places in the uid; §3's candidate U text already says so) and is checked as such. The rule for 3b: `uid-trust` is adopted if it lowers the host work by at least 0.2 ms per generated 4B token against `e3c` with identical logits on both models; otherwise the cache is removed entirely and the contract check stays per graph.

### S1 step 3b spec: candidate S removed, the trusted uid hit (2026-10-31)

**Status:** written 2026-10-31 from step 3's result and decision (above); approved, built by the coder (Opus, effort high) 2026-10-31 and reviewed 2026-11-01 (three reviewers, one dimension each: the cache and `graph_compute`, the probes, the runner and the names; no blocker). Implemented as specified with these differences, all kept: (f) and (l)'s attention graph is stamped with its own uid (`k_attn_uid`), since under the one key a uid of 0 can never hit, so unstamped the word's mutation could not be caught there and (l) would say nothing; with the stamp "(f) one-chunk again" is 1/0 (the same object computed again), the one-chunk, split and both (l) graphs 0/1. Added from the review: (m), the first layer's own object with another uid, a miss by the uid alone (the one clause no probe decided; llama.cpp rebuilds every graph in the same buffer, `src/llama-graph.cpp:1316-1326`, so a rebuilt graph of another shape can carry the same node addresses and count, its new uid what tells it apart); (h) computed a second time, a miss again (an entry of uid 0 never hits; no probe decided that clause either); `w` stamped with the first layer's uid so that (j)'s refusal misses by the pointer walk; under `uid-trust` every hit's key time must equal its lookup time (the clocks' evidence that the check was skipped); the runner's mode column widened to 8 for `plan-ut`. The mutations on the Mac: (1) the pointer walk removed fails (e) under both and the key case "(2)" under `uid-trust`; (2) the word left out fails (l) under `uid` (where (f) still passes: the hit guard sees the split's other contract) and (f) split, both (l) graphs and the key case "(4)" under `uid-trust`; (3) `steps_match` always true fails the guard's "asked with B" and cases (2)-(4); the uid comparison removed fails (m) under both; the uid-0 clause removed fails the matmul checks before `check_plan` runs (NMSE inf: unstamped graphs rebuilt at the same addresses took a stale entry), so (h)'s second run's catch is by reading, not observed. The Mac checks of item 5 all pass (the probes 13/13, 12/12, 12/12; 21, 21 and 20 graphs; test-backend-ops MUL_MAT 3/3 and FLASH_ATTN_EXT 1324/1324 under both; ThreadSanitizer 0 warnings under both; the loader's 8 names). Line numbers are `02a1cb9ee`'s (step 3 committed): `flagos-spacemit.cpp` `graph_compute` :642-770 (the plan section :648-652, :669-671, :676-700 the `expand` lambda, :704-736 the contract loop, :738-764 the lookup, the store and the counters), `core/flagos-spacemit-plan.{h,cpp}`, `tests/check_spacemit.cpp` `plan_guard_probe` :3426, `check_plan` :3517-3790, `scripts/structural-experiments.sh` :55-56, :100, :114-115, :145, :401-402, :434-439, :523.

**Goal of the step:** one cache form, keyed by the split's uid, the node objects and the configuration word, in two settings that differ only in whether a hit skips the per-node contract check: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=uid` (step 3's candidate U with the hit guard, kept for the comparison) and `uid-trust` (the entry taken before the contract check; llama.cpp's CUDA and Hexagon form, the deck's "③ and ④ redone only when the graph's structure changes"). `none` stays the default until the K3 decides. Candidate S and its use of Common's `flagos_graph_plan_cache` are removed, so the core's plan code depends on nothing of Common; a uid of 0 or a new uid is a miss (an expansion and a store), as Hexagon. Outputs bit-identical under all three settings.

**1. `core/flagos-spacemit-plan.{h,cpp}`.** Removed: the `flagos-graph-plan.h` include, `common_`, `pending_`, `key_`, `mode_`, `spacemit_plan_mode::structure`, `spacemit_plan_result::hit_structure`, `rebound` and the `rebinds` counter (a uid hit requires the same node objects, so a rebind cannot happen), `hits_structure`. The modes: `none`, `uid`, `uid_trust` (the switch values `none|uid|uid-trust`; anything else reported once and ignored, as today). The entry: `valid_`, `uid_`, `word_`, `nodes_`, `steps_`, `index_`, `names_`, `n_contract_nodes_`, the two sizes, as today. The key, the same under both settings: `cgraph->uid != 0 && cgraph->uid == uid_ && word == word_ && n_nodes == nodes_.size() && std::equal(nodes_, cgraph->nodes)` (the pointer walk stays: CUDA asserts only the count, Hexagon checks nothing; the walk costs a few µs on 830 nodes and catches a reused uid on other objects, probe (e)). Two lookups:
- `probe(cgraph, word, want_names, steps, names, scratch_size, act_size)` for `uid_trust`: the key alone; on a hit fills the outputs from the entry (the steps' nodes are the entry's, which are this graph's) and returns `hit_uid`; else `miss`. No `node_ops`.
- `lookup(mode, cgraph, word, want_names, node_ops, steps, names, scratch_size, act_size)` for `uid`: the key, then `steps_match(node_ops)` as today (`flagos-spacemit-plan.cpp:81-113`); `hit_uid` or `miss`.
- `store(cgraph, word, node_ops, steps, names, scratch_size, act_size)`: as today (`:176-212`) without `mode` and `pending_`; stored after every miss, a uid of 0 included (the entry is always the last plan; a uid-0 entry can never hit, which keeps the probes' expectations simple).
- The counters: `hits_uid`, `misses`, `lookup_us`, `key_us`; `spacemit_plan_counters_add(result, lookup_us, key_us)`.

**2. `graph_compute` after the step** (the order matters; `:648-764`):
1. `plan_mode`, `clocked`, `start`, the `share` handling and `planned = plan_mode != none && !share`, as today (:651-671).
2. The configuration word computed here when `planned` (today at :744-745): `generation` from `ctx->switches_generation`, `word = (generation << 1) | share`.
3. **`uid_trust` only:** `plan = ctx->plan.probe(cgraph, word, op_stats != nullptr, ctx->steps, ctx->step_names, scratch_size, act_size)`. On `hit_uid` the contract loop (:708-736) is skipped entirely: no `spacemit_find_op`, no `spacemit_data_ready` (the node objects are the ones checked at the store, and llama.cpp allocates a graph only when it builds it, `src/llama-context.cpp:1339-1373`), no `expand`. When `timed`, the counters' `rows` and `n_kv` are still read: a scan over the nodes that stops once both are found (the first `MUL_MAT`'s `src[1]->ne[1]`, the first `FLASH_ATTN_EXT`'s `src[1]->ne[1]`), the counters' own cost, run only when `timed`.
4. Otherwise (`none`, `uid`, or a probe miss under `uid_trust`): the contract loop as today, with `node_ops` filled when `planned`, `expand` when not. Then, when `planned`: under `uid`, `lookup(...)`; under `uid_trust` after a probe miss, the result is `miss` without a second lookup. On a miss: `expand` every node with a contract (:749-753) and `store`.
5. The clocks: `checked` is taken after the contract loop ran, or equals `start` when the loop was skipped; `lookup_us = in_hand - start` (from the top of `graph_compute` to the step list in hand, as today) and `key_us = in_hand - checked`, so on a trusted hit the two are equal and both are the probe's cost. The counters added when `planned`, as today (:762-764); the stats line's fields become `plan_hits_uid`, `plan_misses`, `plan_lookup_us`, `plan_key_us` (`plan_hits_structure` and `plan_rebinds` removed, :326-331, :347-354, :370-371).
6. The rest unchanged (the workspace, the mutex, the replay, the stats).
The comments at :648-650 and in `flagos-spacemit-plan.h`'s header name the two settings and the trust: under `uid-trust` a hit runs the cached step list on the current node objects without re-checking their contracts, so a change to a node's operands or placement under a kept uid and the same objects is not seen; llama.cpp never does this (a reused graph keeps its tensors and their allocations; the uid changes when the graph is rebuilt), and the per-graph fail-closed rule of "S1 design" §3 (i) then holds for every miss and for every graph the scheduler did not stamp.

**3. The check program (`check_plan`, `:3517-3790`; `plan_guard_probe`, `:3426`).** The settings loop runs `none`, `uid`, `uid-trust`; `expect(name, hits_uid, misses)` replaces the four-count form; every output still against the CPU and, for the two cache settings, byte for byte against `none`'s. The probes' expectations (hits by uid / misses), under both cache settings unless stated:
- (a) the same object twice: 0/1 then 1/0.
- (e) the uid stamped on other objects of the same structure: 0/1 (a miss under both: the pointer walk; the probe that fails mutation 1).
- (b) the same structure as new objects, uid 0: 0/1; (c) RoPE's base changed (another graph): 0/1; (d) 7 tokens: 0/1.
- (g) the first layer again after the larger graph grew the workspace: 0/1 (the entry was (d)'s), then its plan replayed in the grown workspace: 1/0.
- (h) a graph view of it (uid 0): 0/1 under both (no structural path any more).
- (f) `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` flipped between two one-token attention graphs: 0/1 each, the step list the other variant's by the executor's step counts, as today; (l) the same value set again: 0/1 by the word alone.
- (j), under `uid`: as today (the CPU-buffer weights refused before any lookup, the first layer a hit after each refusal, `plan_guard_probe` on the cache with the stand-in contracts). Under `uid-trust`: the first refusal (the same structure with its weights in a CPU buffer, new objects) as today: the probe misses on the objects, the contract check refuses the graph, and a refused graph returns before the counters, so nothing is counted, as today; the second refusal, the first layer's own object with one `MUL_MAT` pointed at a CPU-buffer weight, **is not run under `uid-trust`**: the probe would hit and the IME kernel would read an unrepacked weight; that is exactly the check the trust gives up, stated in the probe's comment, with the sentence that llama.cpp's reuse never changes a reused graph's tensors. In its place `plan_guard_probe` under `uid-trust` exercises the key alone through `probe()`: (1) the same uid, nodes and word as stored: a hit with the stored sizes and steps; (2) one node pointer replaced in a copy of the node array: a miss; (3) one node fewer: a miss; (4) another word: a miss; (5) uid 0 with the same nodes: a miss.
- (k) under `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE=shared` nothing counted; (i) under `none` no counter moves.
- The summary line names the probes as today with the new counts and without the rebind field; the `none` setting keeps "outputs kept", the others "outputs identical to none's".
The loader check's name count stays 8 (`spacemit_switches_generation` is still served).

**4. The runner (`scripts/structural-experiments.sh`).** The mode `plan-s` removed; `plan-u` (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=uid`) kept; `plan-ut` (`uid-trust`) added at :55-56, :100, :114-115, :145, :523 (`EXPS=plan` runs `ref e3c plan-u plan-ut`, the KLD modes `plan-u plan-ut`); the E3c summary's columns `p.uid`, `p.miss`, `lookup`, `key` (:434-439; `p.str` and `p.rebind` dropped) and its header lines (:401-402) say so; the usage text likewise.

Decision rule** (step 3's result, the decision paragraph above): `uid-trust` is adopted if it lowers the host work by at least 0.2 ms per generated Qwen3-4B token against `e3c` with identical logits on both models; then it becomes the default path (the switch kept test-only as `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none|uid` for comparisons, `uid` the checked form; "S1 design" §3 (i) amended: the contract check runs on every miss and every unstamped graph, a hit trusts the uid as CUDA and Hexagon do), the acceptance runner then run on both models under the new default. **Result on the K3 (2026-11-01; `build.md` §7 "S1 step 3b on the K3"; `structural-experiments.md` run 12).** The patch on `02a1cb9ee`; the check program passed under the three settings; `EXPS="plan" REPS=3 DEPTH=0`, Qwen3-4B with the perplexity text, Qwen3-0.6B without; the modes `ref`, `e3c`, `plan-u`, `plan-ut` interleaved, three `llama-bench` runs each. The hits as predicted: 0.99 per generated token under both settings (the first graph of a run the miss). The logits on Qwen3-4B the reference's under both (perplexity 9.7736 at 512-token batches and 9.7619 at one token, mean KLD 0.000000, maximum 5.4e-5 and 5.7e-5, same top p 100%, the base file's floor); the text check ran under `ref` alone (`EXPS=plan` adds no mode to it; corrected 2026-11-01: this paragraph said "the generated text identical in every mode", which no run tested), so the modes' sameness is the KLD's, on 4B only. The host work per generated token (µs, with the counters): Qwen3-4B `e3c` 630, `plan-u` 575, `plan-ut` 46; Qwen3-0.6B 446, 486, 39; the lookup from the top of `graph_compute` 616 / 561 / 20 on 4B and 435 / 473 / 16 on 0.6B; the key alone 24 and 18 µs on 4B, 19 and 15 on 0.6B (`uid`, `uid-trust`). tg128 against `ref` (4B `ref` 106.48 pp128, 11.03 tg128; 0.6B 692.28, 54.87): 4B `e3c` -0.8%, `plan-u` -0.8%, `plan-ut` -0.2%; 0.6B -1.7%, -1.8%, +0.4%; pp128 within 0.4% on 4B, `plan-ut` +1.3% on 0.6B within one spread of `ref`'s. **By the rule of item 7, `uid-trust` is adopted:** it removes 584 µs of host work per 4B token against `e3c` (200 required) and 407 on 0.6B, with identical logits. The predictions held: the host left 46 and 39 µs (50-120 and 30-80 predicted), the key 18 and 15 (5-20). The speed gain reads +0.6% on 4B and +2.2% on 0.6B against the counted modes and -0.2% / +0.4% against `ref`, since the counters cost `e3c` 0.8% and 1.7% in this run and `plan-ut` carries them; the gain without counters is an estimate of about +0.5% on 4B and +2% on 0.6B until the A/B of step 3c measures it. The token time itself fell 514 µs on 4B and 390 on 0.6B (91,398 to 90,884; 18,540 to 18,150), the deck's idle share from 2.13% to 1.50% and from 7.56% to 5.56%. Run 11's inference is confirmed by the measurement: the per-node contract check was about 0.55 ms of a 4B token, and skipping it on a hit is what the cache pays with.

**Decision (user, 2026-11-01):** `uid-trust` becomes the default path (step 3c, below). The checked form `uid` is removed with it: it was kept to isolate the contract check's cost from the key's, which run 12 did; it is no performance fallback (its host cost equals `none`'s), no better diagnostic (a wrong trust shows as outputs differing between `uid-trust` and `none`, which the check program and the KLD runs compare), and no precedent keeps a checked form beside the trusted one (CUDA, Hexagon, the deck). The switch stays test-only as `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none|uid-trust`.

### S1 step 3c spec: the trusted uid hit as the default, the checked form removed (2026-11-01)

**Status:** written 2026-11-01 from step 3b's result and decision; approved by the user the same day ("as planned, no need to make it smaller"); implemented by the coder (Opus, effort high) on `65f31604b` and reviewed by three reviewers (no blocker; one fix round). Line numbers are the step 3b tree's. Deviations from the items, all in the fix round: `plan-ut` removed from the runner rather than kept (no `EXPS` selects a mode by name, so it was unreachable, and it equals `e3c` under the new default); the key probe (`plan_key_probe`, renamed from `plan_guard_probe`) has seven cases, not five (one node more, and the names wanted against an entry stored without them, each a clause of the key no other probe reached; its uid case asks with another stamped uid, the uid-equality clause); a probe (o) that a hit really skips the check (one MUL_MAT's compute flag cleared after the store: under `none` 2 steps fewer, under `uid-trust` a hit with the stored count, the bytes equal; the flag never changes on a reused graph in llama.cpp, so a probe of the trust, not a scenario); (n) also checks the old `uid` value falls to the default with one warning; the key-time assertion described as the counters' definition, not as evidence of the skip. On the Mac (2026-11-01): the check program 15 probes under `none` and 14 under `uid-trust`, all outputs identical, under unset, `none`, `uid-trust`, a bogus value and `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_THREADS=1`; `test-backend-ops` MUL_MAT and FLASH_ATTN_EXT unset and under `none`; ThreadSanitizer clean; the riscv64 syntax check 0 errors on the two provider files; seven mutations caught (the four of item 6, the node-count clause, the names clause, the trusted branch made to run the loop). Run on the K3 the same day and **accepted** (the result at the end of this section).

**Goal:** the plan cache of step 3b with one cached form, on by default: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN` unset or `uid-trust` takes the entry before the contract check on a hit; `none` (test-only) is the full path, the contract check and the expansion on every graph. The `uid` setting, the hit guard and everything that served only it are removed. Outputs bit-identical under both settings. No kernel change, no change to Common or to upstream.

**1. `core/flagos-spacemit-plan.{h,cpp}`.** The modes `none` and `uid_trust`; `spacemit_plan_mode_get()` returns `uid_trust` when the switch is unset or empty, `none` for `none`, `uid_trust` for `uid-trust`, and warns once and returns `uid_trust` for anything else (the default, as the other switches do). Removed: `lookup()`, `steps_match()`, `index_`, `n_contract_nodes_`, the `node_ops` parameter of `store()`, the `spacemit_op` forward declaration and the `flagos-spacemit-ops.h` include. Kept: `probe()`, `store(cgraph, word, steps, names, scratch_size, act_size)`, `key_matches`, `fill`, the entry (`valid_`, `uid_`, `word_`, `nodes_`, `steps_`, `names_`, the two sizes), the four counters and `spacemit_plan_counters_add`. The header comment says the default and what the trust gives up, as today's does.

**2. `graph_compute`.** The `planned` flag becomes `cached = plan_mode == uid_trust && !share`; the word computed when `cached`; the probe when `cached`; on a hit the loop skipped as today (step 3b item 2.3, the `rows`/`n_kv` scan when `timed`); otherwise the contract loop expands as it goes under both settings (today's `none` branch: `expand(node, op)` inside the loop; `ctx->node_ops` removed), and after it, when `cached`, `store(...)`. The clocks and the counters as today (`checked` equals `start` on a trusted hit). The comment above `graph_compute` states the rule: every node is checked at its store, and a hit trusts that check under the uid llama.cpp keeps only while the graph is reused unchanged.

**3. The check program.** `check_plan`'s settings loop runs `none` and `uid-trust`; `expect(name, hits_uid, misses)` as today; the expectations as step 3b's for these two settings, with (j)'s swapped-weight case gone (it ran under `uid` only) and `plan_guard_probe` reduced to the five key cases through `probe()` (the stand-in contracts A and B and the six guard cases go). One new assertion before the loop: with `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN` unset (`env_override` to `nullptr`) `spacemit_plan_mode_get()` returns `uid_trust`, and with `none` it returns `none`. The summary line names "the key alone" under `uid-trust`. The other checks (the layer, attention, matmul, `test-backend-ops`) now run under the default; their graphs carry uid 0 (`ggml_backend_graph_compute` on a plain graph), so every one of them is a miss and their expectations do not move.

**4. The runner.** `plan-u` removed. `plan-ut` kept as the explicit setting with the counters (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=uid-trust`, the same as `e3c` under the new default, kept so that old command lines still run). Added `plan-n`: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLAN=none` without counters (build A), and `plan-nc`: `none` with the counters. `EXPS=plan` runs for bench `ref plan-n e3c plan-nc` (the A/B without counters is `ref` against `plan-n`; the host columns are `e3c` against `plan-nc`), for the KLD runs `plan-n` against `ref` (the reference is now the cache; identical logits either way), the depth runs `ref plan-n`. The usage text and the E3c header say so.

**5. Docs with the step (Fable):** "S1 design" §3 (i) amended as the rule of step 3b's item 7 says (the contract check runs at every miss and on every graph the scheduler did not stamp; a hit trusts the uid, as CUDA and Hexagon do, under llama.cpp's reuse rule), §3 (ii) made the one key; `structural-experiments.md`'s switch row; `build.md`'s switch table gains the row; `PROJECT.md` §1 and §8.

**6. On the Mac:** `cmake --build build-host`; the check program with the switch unset, `none`, `uid-trust`, and unset with `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_THREADS=1`; `test-backend-ops` MUL_MAT and FLASH_ATTN_EXT with the switch unset and `none`; ThreadSanitizer unset; `scripts/rvv-syntax-check.sh`; `grep -n 'steps_match\|node_ops\|plan-u\b' providers/spacemit/ docs/flagos-k3/scripts/` prints nothing. ### S1 step 4: the workspace and capture driver, closed without code (2026-11-02)

**Status:** closed 2026-11-02 without code. **Item 5 against the code** ("the core owns the workspace and records the split's steps once per structure; the plugin replays them; the per-token arguments (the KV length) updated in place"):

| Item 5 | In the code | Since |
|---|---|---|

| The split's steps are recorded once | the plan cache: `probe` before the contract check (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:683-691`), `store` after a miss's check (`:768-771`); once per uid, which llama.cpp renews when it rebuilds the graph (A4, "S1 design" §3) | step 3c |
| The plugin replays them | `table->replay` under the device's mutex (`:799-820`); `plugin_replay` runs one launch, or one per step under the test flags (`plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp:356-378`) | step 1 |
| The per-token arguments in place | no code, by the note's design: the steps carry node pointers and the kernels read the node at run time (the KV length, the rows, the data pointers), so there is no `update` ("S1 design" §2, "Not in the table"); measured in run 13 on Qwen3-4B: 0.99 of the generated tokens' graphs hit, and the logits with the cache on and off identical at 512-token batches and at one token per ubatch (mean KLD 0.000000, the base file's floor; maximum 5.4e-5 and 5.7e-5; same top p 100%) | the note (2026-10-29) |
| The stream's lifetime | `stream_create` in `init_backend` (`:922`), `stream_destroy` with the backend context (`:74-77`) | step 1 |

**Checked on the Mac (2026-11-02, read-only greps):** the core's files (`flagos-spacemit.cpp`, `flagos-spacemit-ops.{h,cpp}`, `core/`) include one plugin-side header, `flagos-spacemit-exec.h` for the counters' struct (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:7`), and no kernel header; they call no plugin-side function (the one name the grep finds, `spacemit_mul_mat_ime_supported` at `flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp:140`, is the core's own structural check); they reach the plugin through the table and three names by `get_proc_address` (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:933-937`). Only the executor includes spine-runtime's header (`flagos-spacemit-exec.cpp`); the core's files have no build-dependent code (their one conditional is the loader's `_GNU_SOURCE` on Linux), and the vector and IME flags are set on the kernel files alone (`provider.cmake:75-81`).

**What item 5 leaves, and where each goes:**
1. The workspace's two regions are chosen by the op's name: `graph_compute` gives a `MUL_MAT`'s steps their own region after the other ops' scratch (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:717`, `:785`), so that under the test-only `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_QUANTIZE=shared` the quantized activations outlive the ops between two matmuls of one input. With that mode's overlap test (`:697-708`) it is the only form S1 item 3's alias check has. Nothing in the default path depends on it: an op's steps are appended together (`:709-714`) and run back to back with a barrier after each, and no two-step op leaves a graph tensor unwritten, so there is no required output to check. Recommended: with item 3's required-output and alias checks, to M4, where the fate of the sharing (`fusion-and-splitting.md` §7, rank 1) decides between a property of the contract and a single region; agreed by the user 2026-11-02 (both to M4; step 5 does not touch them).
2. The capture driver sits in the provider's root file beside the vtables, not under `core/`: the directory move is S2's (step 1's spec, §1).
3. The counters' struct reaches the core through `flagos-spacemit-exec.h`: step 5's (step 2's spec, §6).

**S1's exit after this step:** the code steps (1) to (4) are done and (5) is next. The exit's "the core builds without `libspert` and without the IME toolchain" holds by the code read above, and the Mac's builds compile the same core sources without either; the full proof is S2's separate library.

### S1 step 5 spec: the diagnostics under the deck's names (2026-11-02)

**Status:** written 2026-11-02 from "S1 design" §1 and §7 (the exit's step (5)) and `structural-review.md` G8 (the deck's five diagnostics against ours); choices A-F agreed by the user the same day; built on the Mac 2026-11-02 (the coder, Opus, from this spec: the C++ side; its run ended before §5 and §6, which a second coder finished from the same spec), every check of §8 passed on the Mac but one whose expectation was wrong ("Result on the Mac" below); accepted on the K3 2026-11-02 ("Result on the K3" below). Line numbers are HEAD `7543a92b7`'s (the cleanup's commit), every anchor re-read 2026-11-02 after the cleanup moved them; the spec was amended the same day for what the cleanup removed (the ordered launches, the removed switches) and for its result (the runner's base is 40 checks, not 39), and gained two points the first version left implicit: the plan cache's names flag (§2, `TRACE_LAUNCH`) and the runner's signal for attention disabled by name (§5). The choices it makes, each the user's to overturn: (A) the old names are replaced, not aliased: a set old name warns once and names the new one, nothing else; (B) `KERNEL_VARIANTS=0` is the reference kernels, read by the plugin, since the variant choice sits inside the kernels for S1 ("S1 design" §4); the per-op tuning switches keep their names; (C) the diagnostics code moves out of `flagos-spacemit.cpp` into `core/flagos-spacemit-diagnostics.{h,cpp}` (the §7 row's destination), a mechanical move; (D) the loader's path variable takes the deck's name `GGML_FLAGOS_PROVIDER_PATH` (R6); (E) the core's other switches (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_DISABLE`, `PLAN`, `QUANTIZE`, `TEST_DEVICE_TYPE`) keep their names: the deck names none of them, and the deck's five are the step; (F) the K3 run is the acceptance runner on both models, since the runner and both scripts change with the names.

**Goal:** the five diagnostics of the deck's page 7 exist under the deck's names, read by the core where the core acts on them: `GGML_FLAGOS_STATS` and `GGML_FLAGOS_OP_STATS` (today's two counter files), `GGML_FLAGOS_KERNEL_VARIANTS=0` (today's reference kernels), `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS` (new: named ops back to the CPU), `GGML_FLAGOS_TRACE_LAUNCH` (new: the steps by name, the failing one named), `GGML_FLAGOS_SYNC_LAUNCH` (today's one launch per step); the core includes no plugin header (the counters' struct in a core header); outputs bit-identical, speed within spread, one build.

**Approach, and the alternatives.** Our diagnostics were 18 `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_*` variables on 2026-10-26 (`structural-review.md` G8), 16 after the cleanup (the loader's `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN` among them); the deck's are five `GGML_FLAGOS_*` names, and Denglin already reads `GGML_FLAGOS_SYNC_LAUNCH` with the deck's meaning (`flagos-denglin.cpp:371`), so a core-wide name is the convention in this tree too. The step renames what maps onto the deck's five, adds the two the deck has and we lack (`DISABLE_OPS`, `TRACE_LAUNCH`: cheap, as G8 found), and leaves the plugin's own switches alone (the kernels' `MM`, `ATTN`; the executor's `TEST_*`; `STREAM`, `TEST_NO_HUGE_PAGES`): they tune and test the kernel package, which the deck does not name. Alternatives: (a) aliases (both names read): every switch read twice forever and two names in every doc; loses. (b) `KERNEL_VARIANTS` read by the core and handed to the plugin through the table: no member carries it and the choice is not the core's until M3's `when`; loses for S1. (c) `DISABLE_OPS` as a regex, Hexagon's `GGML_HEXAGON_OPFILTER` (`upstream-precedents.md` §3): a comma list of ggml op names is what the deck shows (`DISABLE_OPS=...`) and needs no `<regex>`; a regex can come later. (d) The trace inside the plugin's tile loop (which knows the failing step): the plugin would log, and the core's step list is the natural place; with `SYNC_LAUNCH` the core names the failing step itself by issuing one replay per step, so the plugin is untouched.

**Predictions (before the run):** outputs bit-identical (no kernel change, the same step lists, the same workspace); the check program and every `test-backend-ops` set pass on the Mac; the acceptance runner on the K3 with the perplexity and KLDs of 2026-10-27 to the last digit; tg128 and pp128 within spread (per graph: one more `getenv`, under 0.2 µs on the X100, an estimate; `DISABLE_OPS` parsed once; the trace a bool test when off).

**1. Files.** New: `core/flagos-spacemit-diagnostics.h` and `.cpp` (the switches of §2-§6, the two counter classes, `spacemit_role` and `spacemit_step_label`, moved from `flagos-spacemit.cpp:226-630` as they are; the header declares what `graph_compute` and the check program use), `core/flagos-spacemit-stats.h` (`spacemit_run_stats`, moved from `flagos-spacemit-exec.h:17-43` unchanged, with `SPACEMIT_MAX_TILES` from `flagos-spacemit-ops.h` if the include order needs it). Changed: `flagos-spacemit.cpp` (the move; `graph_compute` on the new names; `flagos-spacemit-exec.h` no longer included), `flagos-spacemit-exec.h` (includes `core/flagos-spacemit-stats.h`), `flagos-spacemit-ops.{h,cpp}` (`DISABLE_OPS` in `spacemit_find_op`), `core/flagos-spacemit-loader.cpp` (the path variable), `flagos-spacemit-kernels.cpp` (`KERNEL_VARIANTS`), `provider.cmake` (the new sources), `tests/check_spacemit.cpp` (the renamed `env_override`s; `check_diagnostics`), `tools/spacemit_check.py`, `docs/flagos-k3/scripts/structural-experiments.sh`, `fusion-experiments.sh`, `op-stats.py` (the names). No kernel body changes.

**2. The names, one by one** (every old name below, when set, is reported once at warning level, "FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_X is now GGML_FLAGOS_Y", and otherwise ignored; a small `spacemit_renamed(old, new)` helper in the diagnostics file, called where the new name is read):
- `GGML_FLAGOS_STATS=<file>` replaces `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_STATS` (`:287`, E3c's per-token budget); `GGML_FLAGOS_OP_STATS=<file>` replaces `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_OP_STATS` (`:482`, the op counters). The deck's `STATS=1` prints counts; ours write JSON lines at exit, so the value `1` or `-` writes them to stderr (the file branch with `stderr` in place of `fopen`), any other value is a path. The JSON fields, the kinds and `scripts/op-stats.py`'s reading are unchanged.
- `GGML_FLAGOS_KERNEL_VARIANTS=0` replaces `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_REFERENCE=1` (`flagos-spacemit-kernels.cpp:18`, the portable reference kernels on the AI cores; read once); unset or any other value keeps the variants. Plugin-side (choice B).
- `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS=<op>[,<op>...]`, new: ggml op names as `ggml_op_name` spells them (`MUL_MAT`, `FLASH_ATTN_EXT`, `GLU`, ...; case as given, spaces trimmed); parsed once into a `bool disabled[GGML_OP_COUNT]` by a function in the diagnostics file; an unknown name warns once and is ignored. `spacemit_find_op` (`flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp:253`) returns `nullptr` first thing for a disabled op, so placement and execution agree (`supports_op`, `:942-944`, and `graph_compute`, `:749`, both call it); the ops stay on the CPU and the scheduler copies their operands out of our buffers as it does for any unsupported node. Note for the docs: disabling `MUL_MAT` makes every weight copied back per graph (read back as the nearest ggml layout), a diagnostic, not a mode.
- `GGML_FLAGOS_TRACE_LAUNCH=1`, new, read once: before each replay the core logs at `GGML_LOG_INFO` one line per step, `FlagOS SpacemiT: step <i>/<n> <contract name> <node name> <op> [<ne0>,<ne1>,<ne2>,<ne3>] <src0 name> <src0 type>` (the contract name from `ctx->step_names`, which is filled when the trace or the op counters are on: the condition at `:707` becomes `op_stats != nullptr || trace`, and so does the plan cache's `want_names` argument at `:686`, since on a hit the names come from the entry, which serves them only if it was stored with them (`core/flagos-spacemit-plan.cpp:60-66`): one flag `want_names = op_stats != nullptr || trace`, used at both sites), then `launch <n> steps` and after the replay `launch <n> steps: <status name>`. With `SYNC_LAUNCH` also on, the core issues one `replay` per step (`n = 1`, the flag set) and logs each step's status, so the first failing step is named by the core; the counters' `launches` count the launches as before. The plugin is untouched.
- `GGML_FLAGOS_SYNC_LAUNCH=1` replaces `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_LAUNCH=per-step` (`:226-247`): any value but `0` or empty is on (Denglin's reading of the same name, `flagos-denglin.cpp:371`, is "set"); read at every graph as today, so a benchmark can switch. Since the cleanup `per-step` is `TEST_LAUNCH`'s only value, so the old switch goes entirely (set, it warns once with the new name).
- `GGML_FLAGOS_PROVIDER_PATH=<path>` replaces `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN` (`core/flagos-spacemit-loader.cpp:79`; choice D): that path only, no fallback, as today.
- Unchanged (choice E and the plugin's): `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_DISABLE`, `PLAN`, `QUANTIZE`, `TEST_DEVICE_TYPE`, `STREAM`, `MM`, `ATTN`, `TEST_THREADS`, `TEST_TCM`, `TEST_FAIL_NODE`, `TEST_NO_HUGE_PAGES`.

### S1 step 5 follow-up: the warnings llama.cpp's fit step hides (spec, 2026-11-03)

**Status:** written 2026-11-03 from a finding on the Mac (`build.md` §7, the `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS` row); the user chose to fix it before the K3 runs of the one-token probes. Built on the Mac 2026-11-03 (the coder, Opus, from this spec; one comment reworded in the main session); every check below passed but ThreadSanitizer, which no longer runs on this Mac ("Result on the Mac" below); accepted on the K3 the same day ("Result on the K3" below).

**The fault (tested on the Mac's simulated device, `llama-completion` with parameter fitting on, the default, against `-fit off`).** llama.cpp's tools first load the model in a trial run that fits parameters to memory (`common/common.h:468`, `fit_params = true`; `common/common.cpp:1239-1247`), and during it pass every log line below ERROR on as DEBUG (`common/fit.cpp:49-53`), which is dropped below `-lv 5`. A once-only warning first triggered inside that trial never shows. Three are: `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS` ("those ops stay on the CPU" and "... is no ggml op name; ignored", `core/flagos-spacemit-diagnostics.cpp:71`, `:78`; first triggered by `spacemit_find_op` under `supports_op`), `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` with an unknown value (`flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:187`; first triggered by the attention contracts' `supports`, `:464-478`) and `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_DEVICE_TYPE` (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:504`, `:508`; first triggered by llama.cpp asking the device's type): 0 lines with fitting on, 1 with `-fit off`. Visible either way, since first triggered after the trial: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_MM`, `QUANTIZE` and `PLAN` with an unknown value, the old names `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_STATS`, `TEST_OP_STATS`, `TEST_LAUNCH` (all read in `graph_compute`) and `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_PLUGIN` (at registration). `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_REFERENCE`'s is read only in the K3's kernel bodies (`flagos-spacemit-ime.cpp:303`, `:339`, `flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:376`, `:450`, `flagos-spacemit-kernels.cpp:416`, `:429`), so at the first compute, after the trial: our reading, not testable on the Mac. The switches themselves work under fitting (the ops go to the CPU, the split counts show it); only the message is lost.

**The change: read the three switches at registration,** which comes before the trial (the probe, `flagos-spacemit.cpp:600-685`, runs when the backend registers, while llama.cpp parses its arguments). The value is the same either way: each switch is read from the environment once per process, or once per reset in the check program.
1. `flagos-spacemit.cpp`, `spacemit_probe_impl`: after the contracts bind and the device fields are set, before `g_present = true`, call `spacemit_device_type(&g_device.device)` and `spacemit_op_disabled(GGML_OP_NONE)`, their results discarded, with a comment naming the reason (the trial load and `common/fit.cpp:49-53`). Both are core-side.
2. `flagos-spacemit-attn.{h,cpp}`: a new `void spacemit_flash_attn_read_switch()`, outside the anonymous namespace, that calls `split_setting_from_env()`; declared in the header beside `spacemit_flash_attn_split`.
3. `plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp`, `plugin_device_open`: call `spacemit_flash_attn_read_switch()` once the device is made. The plugin side reads its own switch; the core includes no plugin header (D10).

**Alternatives, and why they lose.** Logging the three at ERROR, which the trial passes on: the smallest change, but a configuration notice is no error, and the check program and the runner would report errors. Warning at every backend initialization instead of once: the real load's context comes after the trial, so it would show, but each switch's parse-once structure would have to change, and the trial's copy is still demoted. Documenting `-fit off`: no code, but a diagnostic that cannot announce itself in llama.cpp's own tools is the fault. A check in the acceptance runner that the warning reaches `llama-perplexity`'s log: a regression test on the K3, left out of this change (smallest change; the user's to add).

**Not changed:** every other switch and warning, the default paths (nothing the switches decide changes), the runner, the check program.

**Checks on the Mac:** the host-device build (`build-host`) of `flagos-check-spacemit`, `test-backend-ops`, `llama-completion` and `llama-perplexity` without warnings; `./build-host/bin/flagos-check-spacemit --expect-device` all passed under the defaults, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_THREADS=1`, `GGML_FLAGOS_SYNC_LAUNCH=1`, `GGML_FLAGOS_KERNEL_VARIANTS=0` and `GGML_FLAGOS_TRACE_LAUNCH=1`; `test-backend-ops test -b FlagOS:SpacemiT:0` for `ADD`, `MUL_MAT` and `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` with their counts; `llama-completion` (`-p hi -n 1 -no-cnv -t 8 -fa on`, fitting on) prints each of `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS=FLASH_ATTN_EXT`, `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS=NOT_AN_OP`, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN=bogus`, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_DEVICE_TYPE=bogus` and `=accel`'s warning exactly once, the visible ones above still exactly once, and none of them without a switch set; the same for `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS=FLASH_ATTN_EXT` in `llama-perplexity`; ThreadSanitizer (`build-tsan`, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_TEST_THREADS=1`) 0 reports, since the probe now takes the diagnostics' mutex while holding its own; the build without the host device (`build-nohost/`; `build/` has the provider off) "device checks skipped"; `scripts/rvv-syntax-check.sh` 0 errors on the three changed sources. **On the K3 (the user):** the acceptance runner with `--build` on Qwen3-0.6B (43/43, the logits of 2026-10-27 to the last digit, speed within spread), then the one-token probes, whose run with attention disabled now shows the warning in its log.

**Result on the Mac (2026-11-03; on `6cf86f21b`).** Built as specified: `spacemit_probe_impl` reads the device type and the disabled ops before `g_present = true` (`flagos-spacemit.cpp`, +5 lines), `spacemit_flash_attn_read_switch` (`flagos-spacemit-attn.{h,cpp}`, +6), called from `plugin_device_open` (`plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp`, +2). Checks: the host-device build without warnings; the check program all passed under the five settings; `test-backend-ops` ADD 54/54, MUL_MAT 3/3, FLASH_ATTN_EXT 1324/1324; in `llama-completion` with fitting on, the warnings of `DISABLE_OPS=FLASH_ATTN_EXT`, `DISABLE_OPS=NOT_AN_OP`, `ATTN=bogus`, `TEST_DEVICE_TYPE=bogus` and `=accel` exactly once each, now at registration (about 0.3 ms into the run, against 1.9-3.2 s for the warnings that were already visible), those of `MM`, `QUANTIZE`, `PLAN` and `TEST_STATS` still once, none without a switch; in `llama-perplexity` the disabled-ops warning once, with 34 graph splits; the build without the host device "device checks skipped"; the riscv64 syntax check 0 errors on the three sources. The main session re-ran the build, the check program, three of the warning cases and the no-switch case: the same. The 4096-context probe's run with attention disabled (`llama-perplexity -c 4096 -b 4096 -ub 1`, `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS=FLASH_ATTN_EXT`, a Llama-3.2-1B stand-in on the simulated device) re-run on the new build: the warning in its log at registration, 34 graph splits, and every KLD figure the previous build's to the last digit (mean 0.001179, maximum 0.009480, 99.9% 0.008906, same top p 97.557 %), so the fix changed no output there. Not run: ThreadSanitizer, which aborts before any program starts on this Mac since its OS changed during the session (Darwin 23.6 to 27.2; a three-line threaded program aborts the same way, "Interceptors are not working"), likely the Command Line Tools' runtime predating the OS, not confirmed. The lock order read in the code instead: the probe takes the diagnostics' mutex inside its own, and nothing under the diagnostics' mutex takes the probe's (`core/flagos-spacemit-diagnostics.cpp:200-262`), so the order is one-way.
