**Result on the K3 (2026-11-03; pulled from a bundle onto `6cf86f21b`; `build.md` §7 "S1 step 5 follow-up on the K3").** The acceptance runner with `--build` on Qwen3-0.6B: 43/43, both KLDs 2026-10-27's to the last digit, pp128 694.96 and tg128 56.37 (step 5's run 696.56 / 56.39), no TCM block held; `GGML_FLAGOS_DISABLE_OPS=FLASH_ATTN_EXT` in `llama-completion` prints its warning once. **Accepted.** Qwen3-4B not rerun (no model path changed).

### The server check, the baseline before S2 (spec, 2026-11-03)

**Status:** written 2026-11-03 on the user's request ("write the llamaserver in test code and run as baseline before s2"), after the batch-size-1 probes (`build.md` §7) showed that no check drives llama.cpp the way a single-user application does. Designed from three throwaway prototypes run on the Mac (the CPU and the simulated device, Llama-3.2-1B Q4_0 as the stand-in model); their results are below. Built on the Mac 2026-11-03 (the coder, Opus, from this spec), every check of the list below passed ("Result on the Mac"); on the K3 (the user) the first run found two of the checks wrong ("Result on the K3, first run" below), and the runs after the fix gave the baselines (the next section's "Result on the K3").

**Goal.** `llama-server` on our provider, through the single-user paths real clients use and no runner check covers: the server's default KV layout, a follow-up turn reusing its cache, conversations reloaded from the server's host-RAM prompt cache (the KV state read out of our buffer and written back), KV shifting (`--cache-reuse`), and TTFT and time per token as the server reports them; and a record of every reply, the baseline that S2's run must reproduce (S2's exit: identical logits).

**Facts the design rests on.**
- The server is llama.cpp's (`tools/server`), not ours or the deck's; nothing in our tests used it before. Its slot count defaults to auto, which it turns into 4 slots sharing one unified KV cache (`common/arg.cpp:1342`, `tools/server/server.cpp:150-155`), a layout none of our tests used. With a unified cache it saves idle slots to a host-RAM prompt cache and clears them when a new task starts (`tools/server/server-context.cpp:1474-1486`), and loads the best-matching saved prompt back when it selects the slot itself (`:1636-1700`); a request that pins `id_slot` skips the load (seen in the second prototype).
- `--cache-reuse N` keeps the common prefix, then finds a matching chunk of at least N tokens further along the cached prompt and shifts it left (`server-context.cpp:3236-3283`): the case is a cached A+B+C, then a prompt A+C. The shift is a graph of RoPE over the cached keys, run on our backend; its operations (RoPE in place on a shifted cache view, F16 and F32, and the context shift through the scheduler) are checked against the CPU by the check program (`check_spacemit.cpp:2801-2860`), so the server check needs only to show that the shift happens and runs.
- The API: `GET /health`; `POST /completion` with `prompt`, `n_predict`, `temperature`, `cache_prompt`, `n_probs`; its reply has `content`, `timings` (`cache_n`, `prompt_n`, `prompt_ms`, `predicted_n`, `predicted_ms`, `predicted_per_token_ms`, `server-task.cpp:243-255`) and `completion_probabilities[0].top_logprobs` (`token`, `logprob`; `:300-330`).
- On the Mac (`-c 4096 -t 8`, the CPU and the simulated device; prototype 3): a fresh server's greedy 32 tokens equal `llama-completion`'s (whose output ends with two newlines the server's does not have); a follow-up turn reused 37 cached tokens and evaluated 12 (49 from scratch), its first token the same as from scratch with the probabilities 0 (CPU) and 0.0031 (device) apart; two interleaved conversations each reloaded from the prompt cache (`cache_n` 37 and 38, the log's "found better prompt"), 0.0003 and 0.0001 (CPU), 0.0022 and 0.0001 (device) apart; the shift reused 44 of 45 prompt tokens (one "reusing chunk" line). On the device the 12-token texts of the reused and from-scratch turns differ, so the reuse checks compare the first token's distribution, not the text. The from-scratch comparison is wrong for the shift: even on the CPU the shifted turn's first token differs from scratch by 0.20, since the reused chunk's KV still encodes attention over the dropped part.

**The change, all in `tools/spacemit_check.py`.**
1. `llama-server` joins the build targets (`:103-105`).
2. A section `server_checks(bdir, logs, model, baseline)`, called in `main` after `cache_runs`; `--skip-server` skips it, `--server-baseline PATH` adds check 6.
3. The server: a free port (bind `127.0.0.1:0`), `llama-server -m MODEL --host 127.0.0.1 --port P -t 8 -c 4096 -lv 4 --cache-reuse 16`, the environment of the model runs (the crash reporter preloaded, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_DISABLE` dropped), stdout and stderr to `logs/server.log`; ready when `GET /health` answers `{"status": "ok"}`, waited for up to 600 s; always stopped at the end (terminate, wait 60 s, kill), on an exception too. A binary or model that is missing, or a server that does not come up, fails all five checks with the reason, so the count stays the same.
4. The requests, with the standard library only (`urllib`, `json`), each with `"temperature": 0`, a 600 s timeout and no `id_slot` (the server chooses the slot, as clients do); every reply's `content`, first-token `top_logprobs` and `timings` saved under its name in `logs/server.json`:
   - A: `"The capital of France is"`, `n_predict` 32, `cache_prompt` false. Reference: `llama-completion` with the same prompt, `-n 32 --temp 0 -no-cnv -t 8 -c 4096`, the same environment, logged as `server-reference`.
   - B: `p1 = "My favourite colour is green. I live in a small town by the sea.\nQuestion: Where do I live? Answer:"`, then `p2 = p1 + b1.content + "\nQuestion: What is my favourite colour? Answer:"` with `cache_prompt` true, then `p2` again with `cache_prompt` false; `n_predict` 12, `n_probs` 5.
   - C: `"The train leaves at seven and the bus at nine. Tickets cost four euros.\nQuestion: When does the train leave? Answer:"` (x) and `"Anna has two cats and a dog. The dog is called Max.\nQuestion: How many cats does Anna have? Answer:"` (y), in the order x1, y1, x2, y2 (each turn 2 the turn 1 prompt, its reply and `"\nQuestion: How much do tickets cost? Answer:"` or `"\nQuestion: What is the dog called? Answer:"`, `cache_prompt` true), then x2 and y2 with `cache_prompt` false; `n_predict` 12, `n_probs` 5.
   - D: `"Meeting notes.\n"` + `"Item one: the budget for next year is due on Friday, and the report must be sent to the board before then. "` + `"Item two: my favourite colour is green and my sister's favourite colour is blue; we both like the sea and long walks in the hills on Sundays.\n"` + `"Question: What is my favourite colour? Answer:"`, then the same without the second part ("Item one"), both with `cache_prompt` true, `n_predict` 12, `n_probs` 5.
   - E: `" ".join(["The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog."] * 50)` (about 500 tokens), `n_predict` 32, `cache_prompt` false.
5. The checks (`report`):
   1. "llama-server: every layer and the KV cache on the provider, 4 slots in one unified KV cache": the server came up, and its log's last such lines show every layer offloaded (`offloaded N/N`), a `FlagOS:SpacemiT` KV buffer, `n_seq_max = 4`, `kv_unified = true` and `graph splits = 2`; the detail gives those and the seconds to `/health`.
   2. "llama-server's greedy reply is llama-completion's": A's `content` equals the reference's text after the prompt, trailing newlines removed from both; otherwise the detail names the first differing character.
   3. "llama-server: a follow-up turn reusing its cache matches the turn from scratch": the reused turn's `cache_n` > 0 and the scratch turn's 0, the first token's top-1 (the entry of highest probability in `top_logprobs`) the same token in both, its probabilities at most 0.05 apart. The tolerance is an estimate (0.0031 on the simulated device); the K3's batched and one-row paths differ more, so the first K3 run's figure decides whether it holds, and the detail always gives the measured difference.
   4. "llama-server: interleaved conversations reload from the prompt cache and match from scratch": for x2 and y2 the same rule as check 3, and at least one "found better prompt" line in the server's log.
   5. "llama-server: KV shifting (--cache-reuse) runs on the provider": the second D request returns content, its `cache_n` is at least 16 above the two prompts' common prefix (the shift reused a chunk, not only the prefix), and the log has a "reusing chunk with size" line.
   6. Only with `--server-baseline PATH`: "llama-server's replies equal the baseline's": every saved reply's `content` and first-token `top_logprobs` equal the file's exactly.

   Information lines (`info`): the server's load time; E's TTFT (`prompt_n` tokens in `prompt_ms`) and time per output token (`predicted_per_token_ms`); B's reused and scratch `prompt_ms` (what the cache saves); A's text.

**Alternatives, and why they lose.** Upstream's server test suite (`tools/server/tests`, pytest): written for tiny models fetched from Hugging Face (`utils.py:55-56`), whose shapes our provider mostly does not claim, so it would test the server's logic and little of ours, and it needs network access on the board. A separate script beside the runner: S2's acceptance would not run it. Comparing texts: the device's reused and from-scratch texts already differ on the Mac. Pinning slots (`id_slot`): skips the prompt-cache reload, the path that reads and writes our buffer.

**Not changed:** the provider, the check program, the runner's other checks. The expected counts become 48 for Qwen3-0.6B with `--build` and 47 for Qwen3-4B with `--depth` (49 and 48 with `--server-baseline`).

**Checks on the Mac (the coder; the runner's other sections need the K3, so the section is driven by a small script importing the module, with `build-host` and the Llama-3.2-1B stand-in):** `python3 -m py_compile`; on the simulated device checks 1-5 pass and `server.json` is written; with `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_DISABLE=1` check 1 fails by design and checks 2-5 pass (the oracles hold on the CPU); a second device run with `--server-baseline` against the first run's `server.json` passes check 6 (the replies are deterministic), and fails it against a copy with one reply changed; `--skip-server` leaves the section out; two mutations caught: the follow-up sent with `cache_prompt` false (check 3 fails on `cache_n`), and the shift's second prompt equal to the first (check 5 fails). **On the K3 (the user):** the runner with `--build` on Qwen3-0.6B (48/48), then on Qwen3-4B with `--depth` (47/47); each model's `server.json` copied aside as the pre-S2 baseline.

**Result on the Mac (2026-11-03).** Built as specified, `tools/spacemit_check.py` only (+281 lines): `server_checks` and its helpers, the prompts as constants, `llama-server` in the build targets, `--skip-server`, `--server-baseline`, the call after `cache_runs`. Choices the spec left open, the coder's: the `llama-completion` reference runs before the server starts, so the two never use the provider at once; D's common prefix is counted with the server's `POST /tokenize` (`add_special` true, as `cache_n` counts); check 1 reads the last `graph splits` line and the KV buffers after the last context's `n_seq_max` line; after a failed request the later ones are not sent and the checks that need them fail with the error. Driven on `build-host` with the Llama-3.2-1B stand-in by a script importing the runner: on the simulated device checks 1-5 pass (17/17 layers, the KV cache in the provider's buffer, 4 slots, unified, 2 graph splits; 147 characters equal to `llama-completion`'s; the follow-up `cache_n` 37 against 49 tokens from scratch, the first token 0.0031 apart; the interleaved conversations reloaded, 0.0022 and 0.0001 apart; the shift 44 of 45 tokens, one chunk of 40 shifted from [30, 70) to [5, 45)); the CPU control fails check 1 by design and passes 2-5; against the first run's `server.json` check 6 passes (13/13 replies identical across separate runs), against a copy with one reply changed it fails; the two mutations caught (the follow-up without the cache fails check 3 on `cache_n`, D's prompts equal fail check 5); a missing binary, a missing model and a server that cannot load fail every check with the reason. The main session re-ran the device run against the coder's `server.json` (6/6, identical) and the CPU control (4/5, as designed). Not run: ThreadSanitizer (not applicable, Python), the K3.

**Result on the K3, first run (2026-11-03; the user; Qwen3-0.6B with `--build`, on `be47b1417`): 46/48.** Every check outside the server section passed with the previous build's figures (perplexity 14.4471 against the CPU's 14.3706, mean KLD 0.004722 and 0.002533, pp128 700.62 and tg128 56.22 t/s). Server checks 1, 2 and 4 passed (C-x2 0.0008 and C-y2 0.0036 apart, 4 "found better prompt" lines); 3 and 5 failed:
- Check 3: B2 reused (`cache_n` 36, 12 tokens evaluated) against scratch (48): the top token ' Green' in both, 0.6693 and 0.6077, 0.0616 apart, above the 0.05.
- Check 5: D2 `cache_n` 0 of 44 prompt tokens, the common prefix 4 tokens, no "reusing chunk" line: nothing was reused, not even the prefix.

The causes and the fix: the next section. The first run's `server.json` is not a baseline (two checks failed, and the fix changes the prompts).

### The server check, the fix after the first K3 run (spec, 2026-11-03)

**Status:** written 2026-11-03 from the K3 run above (the user: "help me troubleshoot"), with the K3's model on the Mac: Qwen3-0.6B Q4_0 from `build.md` §7's source, 382156480 bytes, the size recorded for the board's file. Paused for the user's review after writing ("document the fix in plan.md and then pause and report to me"); the board's own files then confirmed both causes (the user, read-only on the first run's `server.log` and `server.json`, the commands tested on the Mac first; in the facts below). Built on the Mac 2026-11-03 on the user's go ("build the fix from the spec"; the coder, Opus), every check of the list below passed ("Result on the Mac"); on the K3 (the user) Qwen3-0.6B 48/48 (2026-11-03) and Qwen3-4B 47/47 (2026-11-04), each model's replies saved as its baseline, and the 0.6B repeated against its baseline 2026-11-04, identical ("Result on the K3").

**Facts.**
- Check 5 failed before any shift could run, on the slot. The server gives a request the idle slot whose cached prompt shares the longest common prefix with it only if that prefix is more than `--slot-prompt-similarity` of the request's tokens (default 0.1, `common/common.h:677`; `tools/server/server-context.cpp:1604-1654`), and otherwise the least recently used slot (`:1656-1678`). D2's prefix with D1 is 4 of 44 tokens on Qwen3's tokenizer, 0.091 (the K3's `/tokenize`; on the Mac `llama-tokenize` with `models/ggml-vocab-qwen2.gguf` gives the same counts), and 5 of 45 on Llama-3.2's, which adds BOS, 0.111: the Mac's passing run logged "checking sim = 0.111 (5/45) > 0.100" and "selected slot by LCP similarity". On Qwen3, D2 went to a slot that held nothing of D1. Reproduced on the Mac with Qwen3-0.6B, on the simulated device and with the provider disabled: the K3's detail to the digit. The board's log shows the choice: "checking sim = 0.091 (4/44) > 0.100" for D1's slot 3, then "selected slot by LRU" for slot 2. The slot is chosen before any graph runs, so the prompt was at fault, not the provider.
- Check 3's 0.05 was an estimate, and a fixed bound on one token's probability is no bound for the noise of this comparison:
  - The reused turn's KV for the first reply comes from 11 one-row decoding steps, the scratch turn's from one prefill. The provider's one-row and batched paths round differently: the runner's KLD against the CPU on this model is 0.002533 for one-token ubatches and 0.004722 for 512-token batches, per token 99% points of 0.015 and 0.037, maxima of 0.083 and 0.239, and the top token differs on 2.7 and 4.1% of tokens (`build.md` §7, step 3c's table).
  - A given change of a token's logit moves its probability in proportion to p(1-p), so the most where the model is unsure.
  - On the Mac, the old B prompt on Qwen3-0.6B: on the simulated device the first reply rambles (" The town where I live.\nYou are a language model."), the follow-up's first token is flat (the reused turn's top four 0.15 to 0.26, the scratch turn's 0.12 to 0.31), and the top token itself differs between the reused turn and scratch (' [' 0.263 against ' Green' 0.309). With the provider disabled B is exact, but C-x2's top token is 0.0116 apart: the comparison is inexact on every backend, only by different amounts.
  - Our reading (an inference, not isolated): the K3's 0.0616 is that rounding on an unsure token, not a reuse fault. The board's full top 5 (the first run's `server.json`): the mass moved between two ways to begin the answer, ' Green' and ' My' (0.6077 and 0.2566 from scratch, 0.6693 and 0.1940 reused), and the measure below gives 0.012936 over all five shared tokens, below this model's per-token 99% point for one-token steps (0.015). C, built the same way, gives 0.001344 and 0.000297; every top token is in the other's top 5. The test that would isolate it: B with the first reply prefilled instead of decoded.

**The change, all in `tools/spacemit_check.py`'s server section.**
1. Checks 3 and 4 compare distributions. The measure: the first token's KL divergence of the reused turn from scratch, KL(scratch || reused), over the tokens both top-5 lists hold, plus one bucket for the rest of each distribution's mass. Merging tokens never raises a KL divergence, so this is a lower bound of the full one. The guard: each reply's top token is among the other's top 5, since a token that leaves a list moves its mass into the rest, where the bound cannot see it. The limit is 0.1.
2. The follow-up prompts ask for a name the first sentence gives, in a short Q/A form, so the first token is confident:
   - B: `"Tom has a red bicycle and a cat. The cat is called Luna.\nQ: What colour is the bicycle?\nA: Red\nQ: What does Tom have?\nA:"`, then `"\nQ: What is the cat called?\nA:"`.
   - x: `"The red car belongs to Oscar and the blue car belongs to Mia.\nQ: Who owns the red car?\nA: Oscar\nQ: What colour is Oscar's car?\nA:"`, then `"\nQ: Who owns the blue car?\nA:"`.
   - y: `"Anna has two cats and a dog. The dog is called Max.\nQ: How many cats does Anna have?\nA: Two\nQ: How many dogs does Anna have?\nA:"`, then `"\nQ: What is the dog called?\nA:"`.
3. D's common prefix becomes a header line, `"Minutes of the garden club meeting, Tuesday evening in the village hall. Present: Anna, Ben, Carla, David and Emma. Apologies: Frank.\n"`, and every part ends with a newline, so the kept part tokenizes the same after either. The common prefix is then 32 of D2's 72 tokens on Qwen's tokenizer (0.444) and 33 of 73 on Llama's (0.452); the chunk shifted is 40 tokens.
4. Check 5's detail gives the similarity and whether it is above the server's 0.1, so the failure above would have named its cause.

**Measured on the Mac before choosing** (throwaway scripts, the server started as the runner starts it, Qwen3-0.6B and Llama-3.2-1B, the simulated device and the CPU). The first token of each follow-up from scratch, and the KL of the reused turn on the device (with the provider disabled every row's KL is 0.0000, on both models):

| Follow-up | Qwen3-0.6B, device / CPU | KL, Qwen3 device | Llama-3.2-1B, device / CPU | KL, Llama device |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the old B | ' Green' 0.309 (the reused turn's top token differs) / 0.591 | 0.0369 | 0.942 / 0.937 | 0.0002 |
| luna (the new B) | ' Luna' 0.929 / 0.937 | 0.0012 | 0.990 / 0.969 | 0.0000 |
| mia (the new x) | ' Mia' 0.873 / 0.894 | 0.0016 | 0.987 / 0.986 | 0.0001 |
| max (the new y) | ' Max' 0.969 / 0.966 | 0.0002 | 0.994 / 0.994 | 0.0000 |
| hugo (not used) | ' Hugo' 0.715 / 0.776 | 0.0083 | 0.814 / 0.766 | 0.0026 |

A turn that lost its context, the same follow-up without its first sentence (scratch, the CPU), is caught in all six cases: Qwen3 luna KL 1.98 (' Luna' leaves the top 5), mia 1.10, max 0.29 (' Max' leaves); Llama luna 0.51, mia 0.014 (' Mia' leaves: only the guard catches it), max 1.56. The limit 0.1 lies above every legitimate figure measured (0.0369 at most, the old flat token on the Mac's device; the board's old B 0.0129) and every per-token 99.9% point the KLD runs have recorded against the CPU (0.033 to 0.075, `build.md` §7), and below every lost-context figure the guard does not already catch (0.51 at least).

**Alternatives, and why they lose.**
- A wider probability tolerance: its only basis would be the one K3 figure, and on an unsure token the top token itself can change (the device's old B).
- Pinning D's requests to one slot (`id_slot`): works with any tokenizer, but a client that edits its prompt keeps most of it, so a long common prefix is the realistic case, and the other checks leave the slot to the server on purpose.
- Starting the server with a lower `--slot-prompt-similarity`: it would test a server nobody runs.
- Taking the scratch turn through the same one-row steps: the server cannot decode a from-scratch turn's first reply one row at a time; it would be the reuse path again.
- More top probabilities (`n_probs` 10 to 20) for a tighter bound: the guard already covers the case they would fix.

**Not changed:** the provider, the runner's other checks, the counts (48 for Qwen3-0.6B with `--build`, 46 without; 47 for Qwen3-4B with `--depth`).

**Checks on the Mac (the coder, then the main session):** `py_compile`; on Qwen3-0.6B and on Llama-3.2-1B, checks 1-5 pass on the simulated device, and with the provider disabled check 1 fails by design and 2-5 pass; two device runs give identical replies (check 6 passes against the first); mutations caught: the old D prompts (check 5 fails, its detail giving 0.091 not above 0.1), the reused B turn asking a different question (check 3 fails on the measure, not on `cache_n`), and synthetic replies whose top token leaves the other's list with a small KL (fails on the guard); identical replies give a KL of 0. **On the K3 (the user):** the runner on Qwen3-0.6B with `--build`, then on Qwen3-4B with `--depth`, each model's `server.json` copied aside as the baseline.

**Result on the Mac (2026-11-03).** Built as specified, `tools/spacemit_check.py`'s server section only (95 lines changed): the prompts; `SERVER_KLD_LIMIT` and `SERVER_SLOT_SIMILARITY` in place of `SERVER_TOLERANCE`; `first_probs` and `first_kld` in place of `first_token`; `server_reuse`'s rule and detail; `server_requests` returning D2's length with the prefix; check 5's detail. Choices the spec left open, the coder's: a tie for the top token keeps the server's order, as before; the "Item one" string split over two lines (every joined prompt checked byte for byte against the strings above). The main session's script of synthetic replies expected 0.012963 for the board's B2, an arithmetic slip: the measure on its rounded inputs is 0.012958 (the board's 0.012936 is from unrounded values); corrected. Driven on `build-host` by a script importing the runner:
- Qwen3-0.6B, simulated device: 5/5. B2 ' Luna' 0.9399 reused against 0.9290, KLD 0.001211; C-x2 ' Mia' 0.000028 and C-y2 ' Max' 0.000064, 4 "found better prompt" lines; D2 `cache_n` 71 of 72, the common prefix 32 of 72 (similarity 0.444), one chunk of 40 shifted from [57, 97) to [32, 72).
- Qwen3-0.6B, provider disabled: check 1 fails by design, 2-5 pass (KLDs 0.000000, 0.000004 and 0.000142).
- Llama-3.2-1B: 5/5 on the device (KLDs at most 0.000027, similarity 0.452), 2-5 with the provider disabled.
- A second device run against the first's `server.json`: 6/6, 13/13 replies identical; the main session's own run against it, after a one-line readability edit: 6/6, identical.
- Mutations: the old D prompts fail check 5 with "common prefix 4 of 44 tokens (similarity 0.091, not above the server's 0.1: D2 takes another slot)", the board's failure now naming its cause; the reused B2 asking a different question fails check 3 with `cache_n` 47, on the guard (' Red' 0.8201 against ' Luna' 0.9370; the bound alone 0.002830 over one shared token, the case the guard exists for); synthetic replies: identical ones give a KLD of 0, the board's B2 0.012958 passes, a lost context with a small KLD (0.013559) is caught by the guard, `cache_n` 0 fails.

**Result on the K3 (2026-11-03; the user; Qwen3-0.6B with `--build` under the crash reporter, on `f9ac19fb1` pulled from a bundle): 48/48.** The server section:
- Check 1: up after 5.0 s; 29/29 layers; the KV buffers FlagOS:SpacemiT 448.00 MiB; 4 slots, unified; 2 graph splits.
- Check 2: 153 characters equal to `llama-completion`'s.
- Check 3: B2 reused (`cache_n` 47, 12 tokens evaluated) against scratch (59): ' Luna' 0.9461 and 0.9417, KLD 0.000266 over all five tokens.
- Check 4: C-x2 ' Mia' 0.8767 and 0.8851, KLD 0.000730; C-y2 ' Max' 0.9638 and 0.9663, KLD 0.000181 (`cache_n` 48 and 49, from scratch 60 and 61 tokens); 4 "found better prompt" lines.
- Check 5: D2 `cache_n` 71 of 72 prompt tokens, the common prefix 32 of 72 (similarity 0.444), one chunk of 40 shifted from [57, 97) to [32, 72).
- INFO: E's 500 prompt tokens in 958 ms, 23.9 ms per output token (32 tokens); B2's 12 tokens with the cache in 40 ms, its 59 from scratch in 114 ms.

Every token count above, and check 2's length, is the Mac's simulated device's; the replies are each machine's own (D2 47 characters here, 30 on the Mac's device). The largest KLD, 0.000730, is 1/137 of the limit (the first run's B2, on the old unsure prompt: 0.0129). Every check outside the server section gave the first run's figures to the last digit (perplexity 14.4471 against the CPU's 14.3706 with `-fa on` and `-fa auto`; with ops disabled 58 splits and 17.4526 against 17.3672; mean KLD 0.004722 and 0.002533), the speeds within spread (pp128 694.53 and tg128 56.31 against the first run's 700.62 and 56.22). The predictions given before the run held: 48/48, check 5's figures the Mac's, B2's KLD below 0.01, the other figures unchanged. The replies are the baseline: `~/server-baseline-0.6b.json` on the board, from the provider's code of the S1 step 5 follow-up (`60fdab717`; only the runner has changed since).

**Qwen3-4B with `--depth` (2026-11-04; the user; the same build): 47/47.** Check 1: up after 11.0 s, 37/37 layers, the KV buffers 576.00 MiB, 4 slots, unified, 2 graph splits. Check 2: 150 characters equal. Check 3: B2 (`cache_n` 47, 12 evaluated; scratch 59) ' Luna' 0.9919 and 0.9930, KLD 0.000146. Check 4: C-x2 (48, 11; scratch 59) ' Mia' 0.9999 and 0.9999, KLD 0.000000 over the 4 tokens both lists share; C-y2 (49, 12; 61) ' Max' 0.9974 and 0.9976, KLD 0.000005; 4 "found better prompt" lines. Check 5: the 0.6B's figures, D2 48 characters. INFO: E 5247 ms to the first token, 95.6 ms per output token as the server counts it (98.7 ms per step; `llama-completion` on E's prompt, run apart, 5242 ms and 99.15 ms a step, so the server adds nothing measurable: `build.md` §7). The predictions given before the run held: 47/47; 37/37 layers and 576.00 MiB (computed: 144 KiB a position × 4096); check 5 the 0.6B's figures (the same tokenizer and prompts); `cache_n` of B and C free to differ (C-x's first reply re-tokenizes one token shorter); the KLDs below 0.01; about 5.2 s to the first token and 95 to 100 ms per output token. Every other check's figures are the earlier runs' to the last digit (perplexity 9.7736 against the CPU's 9.774; mean KLD 0.002489 and 0.001229), with ops disabled 11.4679 against 11.4200 in 74 splits. The replies are the 4B's baseline: `~/server-baseline-4b.json` on the board. The speeds and the CPU-only run's crash (question 8's, its fifth dump at the same instruction): `build.md` §7.

**The repeat, Qwen3-0.6B against its baseline (2026-11-04; the user; the same build, without `--build`): 47/47.** Check 6: 13/13 replies equal to `~/server-baseline-0.6b.json`, the content and the first token's top-5 log-probabilities exactly, so the K3's replies repeat from run to run, as the Mac's do, and S2 can be held to them. Every other figure is the previous run's: the server's KLDs 0.000266, 0.000730 and 0.000181, D2 47 characters, the perplexities, the ops-disabled run and both KLDs to the last digit; E 952 ms to the first token, 23.9 ms per output token. The 0.6B's pp128 over the three runs of the same provider code: 700.62, 694.53 and 705.77, 1.6% apart (tg128 56.22, 56.31, 56.48), so S2's "speed within 1%" needs the two builds interleaved in one session (`build.md` §7, "Manual checks").

### Two measurements without code: the KV cache's types on the Mac, speculative decoding on the K3 (spec, 2026-11-04)

**Status:** written 2026-11-04 on the user's request ("help me plan the KV-cache quantization accuracy check (Mac only); speculative decoding with the 0.6B as the 4B's draft; give me commands to run them"), after the batch-size-1 discussion. Neither changes code; both use llama.cpp's own tools. The commands were tested on the Mac (below); the runs are the user's, the first on the Mac, the second on the K3. Part A measured 2026-11-04 (its result after the preview); part B's first K3 run found that a second provider context in one process gets no AI cores, and with a stream per graph it was measured the same day (both below).

**A. The KV cache's types on Qwen3: accuracy on the Mac.**
- Question: what llama.cpp's quantized caches cost Qwen3 at long context, which half carries the cost, and what the Hadamard rotation buys, before a provider kernel for a quantized cache is designed (after S2).
- What llama.cpp has (read in the code, checked on the Mac): the cache types f32, f16, bf16, q8_0, q4_0, q4_1, iq4_nl, q5_0 and q5_1 (`common/arg.cpp:304-314`). For a quantized type a Walsh-Hadamard rotation, on by default: q and k by a 128×128 matrix (the whole head on Qwen3), v and the attention output by a 64×64 one (`src/llama-kv-cache.cpp:318-334` and `:1418-1457`, `src/llama-graph.cpp:2760-2798`), off with `LLAMA_ATTN_ROT_DISABLE=1` (the Mac's log: `attn_rot_k = 1, attn_rot_v = 1` with a q8_0 K and a q4_0 V, 0 with f16). The CPU's attention over a quantized K converts q to Q8_0 and scores with integer dot products, V rows converted to float (`ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:8542-8648`): the arithmetic a provider kernel would use. The provider stores a quantized cache today, but its attention takes F16 K and V only (`flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:402`), so with a quantized cache attention and the rotations run on the X100 cores.
- The runs: `llama-perplexity` on the Mac's CPU (`build-host`, `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_DISABLE=1`, no Metal in this build; `-t 4`, the performance cores, as fast as 8 threads in the test) on wikitext-2's test text (`scripts/get-wikitext-2.sh`; the board's `~/ppl.txt` has no recorded source, and each type is compared with the f16 cache on the same text), `-c 4096 -b 4096 -fa on` (each chunk's second half is scored, 2,047 tokens, so every scored token sees about 2,048 to 4,095 cached positions), the f16 cache's logits as the base. K and V each q8_0 or q4_0, the rotation on and off: 8 runs per model. Qwen3-0.6B over 4 chunks (8,188 scored tokens), Qwen3-4B over 2 (4,094), both from `build.md` §7's source.
- Measured per run: mean KLD and its error, the 99% point, the maximum, the share of tokens whose top token is unchanged, PPL(Q)/PPL(base).
- Decision rule, set before the runs: a configuration is *free* if its mean KLD is at most a fifth of the provider's own against the CPU on that model (0.6B 0.0047, 4B 0.0025, at 512-token batches: so 0.0009 and 0.0005), *usable* up to that figure, *costly* above it. A provider kernel for a quantized cache (after S2) targets the smallest configuration usable on both models; a quantization method of our own is studied only if 8 bits are usable and the best 4-bit configuration is costly.
- Predictions: q8_0 usable on both models, the 4B's KLD lower; the 4-bit K the costly half (K errors are scaled by the query's norm and exponentiated by the softmax, V errors averaged over the attended positions); the rotation helping most at 4 bits.
- Time: the 0.6B about 30 minutes (one chunk took 16 to 17 s with the f16 cache and 52 to 57 s quantized: the CPU's attention over a quantized cache is the slow part); the 4B not measured on this Mac, estimated at 40 to 90 minutes.
- Tests on the Mac: the script on one chunk of Qwen3-0.6B (2,047 scored tokens; a preview, not the measurement; three of the runs, repeated, gave the same figures to the last digit):

| K | V | rotation | PPL(Q)/PPL(base) | mean KLD | 99% KLD | same top token |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| q8_0 | q8_0 | on | 1.0053 | 0.004196 ± 0.000188 | 0.0237 | 96.0% |
| q8_0 | q8_0 | off | 1.0058 | 0.016299 ± 0.000823 | 0.1387 | 92.9% |
| q8_0 | q4_0 | on | 1.0114 | 0.013258 ± 0.001263 | 0.0868 | 92.9% |
| q8_0 | q4_0 | off | 1.0039 | 0.026200 ± 0.001102 | 0.2229 | 90.0% |
| q4_0 | q8_0 | on | 4.57 | 1.82 | 8.81 | 43.2% |
| q4_0 | q8_0 | off | 10.48 | 2.71 | 9.75 | 28.7% |
| q4_0 | q4_0 | on | 3.81 | 1.67 | 8.23 | 44.3% |
| q4_0 | q4_0 | off | 11.65 | 2.80 | 9.77 | 27.2% |

  Read from the preview: a 4-bit K breaks the 0.6B whatever V is (the rotation more than halves the perplexity's rise, yet fewer than half the top tokens survive); with an 8-bit K, the 4-bit V costs a mean KLD of 0.013; the rotation cuts the 8-bit cache's KLD four times (0.0163 to 0.0042), to about the provider's own against the CPU. By the rule: q8_0 for K and V usable, every 4-bit configuration costly, on this model. The perplexity ratio can fall while the KLD rises (q8_0 / q4_0, the rotation off), so the KLD decides.
- Result on the Mac (2026-11-04; the user; 1 h 14 min, the 0.6B 29 min and the 4B 45 min, as estimated): the text `wiki.test.raw` as `scripts/get-wikitext-2.sh` downloads it (sha256 `173c87a53759e0201f33e0ccf978e510c2042d7f2cb78229d9a50d79b9e7dd08`; the file the tests downloaded, since the user's terminal could not reach huggingface.co); the f16 cache's perplexity 17.7714 on the 0.6B (its first chunk 23.2940, the preview's to the last digit) and 14.1797 on the 4B. Qwen3-0.6B over 4 chunks, 8,188 scored tokens (free at most 0.0009, usable at most 0.0047):

| K | V | rotation | PPL(Q)/PPL(base) | mean KLD | 99% KLD | max KLD | same top token | by the rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| q8_0 | q8_0 | on | 1.0037 | 0.004167 ± 0.000087 | 0.0286 | 0.21 | 96.5% | usable |
| q8_0 | q8_0 | off | 1.0135 | 0.017639 ± 0.000565 | 0.1594 | 1.59 | 93.3% | costly |
| q8_0 | q4_0 | on | 1.0053 | 0.012027 ± 0.000380 | 0.0913 | 2.35 | 94.3% | costly |
| q8_0 | q4_0 | off | 1.0197 | 0.027570 ± 0.000717 | 0.2225 | 1.67 | 91.1% | costly |
| q4_0 | q8_0 | on | 5.20 | 1.843539 ± 0.022079 | 9.04 | 20.63 | 45.5% | costly, broken |
| q4_0 | q8_0 | off | 13.93 | 2.872782 ± 0.022946 | 9.17 | 18.32 | 27.9% | costly, broken |
| q4_0 | q4_0 | on | 4.77 | 1.777691 ± 0.021525 | 8.96 | 17.64 | 46.2% | costly, broken |
| q4_0 | q4_0 | off | 14.51 | 2.915012 ± 0.022771 | 9.29 | 19.10 | 27.0% | costly, broken |

  Qwen3-4B over 2 chunks, 4,094 scored tokens (free at most 0.0005, usable at most 0.0025):

| K | V | rotation | PPL(Q)/PPL(base) | mean KLD | 99% KLD | max KLD | same top token | by the rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| q8_0 | q8_0 | on | 1.0090 | 0.001310 ± 0.000050 | 0.0119 | 0.14 | 98.4% | usable |
| q8_0 | q8_0 | off | 1.0118 | 0.002683 ± 0.000515 | 0.0180 | 2.05 | 98.0% | costly, within one standard error of the limit |
| q8_0 | q4_0 | on | 1.0048 | 0.004502 ± 0.000195 | 0.0491 | 0.42 | 97.1% | costly |
| q8_0 | q4_0 | off | 1.0156 | 0.006517 ± 0.000445 | 0.0702 | 1.50 | 96.8% | costly |
| q4_0 | q8_0 | on | 1.0263 | 0.031703 ± 0.001898 | 0.3552 | 3.97 | 93.2% | costly |
| q4_0 | q8_0 | off | 1.0622 | 0.084524 ± 0.003401 | 0.9823 | 4.36 | 88.3% | costly |
| q4_0 | q4_0 | on | 1.0277 | 0.032388 ± 0.001868 | 0.3082 | 3.84 | 92.7% | costly |
| q4_0 | q4_0 | off | 1.0780 | 0.095497 ± 0.003900 | 1.1349 | 5.76 | 87.8% | costly |

  Read:
  - By the rule: none free; q8_0 for K and V with the rotation the only configuration usable, on both models (0.0042 against 0.0047, 0.0013 against 0.0025), so it is what a provider kernel for a quantized cache targets after S2, the rotation included: without it the 8-bit cache is costly on the 0.6B (0.0176) and at the limit on the 4B (0.0027 ± 0.0005). 8 bits are usable and the best 4-bit configuration is costly on both (q8_0 K with q4_0 V and the rotation: 2.6 and 1.8 times the limit), so by the rule a quantization method of our own is worth studying; llama.cpp's other types (q4_1, iq4_nl, q5_0, q5_1, `common/arg.cpp:304-314`) are not measured, and the same script against the same base files would measure them first.
  - The predictions. q8_0 usable on both, the 4B lower: held with the rotation (the default), 3.2 times lower on the 4B. The 4-bit K the costly half: held; with the rotation a 4-bit K multiplies the 8-bit pair's mean KLD by 442 on the 0.6B and by 24 on the 4B, a 4-bit V by 2.9 and 3.4; a 4-bit K breaks the 0.6B (perplexity 4.8 to 14.5 times, under half the top tokens kept) but not the 4B (+2.6%, 93% kept). The rotation helping most at 4 bits: missed; off against on it divides the mean KLD by 4.2 at 8 bits on the 0.6B and by 1.6 with its 4-bit K, by 2.7 to 3.0 with the 4B's 4-bit K and by 2.05 at its 8 bits, and least with a 4-bit V on both (2.3 and 1.45). The time as estimated.
  - The tail: without the rotation the 4B's 8-bit error sits in a few tokens (the 99% point 0.018, the maximum 2.05, the error bar ten times the rotated run's); with it the maximum is 0.14. Likely channels with outliers that one block's scale cannot hold (an inference, not measured).
  - The perplexity ratio is noisier than the KLD (± 0.0017 for the 0.6B's 8-bit pair, ± 0.0033 for the 4B's): on the 4B the 4-bit V shows 1.0048 against the 8-bit pair's 1.0090 with 3.4 times its KLD, so the KLD decides, as the rule has it.

**B. Speculative decoding on the K3: Qwen3-0.6B drafting for Qwen3-4B.**
- Questions: whether two models run together on the provider (both fully offloaded, no failure, no TCM block held after); the share of the 0.6B's greedy drafts the 4B accepts on different kinds of text; the speed against plain decoding at draft lengths 2, 4 and 8; whether the text equals plain greedy decoding's.
- The tool (read in the code, tried on the Mac): `llama-speculative` (`examples/speculative`; not among the runner's targets, so one `cmake --build` on the board). In this tree a draft model needs `--spec-type draft-simple` (the default type is `none`, `common/speculative.cpp:31-43`) and the draft length is `--spec-draft-n-max` (`--draft-max` was removed). Each round the draft model makes 1 + n one-row steps and the target checks n + 1 rows in one graph (`examples/speculative/speculative.cpp:452-520`). Not used: `llama-speculative-simple`, which aborts here (`GGML_ASSERT(n_outputs_max <= cparams.n_outputs_max)`, `src/llama-context.cpp:2189`: it creates its draft context for one output, `examples/speculative-simple/speculative-simple.cpp:64-65`, then decodes the whole checked batch with it, `:242`; upstream's code, not changed), and llama-server, whose per-request speculative fields are compiled out (`tools/server/server-schema.cpp:196-218`), so each setting would need a server of its own. The provider's plan cache is per backend instance (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:60-70`), so the two models do not evict each other's plan.
- The runs: four prompts in files: `p1` prose ("The history of the printing press is a story of"), `p2` code (a Python function's signature and docstring), `p3` a question ("Question: Why do leaves change colour in autumn?" and "Answer:"), `p4` the server check's E (the fox sentence 50 times: a draft's best case, and the prompt of the `llama-completion` split). Each decoded greedily for 128 tokens by `llama-completion` (the baseline: its decoding time, sampling apart) and by `llama-speculative` at draft lengths 2, 4 and 8 (its decoding speed, drafting, checking and sampling included), `-t 8 -c 4096 --temp 0`, one after another in one session; first a short run with `-lv 4` showing where both models' layers went, last the TCM blocks.
- Predictions (estimates from the bottleneck suite's batch curve, `build.md` §7: 2, 4 and 8 rows cost 1.21, 1.27 and 1.51 times one row; the 0.6B's step about a fifth of the 4B's): with 80% of drafts accepted about 1.3, 1.45 and 1.25 times the baseline at lengths 2, 4 and 8; with 60%, no gain at any length; on `p4` (near 100%) about 1.5, 1.9 and 2.2 times. Both models on the provider (37/37 and 29/29 layers, 576 and 448 MiB of cache); the text equal to the baseline's up to a near-tie, where the batched check rounds differently.
- Decision: a gain of 1.3 times or more on the natural prompts makes speculative decoding a single-user option after S2 (the server's `-md` with `--spec-type draft-simple`), with no provider change; less, and it is recorded and set aside.
- Tests on the Mac (Qwen3-0.6B as both models, so the mechanics only, not the speed): the loop's 16 runs complete; with the model drafting for itself every draft is accepted and every text equals the baseline's; on the simulated device two models in one process: 29/29 layers twice, two 448 MiB caches in the provider's buffer, 2 graph splits each, exit code 0.
- First run on the K3 (2026-11-04; the user; the build of `f9ac19fb1` with `llama-speculative` added): the placement as predicted (37/37 and 29/29 layers on the provider, the caches 576.00 and 448.00 MiB in its buffer, 2 graph splits each), then an abort (exit code 134) before any token. The draft's context got no AI cores: `FlagOS SpacemiT: could not get 8 AI cores from spine-runtime`, three times 30 s apart (its warm-up, `common/common.cpp:1428`; its prompt, `examples/speculative/speculative.cpp:195`; its first draft, `:473`), each decode failing with -3; `llama-speculative` ignores `llama_decode`'s result, so it then sampled from the draft's missing logits (`:514`): `GGML_ASSERT(logits != nullptr)` at `common/sampling.cpp:154`. No TCM block held after. The cause: every backend context keeps a persistent spine-runtime stream holding all 8 AI cores until it is freed (`flagos-spacemit.cpp:529`; the plugin's executor, `flagos-spacemit-exec.cpp:233-258`; "Stream lifetime" above), and the target's warm-up took them. The first run with two provider contexts alive at once on the K3: llama-bench frees each context before the next, llama-server has one. The Mac's host executor has no cores to hold: there Qwen3-4B drafted by Qwen3-0.6B runs on the simulated device (21 tokens, 58% of drafts accepted, exit code 0). So any process with two provider contexts (speculative decoding, the server's `-md`, two models in one application) needs the cores shared between contexts: a constraint for S2's stream design, where the device already serializes its contexts' runs (`run_mutex`, `flagos-spacemit.cpp:50, 395`). The 30 s per failure looks like spine-runtime waiting for the cores before it gives up (an inference from the timestamps). Next: the same runs with a stream per graph (`FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_STREAM=per-call`, M2a's switch, 24.3 µs a launch against 9.9 then), every run of the comparison under it.
- With a stream per graph (2026-11-04; the user; `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_STREAM=per-call` for every run, the baselines included): the placement run completes (18 tokens, 36% of drafts accepted, no "could not get", no TCM block held), so the two contexts taking turns is enough; the loop's 16 runs complete, no TCM block held after. The baselines are the persistent stream's (p1 to p3 11.04 to 11.09 t/s, tg128 11.12 in the runner the same day; p4 10.09, the split's figure), so a stream per graph costs nothing measurable at one graph per token.

| prompt | run | t/s | × baseline | drafts accepted | text against the baseline |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| p1, prose | baseline | 11.04 | | | |
| | draft 2 | 11.19 | 1.01 | 68/122 (56%) | differs from character 401 of 736 |
| | draft 4 | 11.77 | 1.07 | 80/200 (40%) | differs from character 401 |
| | draft 8 | 8.60 | 0.78 | 88/368 (24%) | differs from character 332 |
| p2, code | baseline | 11.06 | | | |
| | draft 2 | 14.36 | 1.30 | 82/94 (87%) | equal |
| | draft 4 | 17.75 | 1.61 | 96/128 (75%) | equal |
| | draft 8 | 16.91 | 1.53 | 107/176 (61%) | equal |
| p3, a question | baseline | 11.09 | | | |
| | draft 2 | 11.65 | 1.05 | 70/116 (60%) | differs from character 190 of 727 |
| | draft 4 | 10.81 | 0.98 | 77/220 (35%) | differs from character 469 |
| | draft 8 | 7.52 | 0.68 | 78/400 (20%) | equal |
| p4, the fox sentence 50 times | baseline | 10.09 | | | |
| | draft 2 | 9.89 | 0.98 | 86/86 (100%) | equal |
| | draft 4 | 12.17 | 1.21 | 104/104 (100%) | equal |
| | draft 8 | 13.14 | 1.30 | 120/120 (100%) | equal |

  Read:
  - By the rule: 1.3 times or more on code only (1.30, 1.61 and 1.53), at most 1.07 on prose and 1.05 on the question, so not a single-user option in general: recorded and set aside, with the constraint the first run found (two contexts need the AI cores shared) for S2. Draft length 4 is the best on code and prose; 8 loses on prose and the question (0.78, 0.68).
  - The predictions. Both models on the provider: held, once the contexts take turns. With 60% accepted no gain: held (p1 and p3 at 56% and 60% with 2 drafted: 1.01 and 1.05). With 80% accepted 1.3, 1.45 and 1.25 times: p2 at 87%, 75% and 61% gave 1.30, 1.61 and 1.53, at or above. Near 100% 1.5, 1.9 and 2.2 times: missed, p4 gave 0.98, 1.21 and 1.30. The text equal up to a near-tie: held (p2 and p4 equal; p1 and p3 apart from characters 190 to 469 on, where the batched check rounds differently from one row).
  - Where a round goes (from the speeds and counts: the drafted tokens over the draft length give the rounds, each round's tokens are its accepted drafts plus one): on p1 to p3, whose cache stays shallow, a round takes 190, 222 and 340 ms at draft lengths 2, 4 and 8 whatever the text, about 20 to 45 ms more than the draft's steps (17.8 ms each) and the batched check (1.2 to 1.5 times a 89 ms step) give; on p4, from 500 cached positions, 303, 411 and 685 ms, 60 to 100% longer, where the one-row figures at that depth (the 0.6B's step 24.7 ms, the 4B's 99 ms) explain much less (estimates). So the cost model under the predictions missed a cost per round that grows with the draft length and most of all with the cache's depth. Not yet placed: the loop's logs hold the target's timings only (`common_perf_print`), the draft's (`llama_perf_context_print`, `examples/speculative/speculative.cpp:650`) are dropped at the default verbosity.
- Where a round goes, measured (2026-11-04; the user; code at draft length 4 and p4 at 8 again, with `-lv 4` and `GGML_FLAGOS_OP_STATS`, the stream per graph; the speeds 17.54 and 13.16 t/s, the loop's): a round is its graphs, within 2 to 4%. Code: 229.8 ms a round, the draft's 5 one-row graphs at 18.2 ms each (n_kv 256) and the target's 5-row check at 130.5 ms (its attention 14.1 ms), 8.5 ms left (the target's sampling 1.9 ms of it). p4: 688.7 ms a round, the draft's 9 graphs at 23.5 to 25.5 ms (n_kv 512 and 768) and the 9-row check at 446.6 ms, of it attention 293.6 ms (66%), 13.5 ms left. So the cost model missed the check, above all its attention at depth (predicted about 250 and 115 ms; the draft's graphs as predicted). The cause, read in the code: for 2 to 63 tokens per call the attention takes the one-chunk kernels (the tiled kernel from 64 rows, `flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:451`; the split for one token only, `:209`). They take the rows token by token within each query head (`flagos-spacemit-rvv-attn.cpp:1180-1181`) and read the head's K and V once per row; their 4-row and 2-row kernels, which share one read, need rows of the same token (`:1206-1212`), so they group one token's query heads and nothing once a call has two tokens or more. A 9-token check at 768 positions thus reads 9 × 32 rows × 384 KiB = 108 MiB per layer, 3.9 GiB per graph: in 293.6 ms, 14.2 GB/s, these kernels' bandwidth in M2e.3 (an inference from the code and the arithmetic). At 256 positions a head's 128 KiB apparently stays in the core's cache between its rows (the 5-row check's 14.1 ms). Not only speculative decoding pays it: every batch of 2 to 63 tokens at depth does (a chat turn after a long conversation; likely the unified cache's parallel decoding too, where the bottleneck suite saw a step cost more than its rows). Estimates: a 12-token turn at 4096 cached positions about 2 s of attention (27 GiB read), against about 0.05 s if each head's cache were read once per call for all the rows that use it (its query heads, every token); the 9-token check from 446.6 to about 170 ms, p4 at draft length 8 from 1.30 to about 2.2 times, the prediction. The test that would show it: llama-bench pp12, pp63 and pp64 at depth 4096 (predicted about 5.5, 5.5 and 30 to 40 t/s: the tiled kernel from 64 rows).
- The confirming benchmark (2026-11-04; the user; `llama-bench -p 12,63,64 -n 0 -d 4096 -fa 1 -mmp 0 -r 1`, one context, the default stream): pp12 3.67, pp63 3.66 and pp64 38.42 t/s at depth 4096 (predicted about 5.5, 5.5 and 30 to 40): 10.5 times faster for one token more, where the tiled kernel takes over. Below 64 tokens each token costs the same, 0.27 s, of it attention about 0.26 s (32 heads × 36 layers × 2.1 MiB of K and V, 2.6 GB a token, at 9.7 GB/s: the one-chunk kernels' bandwidth at n_kv 4096 in M2e.3, where the predictions had assumed 768 positions' 14.2), so a 12-token turn at 4096 cached positions takes 3.3 s to its first output token. The tiled kernel already reads the cache once per query head per call: a tile is up to 64 tokens of one head (`flagos-spacemit-rvv-attn.cpp:1412-1416`), each K and V block is transposed or copied once for the tile's rows, every loop runs over the live rows only, each row with its own mask row (`:1494-1610`). So lowering its threshold (64 rows, `flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:30`, as in ggml-cpu and the IME path) would give 2 to 63 tokens one read per query head instead of one per token and head (untested). Estimates: the 9-token check's attention at 768 positions from 293.6 to about 40 to 80 ms (the reads 9 times fewer; the tiled kernel's compute about 130 to 150 GFLOP/s), p4 at draft length 8 about 2 times; the 12-token turn at 4096 positions from 3.3 to about 0.4 to 0.6 s; at 256 positions about as today, where the one-chunk kernels' rows hit the core's cache. A further 4 times would take one read per KV head for its 4 query heads (a tile across heads). The check cannot look at less of the cache: with a shorter window its distribution is no longer the target's, and the output no longer plain decoding's.
- Upstream, read 2026-11-04 (the user's question whether upstream decides the same). ggml-cpu, at llama.cpp master (`4b29d02`, 2026-11-04) and in this tree, takes the split over positions for one token from 512 positions (`ggml/src/ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:9115` here), the tiled kernel from 64 tokens (`:9172-9176`) and below that the one-chunk kernel, a row at a time (`:9194`); llama.cpp master's SpacemiT CPU path has the same thresholds without the split (`ggml/src/ggml-cpu/spacemit/ime.cpp:1185-1222` here), its one-chunk kernels grouping rows of one token only (`rvv_kernels.cpp:1202-1205`), as ours. SpacemiT's own fork decides the same on 9 of its 11 branches. Its two newest, `mtmd-backend` @ `855a53b` and `kernel-agent/gdn-opt` @ `3e1fe3b`, add "split-KV decode (flash-decoding) for 1..FA_SPLIT_KV_MAX_Q query tokens (single-token decode and speculative-decoding verification)", `FA_SPLIT_KV_MAX_Q = 4` (the fork's `ggml/src/ggml-spacemit/rvv_kernels.cpp:40, 1375-1404`, dispatched at `ime.cpp:1410-1421`): a work unit is one KV head (or a chunk of its positions) computing all its query heads, and all the tokens in one pass when query heads × tokens is 2, 4, 8 or 16 (`rvv_kernels.cpp:1435-1439`), so on Qwen3-4B each K/V row is read once per call for 1, 2 or 4 tokens and once per token for 3. From 5 to 63 tokens it keeps the one-chunk kernels; no branch lowers the tiled kernel's 64. So the decision measured here is upstream's; SpacemiT met the same cost in verification and solved it up to 4 tokens, with the read pattern a tile across heads would give (once per KV head, 8 reads per layer on the 4B, against the tiled kernel's once per query head, 32). For the threshold experiment that is a second candidate for 2 to 4 tokens (a port, as their tiled kernel was), beside the tiled kernel from fewer than 64 tokens.

Not covered by either: sampling above temperature 0, the n-gram drafters (`--spec-type ngram-*`, no draft model), the server's own speculative path.

### The KV-head split for a few tokens, from SpacemiT's fork (spec, 2026-11-04)

**Status:** spec written 2026-11-04 on the user's request ("incorporate spacemit's new fork to add the split by kv head option, make it compatible with our setting, and test its performance against our current kernel"); approved by the user the same day ("hand it to the coder as written"); implemented on the Mac 2026-11-04 by the coder from the spec, checked in the main session (result at the end of this section); the K3 runs pending.

**Why.** For 2 to 63 tokens per call the provider's attention takes the one-chunk kernels, which read a KV head's whole cache once per token and query head ("Two measurements without code", part B): on Qwen3-4B at 4096 cached positions each token costs 0.27 s (pp12 and pp63 3.67 and 3.66 t/s, pp64 38.42), and a speculative check of 9 tokens at 768 positions spends 293.6 of its 446.6 ms in attention. SpacemiT's fork (`spacemit-com/llama.cpp`, `mtmd-backend` @ `855a53b` and `kernel-agent/gdn-opt` @ `3e1fe3b`; the local copy `~/spacemit-fetch/llama.cpp-spacemit-mtmd-backend-855a53b.tar.gz` is the former's head) solved it for 1 to 4 tokens, for decode and speculative verification.

**What the fork has** (`ggml/src/ggml-spacemit/` at `855a53b`):
- The eligibility and the chunk count, `flash_attn_ext_f16_split_kv_chunks_vlen1024_vf16` (`rvv_kernels.cpp:1380-1404`): 1 to `FA_SPLIT_KV_MAX_Q = 4` tokens (`:40`), one stream, as many K as V heads, query heads per KV head 2, 4 or 8, no ALiBi, the mask one row per token for every head; `n_chunks = max(1, threads / KV heads)`; none when `n_kv < 128 × n_chunks` ("the per-row path is already cheap").
- The partial pass, `forward_flash_attn_ext_f16_split_kv_partial_vlen1024_vf16` (`:1404-1477`): a unit is (KV head, chunk of positions), dealt round-robin over the threads; it computes the KV head's query heads with the multi-row kernel, all its tokens in one pass when query heads × tokens is 2, 4, 8 or 16 (one K/V pack: on Qwen3-4B 1, 2 or 4 tokens), else one pass per token; each row writes `{M, S, VKQ[DV]}`, laid out [token][query head][chunk].
- The merge, `forward_flash_attn_ext_f16_split_kv_reduce_vlen1024_vf16` (`:1479-1543`): per row (token × query head, round-robin) the chunks' maximum, the weighted sums in F32, the sinks, the dst row.
- The multi-row kernel `flash_attn_ext_f16_one_chunk_inner_vlen1024_vf16_mrow` (`:732-1009`), changed since our copy (`4e782bc`): QLEN 2, 4, 8 or 16, computed in groups of 4 rows from one K/V tile packed in TCM; rows from several tokens, each token's mask row at `mp + m * mask_row_stride` (a position packed if any token sees it); an optional partial output; head sizes to 256 in segments; without enough TCM, a row path that reads the positions directly. Its helpers `rvv_fill_f16`, `rvv_scale_f16`, `rvv_scale_mad_f16`, `rvv_dot_f16_f32` (`:56-100`). V summed in F16 within a unit, widened to F32 in the partial.
- The dispatch (`ime.cpp:1410-1421`): before the tiled or one-chunk choice, when the call has fewer than 64 tokens and TCM; the partials in the workspace (sized at `:994-996`), a grid barrier between the passes.

**The change** (all inside `providers/spacemit/`, so no `upstream-changes.md` entry; comments ASCII; the fork's code cited by commit and line, as the earlier ports):
1. The switch, test-only until the runs decide: `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN_KVH=<lo>-<hi>`, the token counts per call the KV-head split takes, 1 ≤ lo ≤ hi ≤ 63. Unset, empty or `off`: none, so every path is today's bit for bit. Parsed with `spacemit_cached_switch` (the check program toggles it), read at registration in `spacemit_flash_attn_read_switch` (S1 step 5 follow-up's rule); any other value warned once and taken as off.
2. The shape, `kvh_shape` (`flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp`): `spacemit_flash_attn_supported(op)` and q's tokens within the switch's range; one stream (q, K and V `ne[3] == 1`); `k->ne[2] == v->ne[2]`; query heads per KV head `rk2` in {2, 4, 8}; no ALiBi (`op_params[1] == 0`); the mask absent or with `ne[2] == ne[3] == 1`; `n_kv ≥ 128 × n_chunks`. Softcap and sinks allowed (the kernel and the merge handle them). The units: `n_chunks = max(1, 8 / n_kvh)`, 8 being the K3's AI cores as a constant, so that the support check, the workspace and both steps agree whatever the tile count; tile `ith` takes units `ith, ith + nth, ...` (the fork's loop; another tile count stays correct, only less balanced).
3. The contract (`flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp:171-186`): `{ GGML_OP_FLASH_ATTN_EXT, spacemit_always, { "flash_attn.kvh.partial", "flash_attn.kvh.merge" }, 2 }`, first of the attention contracts; both names appended to `spacemit_required_kernels` (16 kernels). With the switch off the two supports are false and the contracts after it decide as today.
4. The plugin's table (`plugin/flagos-spacemit-plugin.cpp:330-348`): `flash_attn.kvh.partial` → `spacemit_kernel_flash_attn_kvh_partial`, `flash_attn.kvh.merge` → `spacemit_kernel_flash_attn_kvh_merge`, both with `spacemit_flash_attn_kvh_supported` and `spacemit_flash_attn_kvh_workspace` (declared in `flagos-spacemit-attn.h`).
5. The workspace: tokens × query heads × `n_chunks` × (DV + 2) floats, the fork's layout.
6. Step 1, the partial: with the RVV kernels (`GGML_FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_RVV`, VLEN 1024, `supported_shape`, not `spacemit_use_reference()`), `spacemit_rvv_attn::kvh_partial(dst, n_chunks, ith, nth, partials, tcm, tcm_size)`: the fork's partial pass, and its multi-row kernel copied as a new template (`kvh_inner_mrow<QLEN>`, the partial output only: the dst branch and `flash_attn_ext_finalize_row_f16` left out) with the four helpers, in a new section of `flagos-spacemit-rvv-attn.cpp`. Our one-chunk, tiled and split kernels stay untouched. Without the RVV kernels (the Mac; `GGML_FLAGOS_KERNEL_VARIANTS=0`), a reference with the same units and layout: per unit, token and query head, ggml-cpu's one-row algorithm over the chunk's positions, scores and V in F32 (as `split_ref`).
7. Step 2, the merge, in portable C++ for both builds: the fork's reduce with scalar loops (rows dealt round-robin over the tiles; dst row `t × ne[1] + h`).
8. The op counters' labels (`core/flagos-spacemit-diagnostics.cpp:168-170`): `kvh-partial` and `kvh-merge`.
9. Docs: the switch's row in `build.md` §7; the results here.

**Edge cases**, each in the tests below: a token whose mask hides a whole chunk (its partial `M = -inf`, skipped by the merge; a row hidden everywhere written as zeros, as ggml-cpu); 3 tokens on Qwen3-4B (12 rows, not fused: 3 passes of 4 rows); GQA 2 (Qwen3-0.6B: up to 8 tokens fused) and GQA 8; n_kv not a multiple of the chunk or of the kernel's position tile; sinks; softcap; no mask; too little TCM for the pack (the kernel's row path); a tile count other than 8.

**Tests on the Mac** (the coder runs them, I rerun them; the Mac runs the reference, so the RVV code is first exercised on the K3):
1. `build-host` builds, no warnings in the new code.
2. Contract selection (the check program's block at `tests/check_spacemit.cpp:400-438`): the switch unset as today; `2-4`: 2, 3 and 4 tokens take the KV-head contract, 5 and 64 tokens `flash_attn`, 1 token the paired split; `1-4`: 1 token the KV-head contract; `2-8`: 8 tokens it; GQA 1 and ALiBi never.
3. Correctness, a new block after the split cases (`:1809-1860`): Qwen3-4B's heads {32, 8, 128} at 2, 3, 4 and 8 tokens and n_kv 256, 512, 1000 and 4096 with causal masks; Qwen3-0.6B's {16, 8, 128} at 4 and 8 tokens, n_kv 512; GQA 8 {32, 4, 128} at 4 tokens, n_kv 1024; head size 64; no mask; sinks; softcap; one token at n_kv 1024 under `1-8`. Each against the CPU within the attention bounds (NMSE 5e-4, worst row 5e-3); the outputs differ from the default's (the path ran) and repeat exactly in a second run.
4. test-backend-ops `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` on the simulated device with `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN_KVH=1-8`.
5. `llama-perplexity` on Qwen3-0.6B, simulated device, `-c 1024 -b 1024 -ub 4 --chunks 2`: the KLD against the CPU with the switch off and `2-4`, both within the runner's bounds.
6. Two mutations caught: the merge skipping the last chunk; a pass taking the wrong token's mask row.

**On the K3** (the user's runs):
1. The build; the check program (`--expect-device`): today's cases unchanged with the switch off, the new cases with the RVV kernel under `1-8`; test-backend-ops `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` under `1-8`.
2. Accuracy: Qwen3-4B, `llama-perplexity -c 4096 -b 4096 -ub 4 --chunks 1 -fa on`, the switch off and `2-4`, the KLD against one CPU base made by `PROJECT.md` §6's rule: `-c 4096 -b 4096 --chunks 1 -fa on -t 1` with the provider disabled, twice, the two files byte-identical (corrected 2026-11-04: this said a base of the same flags, and my first block made it with 8 threads; "The CPU base" below).
3. Speed, the settings taking turns twice, the faster of each:
   - the check program's attention benchmark extended to 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 tokens at n_kv 256, 512, 1024, 4096 and 8192 (Qwen3-4B's heads): µs per call, off and `1-8`;
   - llama-bench (Qwen3-4B, `-t 8 -fa 1 -mmp 0 -r 3`): pp2, pp3, pp4 and pp8 at depths 0, 1024 and 4096 (`-n 0`), off and `2-8`; tg32 at the same depths, off and `1-8` (one token: the fork's path against the paired split); pp64 and pp512 at depth 4096 as controls;
   - speculative decoding (the stream per graph): p2 and p4 at draft lengths 2 and 3 (checks of 3 and 4 tokens), off and `2-4`.

**Decision rule, set before the runs:**
- 2 to 4 tokens: the KV-head split becomes the default if every check passes, the KLD at `-ub 4` is within one standard error of the default's, and every 2 to 4-token shape from n_kv 512 is at least 20% faster in the benchmark and in llama-bench, with no shape at n_kv 256 and no control more than 2% slower; if n_kv 256 is slower, the path starts at 512, as the one-token split does.
- 5 to 8 tokens (one pass per token, a quarter of today's reads): the same 20% bar.
- One token: it replaces the paired split only if at least 2% faster at depths 1024 and 4096 and not slower at depth 0 (M2e.3 round 2's margin); otherwise the paired split stays.

**Predictions** (estimates, from M2e.3's attention bandwidth and the 2026-11-04 benchmark):
- Per call at n_kv 4096 on Qwen3-4B's heads: 4 tokens from about 28 ms (128 rows reading 2 MiB each at 9.7 GB/s) to about 3 to 5 ms (each KV head read once, 16 MiB, plus 16 rows of arithmetic per KV head); 2 tokens from about 14 to 2 to 3 ms; 3 tokens (3 passes) from about 21 to 5 to 7 ms; 8 tokens (8 passes) from about 55 to about 18 ms; one token about the paired split's 1.11 ms (the same reads in total), 1.0 to 1.3 times it.
- llama-bench: pp4 at depth 4096 from about 3.5 to about 15 t/s; at depth 0 about as today.
- Speculative: p4 at draft length 3 from about 1.1 to about 1.6 times its baseline, at draft length 2 from 0.98 (measured) to about 1.3; p2, whose cache stays shallow, about as today.
- The KLD about today's (V summed in F16 within a unit, as the one-chunk kernels).

**Time:** the coder from this spec, about one session; my check on the Mac; on the K3 one build and about an hour of runs.

**Not covered:** 9 to 63 tokens (the tiled kernel's threshold, the other candidate, part B above); several streams; ALiBi; the fork's head sizes 129 to 256 (our supports stop at 128).

**Result on the Mac (2026-11-04; the coder from this spec; read and rerun in the main session).** Built as specified: in `flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp` the switch's parser (`parse_kvh_setting`, kept as lo × 64 + hi), `kvh_shape`, `kvh_units = 8` with `kvh_n_chunks`, the reference partial `kvh_partial_ref`, the two step kernels, `spacemit_flash_attn_kvh_supported` and `_workspace`, and the switch read at registration; in `flagos-spacemit-rvv-attn.cpp` the fork's four helpers, `kvh_inner_mrow<QLEN>` (the fork's kernel with its partial output only) and `kvh_partial` (the fork's pass, the tile's ith and nth); the contract first among the attention contracts and 16 required kernels (`flagos-spacemit-ops.cpp`), the two kernel-table entries, the op counters' `kvh-partial` and `kvh-merge`, the fake plugins of the loader's tests given the two names, the switch's row in `build.md` §7; our kernels untouched. Read against the fork: the copied kernel differs only by the partial-only output and one fix, (g): a position is packed when any token sees it, so a row can meet a tile it sees none of before it has a maximum, where the fork computed exp(-inf - -inf) = NaN; the row is zeroed and skipped (as our split's step), the same bits where the row had a maximum, needed only with more than one chunk per KV head (not on Qwen3's 8 KV heads). Decided by the coder, kept: the RVV partial runs only with TCM for its q and V sums (as the fork's dispatch and our split), else the reference; the kernel's row path for too little TCM is unreachable on the K3 (384 KiB per tile, the pack about 46 KiB) and not tested; the old attention checks do not pin the switch, so the check program runs with `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN_KVH` unset (its new blocks set it themselves).
- `build-host` clean; the riscv64 syntax check 0 errors in both files, the 11 warnings of the copied code as before.
- The check program, all checks passed (16.9 s): 175 contract selections (5 settings × Qwen3-4B, GQA 8 at n_kv 256 and 255, GQA 1, ALiBi × 1 to 64 tokens); 28 KV-head cases (1 to 8 tokens, n_kv 256 to 4096, GQA 2, 4 and 8, 1, 2 and 4 chunks, sinks, softcap, no mask, every cell hidden) against the CPU, max NMSE 8.63e-05 (bound 5e-4), worst row 1.50e-04 (5e-3), the contract taken in 28/28, two runs identical, the steps called directly with 3 and 5 tiles identical in 24/24; 6/28 outputs differ from the default's (with one chunk per KV head the reference repeats the default's arithmetic); every other line the figures of the build before the change.
- test-backend-ops `FLASH_ATTN_EXT`: 1324/1324 with the switch unset and with `1-8` (101 graphs through the KV-head steps).
- Qwen3-0.6B, `-c 1024 -b 1024 -ub 4 --chunks 2`, against the CPU: mean KLD 0.004163 ± 0.000160 unset and with `2-4` (every attention call through `kvh-partial` and `kvh-merge` by the op counters), to the last digit, as one chunk per KV head makes them.
- Two mutations caught: the merge skipping the last chunk (70 checks failed), every token taking token 0's mask row (57 failed).
- `--bench-attn` runs and prints its 25 rows (1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 tokens × n_kv 256 to 8192; meaningless on the Mac's serial reference).

**The CPU base, corrected after the first K3 accuracy run (2026-11-04).** My first block made the base with `-t 8 -ub 4`, which §6 of `PROJECT.md` forbids ("Never use an 8-thread CPU run as a reference"). The run died with signal 11 (`rc=139`; the kernel log puts it at question 8's instruction, `libggml-cpu.so.0.19.0 + 0x99316`, `a2` zeroed, `build.md` §7's sixth dump), so the base held its 16,404 bytes of header and tokens and no log-probs. Both KLD runs then decoded the whole chunk, failed to read the log-probs and returned 0 without statistics, the table's header the only line the block's grep matched. Those two decodes do give a first speed reading, one run each and not taking turns: the whole 4,096-token chunk at `-ub 4` took 633.34 s with the switch off and 164.31 s with `2-4`, 3.85 times faster (618 and 160 ms per ubatch, depth 0 to 4,092, half of the ubatches with 4 rows through the head). It does not count for the rule, which asks for the benchmark and llama-bench; it says nothing about accuracy. Reproduced on the Mac with such a file: `rc=0`, that one line, `failed reading log-probs for chunk 0` in the log. The block now makes the base with one thread, twice, compares the files, starts the KLD runs only if they are identical, and greps the error line.

The base also moved to the default ubatch, after a measurement that overturned my assumption that its ubatch does not matter. On the Mac (Qwen3-0.6B, `-c 1024 -b 1024 --chunks 1`, one-thread CPU runs), the CPU against its own default-ubatch base:

| CPU run | Mean KLD | Maximum KLD | Same top token |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-ub 4` | 0.001896 ± 0.000124 | 0.026864 | 97.65% |
| `-ub 63` | 0.001896 ± 0.000124 | 0.026864 | 97.65% |
| `-ub 64` | 0.000000 | 0.000042 | 100% |
| `-ub 128` | 0.000000 | 0.000042 | 100% |

So ggml-cpu's two attention kernels disagree, the line exactly at the tiled kernel's threshold. By reading, its one-chunk kernel sums V in F16 (`ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:8632`) and its tiled kernel in F32 (`:8944`); that this sum is the cause is an inference, which the one-chunk kernel's F32 branch in a scratch build would settle. The provider divides the same way, by path and by machine: the reference kernels the Mac runs sum in F32 on both settings (`flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:137`, `:536`), which is why the Mac's two settings agree to the last digit; the K3's RVV one-chunk and KV-head kernels both sum in F16 (`flagos-spacemit-rvv-attn.cpp:838`, `:941`, `:1143`, `:1365`), the split and the tiled kernel in F32. Consequences:

- A default-ubatch base shares its rounding with neither setting under test, costs less (on the Mac 9.28 s per pass against 13.55), and is the runner's practice, one base for every batch size. The simulated device at `-ub 4`, both settings alike: 0.003275 ± 0.000201 against it, 0.003377 ± 0.000244 against a `-ub 4` base (maximum 0.030946 and 0.062357, same top token 98.04% and 97.46%).
- The Mac's KLD says nothing about the F16 sums. On the K3 both settings carry that rounding, so their absolute KLDs may exceed the Mac's (estimate); the rule compares them with each other against one base, so it stands.
- Possibly a second reason for the threshold experiment (part B): the tiled kernel sums in F32, so below 64 tokens it might gain accuracy as well as speed (an inference, untested).

**Result on the K3, accuracy (2026-11-05; the user's run; the speed runs still to come, the rule not yet applied).** Qwen3-4B, one chunk of 4,096 tokens. The CPU base by the §6 rule: two one-thread runs without a fault, 2266.35 and 2265.88 s per pass, PPL 14.0963 ± 1.00014 both, the two files of 622,058,764 bytes identical. Against it at `-ub 4`, `-t 8`:

| Setting | Mean KLD | PPL(Q)/PPL(base) | Same top token | Seconds per pass |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| off | 0.002732 ± 0.000101 | 1.003269 ± 0.004533 | 97.362 ± 0.354% | 625.58 |
| `2-4` | 0.002570 ± 0.000089 | 1.007191 ± 0.004528 | 97.167 ± 0.367% | 164.51 |

- The KV-head path's mean KLD is 0.000162 lower, 1.6 of the default's standard errors. Read literally, "within one standard error of the default's" is not met, on the better side. The rule's wording did not say which side it guards. My recommendation is to read it as guarding against lost accuracy, so that this passes; that reading came after the result, so it is the user's decision when the rule is applied. Decided by the user, 2026-11-05: a lower KLD counts as passing.
- The result does not show the KV-head path to be more accurate: the PPL ratio and the top token moved slightly the other way, each inside its standard error. Both runs score the same tokens, so these standard errors do not measure the difference between them directly, and the log gives no per-token figures to pair.
- The prediction "the KLD about today's" holds. The two differ here where the Mac's reference kernels agree to the last digit, so the RVV kernels' arithmetic differs; which step causes it was not looked at.
- Speed, a second reading of the same chunk, again one run each and not taking turns: 3.80 times faster (611 and 161 ms per ubatch), against 3.85 in the first. It does not count for the rule, which asks for the benchmark and llama-bench.

**Result on the K3, the attention benchmark (2026-11-05; the user's run, `--bench-attn`; `rc=0`).** Qwen3-4B's heads, one attention call, µs, the default against `1-8`, the two taking turns twice, the faster of each:

| Tokens | n_kv 256 | n_kv 512 | n_kv 1024 | n_kv 4096 | n_kv 8192 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 140 → 160 (+14.8%) | 270 → 294 (+8.8%) | 389 → 550 (+41.5%) | 1,115 → 1,981 (+77.6%) | 2,064 → 3,258 (+57.9%) |
| 2 | 680 → 196 (-71.1%) | 1,674 → 362 (-78.4%) | 3,512 → 657 (-81.3%) | 15,183 → 2,237 (-85.3%) | 31,840 → 3,931 (-87.7%) |
| 3 | 990 → 380 (-61.6%) | 2,491 → 816 (-67.3%) | 5,306 → 1,598 (-69.9%) | 22,925 → 5,965 (-74.0%) | 47,880 → 11,941 (-75.1%) |
| 4 | 1,290 → 268 (-79.2%) | 3,306 → 484 (-85.4%) | 7,069 → 872 (-87.7%) | 30,683 → 3,431 (-88.8%) | 63,870 → 6,764 (-89.4%) |
| 8 | 2,479 → 873 (-64.8%) | 6,539 → 2,131 (-67.4%) | 14,122 → 4,130 (-70.8%) | 62,328 → 15,994 (-74.3%) | 128,435 → 31,186 (-75.7%) |

- Against the predictions at n_kv 4096: 2, 3 and 4 tokens inside their ranges (2.24, 5.97 and 3.43 ms; predicted 2 to 3, 5 to 7 and 3 to 5). 8 tokens 15.99 ms, better than the 18 predicted. One token missed: 1.78 times the paired split, predicted 1.0 to 1.3, though it reads the same K and V in total; why was not looked at.
- The rule, the benchmark's half only (llama-bench still to run): 2 to 4 tokens and 8 tokens clear the 20% bar at every n_kv, 256 included, so nothing here asks the path to start at 512. 5, 6 and 7 tokens are not measured by the benchmark or by llama-bench; they would rest on 8, which takes the same one pass per token. One token is slower at every n_kv: the paired split stays unless llama-bench's tg32 contradicts this, which I do not expect (inference).
- The pass structure shows in the time per token at n_kv 4096: 0.86 ms at 4 tokens and 1.12 at 2 (one read per call), 1.99 at 3, 2.00 at 8 and 1.98 at 1 (one read per token). So 3 tokens cost 1.74 times 4. Candidate, not built and not part of this decision: 8 tokens as two fused passes of 4, about 6.9 ms against 16.0, and 3 tokens as one fused pass with an unused row, about 3.4 ms against 6.0 (estimates from the 4-token figure). The same grouping could serve 9 to 63 tokens, beside lowering the tiled kernel's threshold (part B); the test would be that candidate against the threshold at the same shapes.

**Result on the K3, llama-bench (2026-11-05; the user's run; `-t 8 -fa 1 -mmp 0 -r 3`, the settings taking turns twice, the better of each).** t/s, the default against `2-8` (pp) or `1-8` (tg32):

| Test | Depth 0 | Depth 1024 | Depth 4096 |
|---|---|---|---|
| pp2 | 19.04 → 18.96 (-0.4%) | 8.54 → 15.66 (+83.5%) | 3.18 → 10.97 (+244.8%) |
| pp3 | 24.21 → 23.92 (-1.2%) | 9.52 → 16.65 (+74.9%) | 3.28 → 9.44 (+187.9%) |
| pp4 | 37.71 → 37.46 (-0.7%) | 11.10 → 28.71 (+158.7%) | 3.43 → 17.71 (+416.8%) |
| pp8 | 61.14 → 60.25 (-1.4%) | 12.58 → 28.31 (+125.0%) | 3.51 → 11.80 (+236.4%) |
| pp64 (control) | 116.37 → 116.29 (-0.1%) | 78.18 → 77.99 (-0.2%) | 38.44 → 38.49 (+0.1%) |
| pp512 (control) | 95.92 → 96.52 (+0.6%) | 69.06 → 69.17 (+0.2%) | 35.92 → 35.92 (-0.0%) |
| tg32, 1 token | 11.18 → 11.16 (-0.2%) | 9.74 → 9.29 (-4.6%) | 7.75 → 6.35 (-18.1%) |

- The prediction for pp4 at depth 4096, about 3.5 to about 15 t/s, was beaten: 3.43 to 17.71, a graph of 1,166 ms to 226 ms.
- The benchmark predicts these gains: at depth 4096 the time saved per graph is 447, 597, 940 and 1,601 ms for 2, 3, 4 and 8 tokens, against 466, 611, 981 and 1,668 ms from the benchmark's saving at n_kv 4096 × 36 calls, within 5%. tg32's loss, 28.4 ms, against 31.2 ms by the same sum.
- Against the rule so far. One token: settled, it fails (-4.6% and -18.1% where it had to gain 2%), so the paired split stays and the switch's range would start at 2. 2 to 4 tokens and 8 tokens: every shape from depth 1024 past the 20%, no control more than 0.2% slower, depth 0 inside the 2%.
- One point open (accuracy's side of the bar was decided, above): "if n_kv 256 is slower, the path starts at 512": at depth 0 all four shapes are slightly slower in llama-bench (0.4 to 1.9 ms per graph), while the benchmark has n_kv 256 62 to 79% faster. They measure different things: the benchmark's cache is full (`n_past = n_kv - n_tokens`), while at depth 0 almost every position is masked, and both kernels skip masked positions (by reading), so attention costs little either way (the inference that this explains the difference). llama-bench has not measured n_kv 256 with a filled cache, which is where a short conversation runs. The extra block settles it: pp2, 3, 4 and 8 at depths 64 and 200 (n_kv 256), and pp5, 6 and 7 at depths 0, 1024 and 4096, which no run has measured. Predictions (estimates, from the benchmark): pp4 about +8% at depth 64 and about +30% at depth 200; pp5 to pp7 at depth 4096 about pp8's 11.80 t/s or better. How it will be read, set before the run: the path keeps starting at n_kv 256, the depth-0 loss accepted, if no shape at depth 64 or 200 is more than 2% slower; otherwise it starts at 512. 5 to 7 tokens take the same bar as 8: at least 20% faster at depths 1024 and 4096, no more than 2% slower at depth 0. Checked on the Mac (Qwen3-0.6B, the simulated device; op counters): depths 64 and 200 run at n_kv 256, and 2, 7 and 8 tokens go through `kvh-partial` and `kvh-merge`.
- What this does not cover, for TTFT: 9 to 63 tokens stay on the one-chunk kernels (pp12 at depth 4096, 3.67 t/s, part B), and the chat tails that matter are about 20 to 130 tokens (`batch-one.md`).

**Result on the K3, the extra llama-bench run (2026-11-05; the user's run; same flags, the settings taking turns twice, the better of each).** t/s, the default against `2-8`:

| Test | Depth 64 (n_kv 256) | Depth 200 (n_kv 256) |
|---|---|---|
| pp2 | 18.12 → 18.58 (+2.5%) | 16.23 → 18.07 (+11.3%) |
| pp3 | 23.15 → 23.23 (+0.3%) | 20.40 → 22.88 (+12.2%) |
| pp4 | 35.11 → 36.89 (+5.1%) | 28.76 → 35.70 (+24.1%) |
| pp8 | 55.03 → 57.00 (+3.6%) | 41.70 → 54.38 (+30.4%) |

| Test | Depth 0 | Depth 1024 | Depth 4096 |
|---|---|---|---|
| pp5 | 43.03 → 42.31 (-1.7%) | 11.53 → 23.90 (+107.3%) | 3.45 → 11.06 (+220.9%) |
| pp6 | 42.53 → 42.39 (-0.3%) | 11.57 → 23.69 (+104.8%) | 3.44 → 10.94 (+218.2%) |
| pp7 | 43.91 → 43.00 (-2.1%) | 11.67 → 24.04 (+106.0%) | 3.43 → 10.84 (+215.7%) |

- n_kv 256: settled by the reading set beforehand. No shape at depth 64 or 200 is slower, so the path keeps starting at n_kv 256, and the depth-0 loss is accepted. The estimates for pp4 were about +8% and about +30%; measured +5.1% and +24.1%.
- 5 and 6 tokens pass the bar set beforehand. 7 tokens misses it by 0.1 point at depth 0 (-2.1% against 2%, 159.4 to 162.8 ms per graph), past 20% at the two other depths. The default's pp7 stands above its pp5 and pp6 there while the KV-head's does less so, which may be noise (inference, one comparison). A repeat settles it: pp5, 6 and 7 at depth 0, six turns of each setting, `-r 5`. Reading set beforehand: the same statistic as the bar, the better of the turns; pp7 passes if no more than 2% slower. If it stays above 2%, the range is the user's decision.
- The estimate for pp5 to pp7 at depth 4096, about pp8's 11.80 t/s or better, was slightly high: 10.84 to 11.06.
- The pass structure again: at depth 4096 a graph takes 226 ms at 4 tokens and 452 ms at 5, one token more doubling it (the fused passes of 4 above are the candidate, not built).
