The repeat (2026-11-05; the user's run; six turns of each setting, `-r 5`, depth 0), t/s, the default against `2-8`:

| Test | Best of the turns | Median of the turns | Spread, off and `2-8` |
|---|---|---|---|
| pp5 | 42.82 → 42.54 (-0.7%) | 42.23 → 42.35 (+0.3%) | 1.6%, 2.9% |
| pp6 | 42.80 → 42.25 (-1.3%) | 42.34 → 41.91 (-1.0%) | 1.4%, 1.0% |
| pp7 | 43.69 → 43.61 (-0.2%) | 43.59 → 43.16 (-1.0%) | 0.7%, 1.3% |

pp7 passes by the reading set beforehand, -0.2%, as do pp5 and pp6. The two settings' spreads across turns are as large as the differences, so the first run's -2.1% was within what this measurement varies by; a loss of about 1% or less at depth 0 is not ruled out, and the medians of pp6 and pp7 lean that way.

**The decision rule applied (2026-11-05).** One token fails: the paired split stays. For 2 to 8 tokens the speed bars are met in the benchmark (8 tokens and 2 to 4; 5 to 7 not in it) and in llama-bench, with no control more than 0.2% slower, the path starting at n_kv 256; the KLD at `-ub 4` passes on the user's reading.

**A gap found on applying it: the accuracy run covers only one of the kernels.** On Qwen3-4B a call of 4 tokens is one pass of 16 rows, the only shape `-ub 4` runs. 2 tokens are one pass of 8 rows, and 3 and 5 to 8 tokens are one pass of 4 rows per token (`flagos-spacemit-rvv-attn.cpp:2165-2169`). The spec's rule asked 5 to 8 tokens only for "the same 20% bar", so this does not fail the rule as written, but the default would send those shapes to arithmetic no model-level run on the board has checked. The check before the default: the same perplexity run at `-ub 2` and `-ub 8`, off and `2-8`, against the same base (`~/kvh/base1.kld`). Reading set beforehand, as the user's decision on `-ub 4`: each passes if its mean KLD is no more than one of the default's standard errors above the default's at that ubatch.

**Result on the K3, accuracy at `-ub 2` and `-ub 8` (2026-11-05; the user's run; the same base, the file checked intact):**

| Ubatch, kernel | Setting | Mean KLD | PPL(Q)/PPL(base) | Same top token | Seconds per pass |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `-ub 2`, 8 rows | off | 0.002035 ± 0.000093 | 1.004814 ± 0.004557 | 97.509 ± 0.345% | 736.61 |
| `-ub 2`, 8 rows | `2-8` | 0.001960 ± 0.000073 | 1.004044 ± 0.004579 | 97.655 ± 0.335% | 287.41 |
| `-ub 8`, 4 rows per token | off | 0.002732 ± 0.000101 | 1.003269 ± 0.004533 | 97.362 ± 0.354% | 587.08 |
| `-ub 8`, 4 rows per token | `2-8` | 0.002570 ± 0.000089 | 1.007191 ± 0.004528 | 97.167 ± 0.367% | 209.41 |

- Both pass: the KV-head path's mean KLD is below the default's at each ubatch. 3 and 5 to 7 tokens were not run; they take the same 4-row passes as 8 (by reading).
- `-ub 8` matches `-ub 4` to every printed digit, on each setting. The runs are separate (the times differ), and it has precedent: the CPU at `-ub 4` and `-ub 63`, and the simulated device at `-ub 4` and `-ub 8`, agree to the last digit too. What follows is an inference: a row's arithmetic does not depend on the ubatch above 4 rows, and the 4-row quantizer's groups line up at 4 and 8. If so, the KV-head path's 16-row and 4-row passes give the same results at the printed precision, a cross-check of the two kernels. The maximum KLD of the four logs is one more digit of evidence, not a proof.
- `-ub 2`'s lower KLD on both settings is near the 0.00205 measured at `-ub 1` earlier, so these rows may take the one-row quantizer rather than the 4-row one (inference; not read in the code).
- Speed, one run each, not counted by the rule: 2.56 times faster at `-ub 2` (360 to 140 ms per ubatch) and 2.80 at `-ub 8` (1,147 to 409 ms).

**So the rule is met for 2 to 8 tokens, from n_kv 256, on Qwen3-4B.** No model-level run on the board covers Qwen3-0.6B (rows per pass 4, 8 and 16 at 2, 4 and 8 tokens); it passed the check program against the CPU, and the spec below adds its run.

**Spec, for review (2026-11-05): 2 to 8 tokens through the KV-head split by default.**
1. `parse_kvh_setting` (`flagos-spacemit-attn.cpp:405`): unset or empty means `2-8` (kept as 2 × 64 + 8); `off` none; `<lo>-<hi>` and the warning for anything else as today. Its comment no longer says test-only. The switch stays, for comparison and to turn the path off.
2. The one-token path, the tiled kernel, 9 to 63 tokens and every other contract: unchanged. `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN` does not select the KV-head path, as today: the row in `build.md` §7 says that comparing the one-chunk kernels at 2 to 8 tokens takes `FLAGOS_SPACEMIT_ATTN_KVH=off`.
3. The check program (`tests/check_spacemit.cpp`):
   - the contract table (`:450`): unset now takes 2 to 8, empty added, `off` none;
   - the attention cases written for the one-chunk kernels pin `off`, so those kernels keep their coverage at 2 to 8 tokens;
   - the KV-head block (`:2035`) compares against `off`, and adds one case with the switch unset that must take the contract;
   - `--bench-attn` (`:4514`) compares `off` with `1-8` and labels it so.
4. Docs: `build.md` §7 (the row, the result), `PROJECT.md` §8, `progress.md` item 42, this section.

**Tests on the Mac:** `build-host` without new warnings; the check program, all passed, with today's figures under `off`; test-backend-ops `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` unset and `off`, all passed; `llama-perplexity` on Qwen3-0.6B, simulated device, `-c 1024 -b 1024 -ub 4 --chunks 2`, unset and `off`, identical as on the reference kernels, and the op counters showing `kvh-partial` unset and not under `off`. One mutation caught: the default left at none.

**On the K3 (the user's runs):**
1. The build, the check program (`--expect-device`), test-backend-ops `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` unset and `off`.
2. Qwen3-0.6B's accuracy: a one-thread CPU base twice, byte-identical, then `-ub 2`, `-ub 4` and `-ub 8`, `off` and unset, with the same reading as Qwen3-4B's.
3. That the default is the path measured: Qwen3-4B, pp4 at depth 4096, unset against `off`, about the 17.71 and 3.43 t/s above.
4. `spacemit_check.py` in full, its checks all passing, which gives the new server baseline before S2.

**Not in this spec:** one token; the fused passes of 4 tokens; the threshold for 9 to 63 tokens (both after S2, with `batch-one.md`). **Time:** the coder, a short session; on the K3 one build and about 1.5 hours of runs (estimate).

**Result on the K3, speculative decoding (2026-11-05; the user's run; 128 tokens, the stream per graph, one run each, not taking turns; the decision rule does not count it):**

| Prompt, draft length | Tokens per check | Accepted | Plain decoding | Off | `2-4` | Change |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| p2, 2 | 3 | 82/94 | 11.05 t/s | 14.38 t/s (1.30×) | 14.64 t/s (1.32×) | +1.8% |
| p2, 3 | 4 | 90/117 | 11.05 t/s | 17.01 t/s (1.54×) | 17.50 t/s (1.58×) | +2.9% |
| p4, 2 | 3 | 86/86 | 10.07 t/s | 9.92 t/s (0.98×) | 12.60 t/s (1.25×) | +27.0% |
| p4, 3 | 4 | 96/96 | 10.07 t/s | 11.71 t/s (1.16×) | 17.20 t/s (1.71×) | +46.9% |

- The predictions hold: p4 at draft length 3 about 1.1 to about 1.6 (1.16 to 1.71), at draft length 2 from 0.98 to about 1.3 (1.25); p2 about as today.
- The accepted counts are the same on both settings, so the drafts did not change. On p4 every draft was accepted, the best case for speculation; this says little about prompts where fewer are.
- It turns draft length 2 on p4, which lost to plain decoding, into a gain. Draft length 3 (a 4-token check, one read) gains more than draft length 2 (a 3-token check, three passes), as the benchmark's 3.43 and 5.97 ms suggest; the fused 3-token pass above would be the test of how much of that gap is the pass structure.

### Not planned

- Design Phase 5 (split compiler): the K3 has no vendor graph compiler. One launch per split is an executor detail, not a compiler.
- Design Phase 6 (pre-placement partition): every K3 op can run on its own; no fused-only constraint.

## 5. Verification and acceptance

| Area (Design §15) | K3 check | From |
|---|---|---|
| Registry, lifecycle | `flagos-check-*`, `flagos-check-spacemit`; repeated model load/free | M1 |
| `supports_op` negative matrix | `test-backend-ops support`: every unclaimed shape/type reported unsupported, never wrong | M2 |
| Numerics | `test-backend-ops test -b FlagOS:SpacemiT:0 -o <op>`; where no `test-backend-ops` case fits the claim (Q4_0 IME layout), `flagos-check-spacemit` compares with the CPU backend; perplexity vs M0.6, with the default `-fa auto` and with `-fa on` | M2 |
| Memory | VmRSS; weights stored once; `get_tensor` round trip | M2f |
| State and alias | KV write (`SET_ROWS`) and in-place ops with views; context shift | M2d-M2e |
| Fail closed | forced kernel failure returns `GGML_STATUS_FAILED`; no partial writes before launch checks | M2a |
| TCM hygiene | 0 blocks held after every run, including failures | M2a |
| Performance | interleaved A/B vs the three M0 baselines; pp128, tg128; split count; first run vs steady state | M2b onward |
| Package/ABI | Design §15.3 matrix | M3 |
| Stability | long generation (thousands of tokens), repeated sessions | M2e |

Design §15.6 gates, as they apply to the K3: no `supports_op` false positives (1); the target model runs entirely through direct paths plus CPU fallback (2, 3); KV/state writes tested (4); AOT fails closed (5, M3 only); full-model correctness (7); interleaved pp/tg A/B (8); default path needs no development environment variables (9); errors never reported as success (11); lifecycle stress (12).

## 6. Risks

| ID | Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Resolved 2026-10-07: A100 VLEN is 1024, X100 256, and a thread's `vlenb` changes when it migrates. Remaining risk: code that reads VLEN on the main thread (X100) and runs on A100 workers picks the wrong path; ggml-cpu does this (`ggml-cpu.c:742`, `repack.cpp:4591-4712`) | provider kernels read VLEN on the worker thread; profile reports 1024 |
| R2 | spine-runtime is a closed binary: `shared_buffer()` falls back to heap silently if TCM is unavailable (on this board it gets real TCM, 2026-10-07), all-or-nothing core grants, no exception handling around tiles | log a warning when the buffer is outside `/dev/tcm`; executor interface keeps option 2 open |
| R3 | Stale TCM blocks after a crash block all later runs | probe-time warning; release on every path; `tcmrelease` documented |
| R4 | Lossy repacks (Q4_1, Q4_K, Q6_K) change results. Q4_1 (M2c.1): on random weights the zero-point conversion changes a matmul by NMSE 5.9e-3, about doubling Q4_1's own error | claim only with KLD and perplexity evidence on the model (M2c); fallback for Q4_1: keep the exact minimum (M2c design). Q4_1 accepted 2026-10-10: on Qwen3-4B it raised the mean KLD against the CPU from 0.0024 to 0.0032 with 512-token batches (0.0020 with `-ub 1`), perplexity -0.10%. Q6_K (requantized to Q8_0) accepted 2026-10-10: +0.0001 (512-token batches) and +0.0002 (`-ub 1`), perplexity -0.16% |
| R5 | Under ACCEL, `-fa auto` switches flash attention off when the provider runs attention, so default runs use non-flash attention | C5a in the fork (decided 2026-10-12, its own commit with M2e.1; approved by the mentor 2026-10-14, so M2e.2 is not needed); upstream fix candidate (§2.4); reverted 2026-10-28 with C5b (user): inert under IGPU, the risk itself gone with the device type |
| R6 | Under ACCEL, a provider op that refuses CPU buffers forces copies and splits (E1: 280 splits per generated token). Also many splits until M2d, while each matmul is surrounded by CPU ops | accept host buffers for non-weight operands (§2.3); X3 measures it; split count in every benchmark |
| R7 | ggml-spacemit over-claims (X0: wrong F16 matmul, masked softmax, CPY; aborts in RMS_NORM, ROPE) | port piece by piece with fixes and tests; the mentor allows changing it |
| R8 | FlagTree SpacemiT is immature (no AOT tool, no tests, QEMU-only CI with VLEN 1024) | M3 is conditional and starts with one op |
| R9 | Common gaps: no `get_proc_address` forwarding (thread count, abort callback), no RVV/IME feature bits, no `LOCAL_SCRATCH` cap for TCM (mentor design §8.1, not in code) | work around in the provider; propose C2-C4 only when needed |
| R10 | The IME Q4_0 32x256 kernel has never been checked op by op: no `test-backend-ops` case fits the layout, the 1198/1198 run used plain buffers, and X0 ran only the cases ggml-spacemit claimed (its Q8_0 and Q6_K IME matmuls failed there) | M2b compares with the CPU backend at the model's shapes, and the IME kernel with its scalar reference |
| R11 | Copied kernel code (D6) misses later upstream fixes, and SpacemiT's fixes that never reach upstream | at each upstream merge, check `git log` of `ggml/src/ggml-cpu/spacemit/`, and SpacemiT's fork too (`spacemit-com/llama.cpp` `mtmd-backend`: its in-tree copy and `ggml-spacemit` differ, and a fix may be in one only); copied functions keep their bodies unchanged and note their origin. Checked 2026-10-28 function by function (upstream `8ca4060b`, the fork's `855a53b`): one fix taken, SpacemiT's Q4_0 scale fix (the fork's PR #30, in its in-tree copy only), with the factor 0.125 for its 0.1 so that the outputs stay bit-identical where nothing overflowed (`build.md` §7); the rest is new capability or code the provider does not copy (`PROJECT.md` item 26) |
| R12 | Upstream's active branch of the 1-row Q4_1 IME kernel (`gemm_kernel_i8i4_m1`) multiplies zero point and activation sum in 16 bits (`vmul.vx` at SEW 16), which wraps for 32 activations near their block's maximum and of one sign against a zero point above 8, and adds each block in fp16. Found by reading the code (and by the review), from the ISA's definition; not run on the board | the provider enables the function's exact branch (`#if 0` upstream, M2c design); a probe in `flagos-check-spacemit` fails the run if the 1-row result is wrong; KLD with `-ub 1`; fallback: 1 row through the 4-row kernel. Confirmed on the board 2026-10-10: the probe gives 3.0e-7 for 1 row and for 4, mean KLD 0.0020 with `-ub 1` |
| R13 | The copied 4-row 8-bit kernel (`gemm_kernel_i8i8_m4`) decrements an input-only asm operand inside its loop over 32-column groups, so a call with more than 32 columns depends on register allocation | every multi-row call passes at most 32 columns; the M2c.2 dispatch asserts it (a loop over 32-column calls would not help once the kernel is inlined). On the K3 2026-10-10 the 4-row kernel matches the reference at every shape |

Optional Common changes, each needing separate approval: C2 RVV/IME/TCM feature bits in `flagos-target.h` (append-only); C3 forward provider functions through `get_proc_address`; C4 `LOCAL_SCRATCH` memory cap.

Outside FlagOS (approved by the mentor as fork patches, 2026-10-14; `upstream-changes.md` U1, U2): C5, in `src/llama-context.cpp`, (a) let the automatic flash-attention check accept an ACCEL backend that supports the op when the layer's device is the CPU (`:504-562`; **decided by the user 2026-10-12** for M2e.1, as its own commit like (b): once the provider claims `FLASH_ATTN_EXT`, `-fa auto` would otherwise turn flash attention off and leave attention on the X100 cores, see M2e design); (b) skip norm pinning when the layer's device is the CPU (`:2499-2520`, marked FIXME upstream; **implemented 2026-10-09** for M2d, skipping the pin only when an ACCEL backend supports the op, see M2d design). Without C5, ACCEL users need `-fa on` and get 30-39% slower generation (Qwen3-4B / 0.6B) (`device-type.md` §5, X3b).

## 7. Questions for the mentor

1. ~~Device type~~ decided: ACCEL (2026-10-08), changed to IGPU (agreed by the mentor, recorded 2026-10-26; §2.8). Still open: FlagOS `caps.kind`, `cpu_accelerator` (proposed) or `ai_accelerator`?
2. Memory under ACCEL: weights in an IME-repacked buffer, stored once, with the provider reading CPU buffers for everything else (A2, §2.3). We are building M2b on A2 (2026-10-09); please confirm. The alternative (A1) doubles quantized-weight memory and would change only the buffer part. Ownership decided 2026-10-26 (with the mentor): the layout and its repack are the plugin's (`flagos-spacemit-weights.{h,cpp}` part of the plugin, D10), so A2's shape stands as the plugin's choice; what remains of this question is the requantization's accuracy trade-off.
3. Is spine-runtime (closed `libspert`) acceptable as a hard dependency of the provider?
4. Kernel reuse: decided 2026-10-09 to copy, per milestone, only the functions used into `providers/spacemit/` (§2.5), following the mentor design's "reference, not boundary" (§14.1) and SpacemiT's own ggml-spacemit. Objections? The alternative is compiling the in-tree sources in place.
5. Target for Phase 0: Qwen3-4B Q4_0 (SpacemiT has reference numbers) or Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M (comparable with AMD, but needs lossy Q4_K/Q6_K repacks and gated-delta-net ops early)?
6. Is FlagTree AOT (M3) in scope, and should it be cross-compiled on x86 or built on the K3? Refined with the M3 design (2026-10-14): which compiler, FlagTree's copy of the SpacemiT backend or SpacemiT's more mature `spine-triton`? Compile on x86 in FlagTree CI's Docker image, or on the K3? Manifest: AMD's format 2 extended to the §9.4 envelope, or the mentor design's v3 (semantic op, constraints, `kernel_abi_hash`)? Packages out of git, as AMD's? And is success an AOT op that runs (the FlagOS path shown on the K3), or only AOT ops that are at least as fast as the hand-written kernels?
7. Is co-building in `providers/spacemit` agreed with SpacemiT, given they ship ggml-spacemit as an `ACCEL` backend?
8. Should we report the TCM issues to SpacemiT (no dead-owner recovery; `try_wait` timeout unit about 12 µs, not 1 µs)? Also a CPU-backend crash whose register state the program cannot produce, now seen twice at the same instruction (2026-10-09 and 2026-10-13, both in long CPU-only runs; targeted tests with about 80 million page faults did not reproduce it). The second time the crash reporter captured the registers: every register matches the loop's state except `a2`, which is 0 where the code had just set it to a valid pointer, so something outside the program cleared it (kernel or core; `build.md` §7). And, found by reading: the 1-row Q4_1 IME kernel's active branch multiplies zero point and activation sum in 16 bits (R12).
9. ~~ACCEL gaps in llama.cpp (C5, §6)~~ decided: the mentor approved both fork patches, (a) the automatic flash-attention check (`64e6cbd`) and (b) norm pinning (`f1cf809`), 2026-10-14 (reported by the user), so M2e.2 is not needed. The question was: patch both in our fork, or raise an upstream llama.cpp issue? Without them ACCEL needs `-fa on` and generates 30-39% slower (`device-type.md` §5, X3b).
10. ~~Order after M2d~~ decided by the user 2026-10-10: M2c first, starting with the Q4_1 matmuls (M2c.1), then the Q6_K head (M2c.2), then M2e. The question was: M2c (Q4_1 and Q6_K on the AI cores), then M2e (attention)? Measured on the K3 (`build.md` §7, M2d): moving the 4 Q4_1 `ffn_down` matmuls alone raises pp128 from 53 to 83 t/s, past the pp128 target, and moving the head saves about 12-25 ms per token; attention on the X100 cores costs +445 ms per token at a context of 4096 tokens (IME: +68 ms), so M2e decides generation speed at long context. M2e brings the flash-attention question of item 9 (C5a) back.
